In-dialog Undo/Redo uses project history, and closing returns keyboard focus to the resident’s creator button. `verify-character-creator.mjs` covers preview-only checkpoint equality, every preview activity, matching look changes, cache reuse, keyboard tabs, reduced motion, desktop layout, actual setting changes, undo, durable reload and native playtest data. Existing resident authoring and reusable-preset browser journeys also pass. Screenshots were inspected and inherited canvas sizing was corrected. Category locks, randomization and per-category reset are now implemented through the same undoable project transactions. Identity begins locked, the screen lists the categories that will change, and unlocking identity explicitly discloses replacement of the name and optional text. Random choices use supported appearance, personality, routine, occupation, background and work-preference catalogs; generated experience fits the balanced budget without changing the author’s policy. Reset restores only the selected category to its values when the screen opened, including removal of originally absent optional fields. `verify-character-choices.mjs` checks 320 generated characters across every lock combination, both native budget validators, immutable identities and category isolation. The actual browser journey covers locked and all-locked states, optional-field reset, undo/redo, a non-default biography and nonzero friendship, durable reload and native playtesting. The existing creator animation/tab journey also passes; screenshots were inspected and custom-checkbox sizing corrected. This supplies the current bundled-look creator controls; modular body/hair/clothing artwork and the remaining Stage 4 requirements are still open.

Playable saves now use version 43; supported older villages, including versions 29 through 42, migrate without changing their existing camp or ownership. Skill, workforce and simulation component schemas 2 store resident records by ID; old slot arrays migrate only through supported older-save paths. Authoring schema 1 is separate from the save schema. Authored content currently passes through `scenario_template`, `scenario_catalog`, `validate_scenario`, and `load_scenario`; it does not pass through development-fixture restaging. Starting gifted buildings have no paid-material salvage credit.

The local WASM baseline at the start of implementation sampled 300 steps per scene: median tick/full-snapshot/frame-sample times were 1.49/2.53/0.15 ms for arrival, 2.66/2.61/0.35 ms for the established village, and 1.47/2.38/0.29 ms for autonomy. This single run shared the machine with native tests and is a comparison reference, not a desktop frame-rate or final performance result.

A separate version-32 WebAssembly sample after ID-keyed workforce records used the same 300-step benchmark script on 64×64 scenes. Median tick/full-snapshot/frame-sample times were 1.13/1.86/0.09 ms for arrival, 1.38/1.78/0.28 ms for the established village and 1.07/1.77/0.25 ms for autonomy. This is a current reference run, not a controlled improvement claim, browser frame-rate measurement or maximum-population benchmark.

Resolved regression fixture (2026-10-23): `verify-landscape.mjs` now stages relative to the current camp and starter warehouse. The original 110-step work budget and former-window exclusion remain, with additional assertions proving real material hauling, labour, consumption and exact warehouse debit. It passes the default-landscape regression, shelter and one-day accounting/save checks; this is not a full all-biome release verdict. Evidence: `assets/validation/landscape-centered-v1.json` and `.github/builder-review/landscape-centered-fixture.json`.

A modular-art production prototype now exists under `web/art/characters/prototype-v1/`, with a neutral sixteen-pose base sheet, a body-derived shared-transform compositor, a technical head-position guide and an isolated artwork comparison surface at `artwork/character-prototype.html`. Both generated hair overlays are explicitly rejected and excluded from gameplay: none of their sixteen poses passes the approximate head-region gate, and the guided attempt introduces ear/skin pixels. The actual composition board shows faces or necks covered while crowns remain exposed. `verify-character-parts.mjs` measures the source pixels and checks missing/failed part rejection; `verify-sprite-frames.mjs` still passes. Exact built-in image-generation prompts, reference roles, output paths, fingerprints, measured reports and the inspected board are recorded in `assets/art-direction/character-prototype-v1/review.json`. These are failed-registration findings and prototype infrastructure, not completed modular artwork or a shippable new resident. Continue with co-authored per-pose layer masks and measured attachment landmarks, keeping one body-derived scale/anchor, before expanding to the complete animation set and multiple resident appearances.

The current bundled-look creator limit is twelve residents. `verify-custom-casts.mjs` measures 24 64×64 configurations (three biomes; one, two, five and twelve people; shared/mixed bundled looks), each with 120 frames at 1× and 16×, and checks arrival, material conservation and exact save restoration. In the initial local run, the worst twelve-person compile was 16.43 ms and the worst engine-plus-compact-frame p95 was 0.20 ms at 1× / 17.64 ms at 16×. `verify-creator-casts.mjs` sampled 90 rendered twelve-person arrival frames in headless Chromium at 1600×900: median interval 8.3 ms, p95 9.1 ms. These are separate short local measurements, not unique modular-appearance, long-simulation, maximum-entity, panning or player-device guarantees. The raw engine sample is in `assets/validation/custom-casts-v1.json`; final Stage 8 performance and art gates remain open.

Scenario authors can now choose initial building availability and grant construction unlocks through one-shot events. Native planning and the build menu use the same persisted lock set; source locks are reconciled against exact applied unlock receipts on restoration. Existing starting buildings and projects remain usable. A conservative reachability check rejects required progression that depends on its own unavailable building, including supported production/service dependencies, while permitting real alternatives and starting projects; it does not establish resource or land solvability. Native tests cover blocked placement without mutation, exact reload, corrupted lock rejection, circular goals, gifted projects and permanent unused exclusions. The 632-test run passed 630 tests and found two built-in Story refusal-text regressions; both passed focused reruns after preserving the original Story wording. Strict all-target linting, release WASM compilation, the new native/UI unlock verifier and existing event verifier pass. Public browser coverage verifies initial availability, undo/redo, unlock authoring/export, locked-card journal navigation, goal-triggered availability, actual newly unlocked Home placement through the canvas, invalid availability repair, event history and exact checkpoint restoration; screenshots were reviewed. The other Stage 6 requirements remain open.

One-shot supply events now deliver into a native arrival store or a stable authored receiving-building identity, including appropriate project, pantry, farm and workshop inventories. Full capacity includes other reserved arrivals; partial gifts are never delivered. A queued gift keeps its intent after its guard changes, waits during move/demolition work and requires walkable access from the arrival area. Missing or no-longer-receiving destinations stay queued without redirecting supplies. Native receipts match the authored material, quantity and destination and reconcile aggregate gifts against material inflows. The normal receiving API preserves freshness and conservation; direct gifts do not count as purchases, production or resident-carried deliveries. Source validation rejects unknown destinations/materials, zero quantities, deliveries beyond the receiving capacity and disabled logistics. Focused tests cover capacity, latched guards, pending/applied restoration, no replay, corrupted receipts/accounting, real move and unfinished-site demolition cancellation, and a warehouse isolated across an uncrossable river. The release-WASM supply verifier covers pause and exact 16× continuation. The public browser journey authors and exports a delivery, repairs invalid capacity with Undo, uses an explicitly imported full-store setup, reads native queue reasons, frees capacity through a real Trade sale, receives the gift and restores its checkpoint. Editor, queued and applied screenshots were inspected. Existing event and unlock browser journeys also pass. The remaining operational measurements, challenge handling, Story adapter and wider release gates remain required.

Final supply-event checks: the complete native regression run passes 635 tests. The subsequent unfinished-site demolition guard passes its focused test, strict all-target linting and the rebuilt release WASM. These checks complete the initial one-shot message, funding, building-unlock and supply-delivery effect set; they do not complete Stage 6 or the overall builder release.

Starting material supplies now have a player editor for all 29 materials, real native receiving locations, empty starts, allocation removal and owner merging. Native metadata exposes each store’s capacity and the shared village limit. Automatic distribution is a read-only native proposal that preserves material totals, prioritizes food at storage houses/kitchens and reports every unassigned unit; an overflowing proposal cannot be applied. Applying a fitting proposal uses one undoable transaction and checks the source project/revision. Unspecified food is fresh at the scenario date; configurable aged batches are now implemented as described below. Workshop starting inputs now bind to their actual production store after its initialization, sharing owner resolution with supply events. Three native tests and the release-WASM verifier cover conservation, global/local capacity, unknown stock, read-only previews, undo/redo, real workshop/pantry/project ownership, opening accounting and exact restores. The public browser journey edits quantities and locations, creates an empty start, repairs an overflowing allocation, previews shortage without dropping stock, combines owners and undoes it, applies food-aware distribution in one undoable edit, exports/reopens the draft and verifies fresh physical stock in an isolated run and checkpoint. All three dialog screenshots were inspected. The supply-event browser journey now prepares its full destination through these quantity controls, without a hand-edited import. This advances Stage 5; equipment, farming/animals and the broader release requirements remain open; located liquids are now implemented below.

Starting-supplies regression evidence: all 638 native tests pass, along with strict all-target linting and the release build. Compiler, initial-stock and one-shot event release-WASM checks pass. The existing creator lifecycle and updated supply-event browser journeys pass while leaving ordinary Story/Sandbox storage unchanged.

Authors can now define perishable starting batches by material, quantity and age before arrival. Unspecified units are fresh. Validation bounds batch counts and quantities, rejects non-food, over-allocation, expired ages and ages the simulation clock cannot preserve. Food may predate day one; save validation accepts those negative production dates only when backed by the frozen authored origin, collecting permitted dates once rather than scanning the source for every live lot. Normal saves still reject invented negative dates. Initialization uses real dated lots, and automatic distribution preserves every age while splitting inventory among stores. Owner merges, copies and moves retain batches. Quantity increases add fresh units; decreases preserve explicit batches where possible and trim the oldest excess aged units. Authors may make a batch fresh without changing food totals. The browser journey covers mixed ages and implicit fresh units, merges/undo, quantity trimming, freshening, invalid age/quantity repair, distribution preview/apply, export/reopen and exact physical pre-day-one dates after playtest/checkpoint. Both screenshots were inspected. Native checks prove real expiry/spoil accounting, exact saves and corrupted-source rejection. The release-WASM verifier covers matching food expiry/accounting at 1×/16×, exact restored continuation, copy/move isolation and 600 distinct batches. All 640 native tests pass. Equipment, perennial/animal states, the other remaining stage work and release gates remain required; located liquid classes/owners are now implemented below.

Final aged-food checks also pass strict all-target linting, the rebuilt release, the compiler and stock verifiers, and browser regressions for ordinary fresh starting supplies and supply-event recovery.

Starting liquids now support explicit per-container fresh water, reclaimed irrigation water, greywater and sewage, with independent native capacities and sealed waste classes. Automatic legacy fresh-water setup remains available; adopting exact contents requires a preview, and returning to fresh-water-only setup cannot discard non-fresh contents. The compiler validates completed compatible tanks, stable owners, quantity bounds, duplicate locations and the mirrored fresh total. Initialization records genuine opening balances with no received-volume grants or invisible ground tanks. The read-only distribution proposal preserves classes, prefers compatible treatment where present and lists any unassigned volume; overflowing proposals cannot be applied. Player controls include capacity feedback, owner merges, removal repair links, source/revision checks and undo/redo. Building copies clone owned tank contents to fresh building identities and recalculate the fresh total; moves preserve ownership. Native tests cover class separation, actual fresh-water draw without spending other classes, exact restores, empty starts and atomic malformed content rejection. The release-WASM verifier covers 16× continuation, lossless proposals, shortages and real native compilation after copied/moved tank ownership. The public browser journey authors all four classes, checks incompatible compartments, repairs capacity and removed-container errors, exercises merge/copy undo/redo, exports/reopens the creation and verifies exact physical contents and opening balances after playtest/checkpoint. Three screenshots were inspected. All 643 native tests, strict linting and the release build pass, as do the existing creator lifecycle, aged-food and group-copy verifiers. Equipment, routes, perennial/animal states and the remaining release gates remain open.

The compiler’s overnight-bed warning now uses the authored roster size. Focused checks cover one, three, four, five and twelve residents against zero, one, two or four completed homes.

Starting gardens and greenhouses now support the six native crops as empty beds, newly planted crops or harvest-ready plants, with a next-crop plan, replanting choice and initial root-zone wetness. The compiler resolves canonical native growth rather than importing arbitrary work/harvest counters. Crop moisture and physical ground water remain consistent; empty beds may also start with wet ground while retaining zero planted-crop moisture. Unsupported varieties, non-farm buildings, unfinished projects and invalid wetness are rejected atomically with map-linked diagnostics. Starting plants grant no harvest history or produced food: the native and release-WASM tests wait for an actual resident harvest before checking credited output. Copies and moves preserve the crop definition without aliasing nested data. The player inspector covers configuration/reset, crop rotation, wetness, replanting and construction-state repair through undo. The browser journey exports/reopens both planted and mature plots and restores their exact crop and ground state. Visual review caught missing crop overlays in the editor; editor/game now share the registered crop painter and loaded atlases, including captured copy previews. A pixel comparison verifies mature leaves actually replace the empty-bed image, and the updated editor/playtest screenshots were inspected. Animal occupancy/stocking and perennial states still require their own native support; production toggles are not treated as empty enclosures. The rest of Stage 5 and the full release scope remain open.

Final crop-authoring evidence: all 648 native tests pass, including wet empty-bed restoration and real resident harvesting. Strict all-target linting and the rebuilt release pass. The release-WASM crop matrix covers every supported variety/stage for gardens and greenhouses, exact 16× continuation and copy/move isolation. The final browser journey adds a pixel assertion for mature crops in the editor, valid wet empty-bed controls, construction-state repair and exact crop/soil checkpoints. Existing creator lifecycle, liquid authoring/copy ownership, compiler and group-copy verifiers also pass.

Starting orchards now support establishing trees, established trees and fruit ready for collection, plus activity and physical root-zone wetness. Canonical native orchard durations and yields drive the catalog and compiler; starting fruit creates neither collected-food history nor material inflow. Native resident-work tests and the release-WASM verifier confirm actual collection, conserved output and exact continuation. The public inspector supports all phases, pause/resume, validation, reset/undo, independent copies, export/reopen and exact playtest checkpoints. The browser screenshots were inspected; this uses the existing orchard artwork, without new phase-specific sprites or altered proportions. All 651 native tests, strict all-target linting, the release build, crop regression and object-copy checks pass. Animal occupancy/stocking, extraction configuration, utility/equipment setup and the remaining release requirements are still open.

Resource-site starting definitions now compile timber and bamboo as unplanted, newly planted or mature plots, with independent automatic replant/harvest choices and physical root-zone wetness. Earth, stone and reclaimed-material sites bind to the actual mapped finite deposit through native placement checks. Inherited sites add no establishment charge, harvest count or material inflow. Four focused native tests and the release-WASM verifier cover canonical phases, all three extraction materials, deposit ownership, real resident harvesting, exact save/16× continuation and atomic invalid loads. Strict all-target linting and release compilation pass. This is the native foundation only: player placement/inspection, resource-site editor rendering, copying and browser verification remain required before resource-site authoring is considered delivered.

The player creator now exposes timber, bamboo, earth, stone and reclaimed-material sites through a material-plot palette, native-valid map placement and a selectable inspector. Players can set renewable phases, automatic replant/harvest policy and root-zone wetness, move or duplicate a site with a new identity, remove it and undo those edits. Extraction tools reveal the matching geological overlay; ground inspection uses the actual 9- or 4-cell footprint. Editor and game share the existing resource-site painter, including empty stakes, growing timber/bamboo and extraction props; sprites retain their established geometry. The public browser journey covers invalid-draft repair, independent copy editing, movement, removal/undo, geological setup, export/reopen, native playtest and exact checkpoints. A canvas pixel check verifies that mature timber visibly replaces empty stakes. Updated editor/game screenshots were inspected. The 655-test native regression completed successfully; compiler, object-copy, release-WASM resource checks and existing crop/orchard browser journeys also pass. Animal occupancy/stocking, located equipment/utilities, broader release QA and the other unfinished stages remain required.

Goat pastures and stingless-bee apiaries now distinguish an inherited occupied enclosure from an empty one. Authors can set production policy, remaining care/inspection time, goat wellbeing, produce awaiting real collection, and an optional stocking request. Empty sites neither produce nor request feed/water or provide pollination. Stocking is normal resident work: a purchased pair of goats or bee colony must be physically delivered and consumed once when a resident settles it. Cancelling the request preserves the delivered animals; moving the enclosure preserves its state. Inherited produce grants no output or collection credit. Save version 44 preserves occupancy, rejects missing current-format occupancy fields, and migrates older occupied villages without changing their identities. Existing current-version verifier expectations were updated.

The player inspector supports configuration/reset, unfinished-project repair, undo, independent copies, export/reopen and native playtesting. Regular gameplay exposes request/cancel stocking controls and accurate empty-site status. The browser journey supplies animals through the player editor, cancels/requeues a request, observes actual resident stocking, reaches nightfall with occupied and empty pastures, and reloads the exact latest checkpoint. The release-WASM check also observes real animal hauling from camp and conserved one-time stocking through 16× continuation. All 659 native tests, strict all-target linting, the release build, existing farming/compilation/orchard/resource-site regressions, creator lifecycle and animal browser checks pass.

The empty pasture uses a separately generated edit of the existing sprite, retaining its common camera, registration and ground footprint. Day/night and editor/game captures were inspected beside residents and a home; prompt, hashes and acceptance limits are recorded in `assets/art-direction/empty-pasture-stock-state.json`. Artwork coverage, source fingerprint and grid checks include the empty state. Existing regional art and explicitly rejected character prototypes are now catalogued with their actual usage; rejected prototypes remain outside gameplay. **Why:** an author should be able to start with infrastructure awaiting animals, then let residents establish a living system through ordinary supplies and work. Poultry occupancy/stocking still needs its own material and work model; a feeding toggle is not treated as an empty coop. Utility equipment/routes, modular character artwork, remaining goal/event requirements, sharing/recovery and the full release gates remain open.

Chicken coops now support genuinely empty or occupied starts and an inherited fed laying interval, using the same animal-stocking request and resident work model as goats and bees. A tradeable three-hen group is the thirtieth stable material key. New coop construction requires one group; existing saved coop projects retain their original recipe. Save version 45 extends prior material ledgers, stores, project kits, receipts and maintenance/utility accounting without changing previous quantities or prices paid. Current-format short vectors are rejected rather than guessed; legacy recipes remain private to retained saves. A shared browser material-key list replaces remaining hard-coded basket lengths and is checked against the native catalog.

Empty coops neither consume feed nor lay eggs, and display no roaming hens. Authors can configure a stocking request or an already-fed occupied coop; already-laid eggs belong in located starting supplies. Inherited feed grants no initial egg credit: the native daylight interval must finish before eggs are produced. Production remains governed by the existing village feeding controls, rather than an ineffective per-coop toggle. Ordinary occupied-coop visits remain available. The creator now draws the existing hen artwork through the same flock and chicken painter as gameplay. No chicken or coop raster asset was changed.

The player browser journey covers empty/fed starts, invalid-time and blueprint repair, independent copies, named hen supplies, export/reopen, request cancellation/requeue, actual resident stocking, three-to-six visible birds and an exact checkpoint. Editor/game screenshots were inspected in a clear location beside residents and a home. Release-WASM checks observe actual hen hauling from camp, conserved one-time stocking input, invalid-state rejection and exact 16× continuation. New-coop verification buys hens through the real town order before autonomous construction completes. All 661 native regression tests pass, as do focused migration/state checks, strict linting, release compilation, supplier/quote checks, goat/bee and creator browser regressions, and artwork checks. **Why:** an empty coop must represent infrastructure awaiting a living flock, with real trade and resident work needed to establish it. This completes the supported livestock starting-state controls; located utility equipment/routes, modular resident artwork, remaining goals/events, sharing/recovery and release-wide biome/balance/performance gates remain open.


Utility planning now follows the authored camp instead of the old fixed arrival coordinates. Planning and installed-line validation share one compatibility table: orchards accept fresh and reclaimed irrigation water, goats accept fresh water, and coops/workshops are not offered nonexistent water tanks. Centered and moved-camp tests plan real routes, queue them through ordinary APIs and restore exactly.

Starting battery definitions now support explicit charge and recoverable hardware in completed homes, solar farms and river generators, plus standalone banks with the native 4, 18 or 36 unit capacities. Native placement rejects banks over structures, roads, material plots, the camp or protected ground. Opening energy and equipment are backed by the frozen source definition, with no generated-energy, purchase, inventory or resident-work grants. Stable authored bank identities bind to runtime IDs and survive actual movement and salvage; corrupt bindings and altered opening balances reject atomically. Native residents carry and salvage inherited banks, preserving charge during movement and discharging it exactly once when recovering one physical module.

The 666-test native regression suite passes; focused binding/conservation tests, strict all-target linting and release compilation also pass. The release-WASM verifier covers every supported building bank and standalone capacity, recoverable/unrecoverable modules, actual resident work, exact 16× continuation and invalid setup rejection. The public import/export/playtest/checkpoint journey preserves the asset definitions and exact opening balances. Editor and game screenshots were inspected using the existing battery sprite and common painter; no artwork was regenerated. Existing creator, compiler and starting-liquid checks pass. **Why:** pre-existing power assets must be intentional owned hardware and stored energy, not free ongoing generation or a global pool. This is the native battery foundation and rendering support; player-facing battery authoring controls, authored pipe/cable routes, maintenance setup and the other unfinished stages remain required.


Player-facing starting battery authoring is now implemented. Authors can place standalone banks with supported capacities and exact initial charge, select them on the map, drag or explicitly move them, duplicate independently, remove them and undo edits. Completed homes, solar farms and hydro generators expose starting charge and optional recoverable hardware in their inspector. Native diagnostics retain invalid charge/capacity/project edits for repair and prevent testing until repaired. View restoration retains the selected bank; the map reuses the existing battery artwork, including placement previews and selection footprints.

The desktop browser journey exercises placement, independent capacity/charge edits, dragging, copying, removal undo, invalid-state repair, saved selection, export and isolated playtest/checkpoint restoration. The resulting run contains exactly the authored 10.75 energy units and three recoverable modules, with no generated energy or received-material credit. Full-screen artwork/control capture was inspected; existing material-site controls pass their regression journey. **Why:** authors should set up physical starting power assets through the ordinary creation interface rather than editing exported files. Pipe/cable route authoring, maintenance setup and the remaining plan stages are still required.


Starting utility routes now have a native authoring model for fresh water, reclaimed irrigation, greywater, sealed sewage and electricity. Definitions use stable references to the camp, named buildings/material plots, standalone battery banks and authored line pumps. Explicit corridor cells preserve an author's detours; the ordinary physical port planner validates compatible direction, completed endpoints, accessible footprint edges, continuous clear inland corridors and duplicate connections. Liquid lines are resolved before their pump cables regardless of file order. Read-only source preview APIs expose compatible portable endpoints, shortest route proposals and named material kits without allocating identities in the running world.

Completed opening lines own their embedded kits through immutable source bindings, without fabricated purchase/spend receipts, material deliveries, earned labour or generated energy. Planned lines still require ordinary parts delivery and resident installation. Opening pumps participate in maintenance; flow consumes actual source water and local electricity. Residents dismantle opening lines through the normal work system and recover the usual kit once. Retired authored records stay bounded and retained so their source bindings remain auditable. Persistence checks source identity, geometry, initial ownership and exact restoration. The editor now renders imported routes with the same registered painter as gameplay.

The 670-test regression run passed. The final five focused native checks cover all utility classes, arbitrary detours, order-independent pump wiring, real construction of planned lines, real pumping/energy use, resident dismantling/recovery, invalid route/binding rejection and exact saves. Strict all-target linting and release compilation pass. The release-WASM verifier checks portable previews, conserved opening hardware, actual pumping and resident recovery, exact 16× continuation and atomic invalid setup rejection. A public browser journey imports these definitions, renders the routes, saves/reopens/exports them and restores an isolated playtest checkpoint; full-screen captures were reviewed. Existing battery lifecycle checks also pass. **Why:** an inherited village needs real installed infrastructure, while unfinished connections must retain the normal resource and resident-work tradeoffs. This is the native route foundation and preview rendering; ordinary player route creation/editing controls, equipment-condition setup and the remaining plan stages are still required.


The regular player creator now includes a Pipes and cables panel for all five supported utility classes. Authors choose compatible typed endpoints, inspect native shortest-route and material-kit previews, or draw a continuous corridor between visible supply/receiving ports. Installed versus planned starts use the same native ownership and resident-work rules. Drawing back over a stroke trims its tail instead of creating repeated-cell loops. Invalid strokes remain transient and cannot be applied. Route selection/highlighting, locate, editing, removal (explicitly including dependent pump cables), keyboard deletion, erase and undo use the ordinary editor flow. Route proposals are discarded when source edits invalidate them. Imported endpoint field ordering does not affect selection, and pump labels include their locations.

Rerouting keeps the original route identity and preserves downstream cables. Moving a pump may leave a cable as an explicit invalid draft; the author can select and reroute that cable, and testing remains blocked until repaired. Scratch planning omits only the connection being edited and its dependent pump cables; applying edits preserves the actual document's other connections. The desktop browser journey creates all five classes through the public UI, exports a drawn detour, rejects a stroke through a building, repairs a displaced pump cable, removes/undoes a linked line, imports reordered endpoint fields, retains map selection after reopening, exports portable source definitions and verifies installed/planned state plus an exact playtest checkpoint. Battery and material-site browser regressions pass. Full-screen controls and map rendering were reviewed. **Why:** players should be able to design and revise an inherited village's infrastructure without editing files or bypassing physical ownership. Equipment-condition authoring, the remaining goals/events and Story adapter, modular resident artwork, sharing/recovery and the full release gates remain unfinished.


Starting equipment condition now uses portable typed references to workshop tools, building battery systems, standalone banks, installed water-line pumps and the shared pickup. The compiler accepts one finite 0–100 percent condition per existing unit and rejects duplicates, missing assets, unfinished structures and uninstalled pump hardware. Overrides seed actual native records at the opening date with zero observed exposure/use and no repair jobs or material credit. Authors may choose automatic service globally or defer automatic requests for individual units. Disabled maintenance retains authored condition without aging or operating penalties; enabling it uses the same physical record.

A player-facing Starting equipment condition section lists real native assets and their locations, condition, due/failed status, manual-service deferral, healthy defaults and locate controls. Invalid values remain repairable drafts and block playtesting. Native scratch previews are read-only; settings survive export/reopen and exact run checkpoints. In-game upkeep displays the authored failed pickup and pump with the existing normal service controls. Source settings never reapply over progressed saves: real use, moves and attended repairs continue normally.

All 675 native regression tests pass, including four focused source-condition checks. Strict all-target linting and release compilation also pass. The release-WASM journey verifies every supported asset type, invalid setup atomicity, zero fabricated wear/work/parts, real resident repair using its kit, condition following a moved battery, disabled-rule persistence and exact 16× continuation. The public browser journey authors all six opening units, changes automatic policy and per-unit deferral, repairs an invalid percentage, resets/undoes defaults, toggles dormant rules, exports/reopens, reads the resulting condition in normal gameplay upkeep and restores an exact checkpoint. Editor and game captures were inspected; the pipe/cable authoring regression also passes. **Why:** inherited-village scenarios need believable worn infrastructure and genuine repair tradeoffs rather than pretending that all equipment is new or inventing past service work. Broader population-aware setup feedback, remaining objective/event and Story-adapter work, modular resident artwork, sharing/recovery and release-wide biome/balance/performance gates remain open.


The creator now shows an Opening needs snapshot for the actual cast: overnight beds and friends staying elsewhere, ready-to-eat stock versus one shared meal, fresh water versus current native refill targets, stock/reserved space versus capacity, shared daytime shelter and animal groups needing feeding/care. The compiler adds quantified, non-blocking warnings for insufficient beds, less than one edible portion per friend, food expiring within the first game hour, low initial fresh-water buffers, no rain cistern, full material storage, missing opening livestock feed and occupied apiaries without native forage. Mature crops, uncollected produce and eggs are not treated as edible stock. Empty or already-fed enclosures do not acquire fictitious feed demand. Hens' food alternative accounts for the native raw-food threshold between successive cycles and fodder competition with goat care.

Housing uses the same bed constant as resident assignment; water targets come directly from native restocking demand, and feeding quantities share the actual spending constants. These are opening counts and care/refill targets, not predicted daily diets or a proof of future solvability. Relaxed Sandbox replenishment appears as Information rather than false finite-resource shortages; disabled food ageing produces no expiry warning. Invalid drafts hide old numeric readiness values instead of presenting the last valid world's counts as current. Map-linked animal notes and supply/water/cast links lead to ordinary player tools. Quantity dialogs distinguish non-blocking setup notes from actual invalid inputs, and the review can expand beyond its first 40 notes.

The 681-test regression run passes; final focused coverage includes seven setup tests and the ordinary three-bed shelter test. Strict all-target linting and the final release compilation pass. Release-WASM checks cover 1, 5 and 12 friends, portable warning/Information semantics, actual water targets, active/disabled spoilage, read-only summaries, unchanged time/materials and exact restoration. The desktop player journey authors a twelve-person arrival, changes supplies/housing/animals/rules, follows map/tool links, keeps intentional warnings playable, repairs invalid data without stale counts, exports/reopens and starts an unchanged paused playtest. Existing supply and liquid allocation/distribution browser journeys pass, and the full-screen readiness capture was inspected. **Why:** scenario authors need concrete feedback about the friends and owned supplies they have actually configured, without an optimistic forecast or hidden resource grants. Longer-horizon/access/sustainability checks, inherited building salvage policy, remaining resident configuration/artwork, goals/events and the Story adapter, sharing/recovery and full release QA remain required.


Completed buildings now have an explicit inherited structural-material policy: no declared recovery (the default), or half the structural materials from the selected native construction recipe, rounded down to whole units. The shared catalog previews named recoverable quantities for every supported building/recipe, including adobe houses. Structural fabric, fittings and panels are included; battery modules follow their separate ownership model, while pumps, consumed tools, food, plants and livestock are excluded. A blueprint cannot declare inherited fabric and remains governed by materials residents actually deliver and use. Copies retain independent source policies, and changing the recipe updates the native preview.

Recovery resolves the frozen source building identity and persists through actual moves. It does not add loose starting inventory, consumption receipts, purchases, material spending or construction participation. Paid receipts and declared fabric describe one structure rather than two grants. Ordinary resident salvage releases the declared fraction once, demolition releases none, and removing an owned roof battery's building leaves its charged module as separate equipment without adding battery stock. Normal storage-capacity and resident-work gates remain in effect. Saves retain the source policy; moved and queued salvage checkpoints restore exactly.

All 689 native regression tests pass, including seven focused inherited-recovery checks. Strict all-target linting and release compilation pass. Release-WASM checks cover named catalogs for all supported recipes, no-recovery defaults, atomic invalid policy/project rejection, real resident moving and salvage, one-time returns, separate charged battery retention and exact 16× continuation. The public browser journey sets an adobe home's policy, compares the recipe preview, repairs an invalid blueprint, independently edits a copy, exports/reopens the definition, queues salvage through normal gameplay controls, observes the resident completion and restored materials/battery, and reloads an exact checkpoint. Editor/game captures were inspected; the starting-equipment browser regression also passes. **Why:** an inherited structure can contain recoverable physical materials without pretending the new community purchased or built it. Remaining resident starting-state configuration/artwork, longer-term/access/sustainability validation, objective/event and Story-adapter work, sharing/recovery and the full release gates remain open.

### Resident starting circumstances — implemented

Scenario authors can set each friend's opening energy, hunger and social fulfilment, plus a clear starting point or the ordinary group arrival. These belong to the scenario under stable cast-member identities; reusable resident presets and duplicates retain independent default circumstances. Wellbeing is derived from the selected needs. Starting choices do not grant cargo, work history, earned progress or resources, and normal autonomy decides subsequent actions. Positions must be walkable, connected to the arrival clearing and at least 1.1 cells from other starting residents. Invalid numeric drafts remain repairable and cannot launch.

The character creator's Arrival tab supports default/reset actions, map placement and locate; the map supports resident selection and dragging through native validation. Removal clears the person's scenario setup, Undo restores it, and saved projects, portable exports and isolated playtests retain authored circumstances. A delayed modal-close event is guarded when reopening after map placement. The full 694-test native regression run, five focused starting-state tests, strict all-target linting and release compilation pass. Release-WASM checks prove exact opening values, atomic rejection, stable identity bindings and exact 16× continuation. Public browser checks cover editing, invalid-value repair, map placement/selection, rejected occupied locations, reset/removal undo, independent duplicates, export/reopen and native playtest values; existing animated character preview and character-choice regressions pass. The complete Arrival screen was visually reviewed. **Why:** an exhausted or hungry arriving friend should be an intentional scenario circumstance governed by normal human needs, without contaminating reusable character identity or scripting their next activity. Modular resident artwork, remaining objective/event and Story-adapter work, longer-horizon/access validation, sharing/recovery and full biome release gates remain unfinished.

### Living-land objectives — implemented

The native journal condition catalog now includes selected planted/harvest-ready crop beds, established/fruit-ready orchards, occupied/currently cared-for animal enclosures, and planted/harvestable material plots. Each supports a bounded count and an optional named region requiring the entire footprint. Crops use their actual current variety and native maturity duration; orchard establishment and uncollected fruit are separate. Animal counts mean enclosures rather than boxed animals or individual headcounts. Current care uses real goat-care periods, bee inspections plus nearby flowering forage, and paid active hen-feeding batches. Material readiness uses active established plots, native growth, remaining deposits and actual batch quantities. Cleared plots, construction projects and buildings undergoing resident removal or relocation do not qualify.

These are explicit current-state conditions: inherited assets may count, without granting harvested output, extraction receipts or care-work history. They also work in bounded All/Any groups and native event thresholds/guards. Rule, material/stage and region validation rejects unsupported setups; conservative unlock checks detect crop and livestock goals requiring their own still-locked building. The ordinary journal editor exposes all four types, compatible phase choices and repairable numeric errors. Gameplay shows native measurements and explanations; existing relevant plots link to their management controls, missing plots retain building shortcuts, and material goals link to Land & materials.

The 703-test native regression run passes, with nine focused living-land tests covering all crop varieties, real orchard collection and deposit depletion, current care versus occupancy, full footprints, active building-work exclusion, disabled rules, invalid-condition atomicity, unlock loops, threshold grants and exact saves. Strict all-target linting and release compilation pass. Release-WASM checks cover inherited assets without fabricated activity, once-only threshold support and exact 16× continuation. The public browser journey authors all four conditions, selects regions, repairs invalid targets with Undo, switches compatible extraction phases, exports/reopens, reads actual gameplay measurements, follows plot/material links and restores an exact checkpoint. Existing engine and player-journal regressions pass; full-screen editor and game captures were inspected. **Why:** a scenario about farming and resource stewardship must distinguish living, cared-for assets from merely having finished their buildings. These current-state goals do not substitute for the still-required configurable welfare/service reliability conditions, challenge deadlines and eligibility, resident-bound speakers/roles and official Story adapter, modular resident art, sharing/recovery, longer-term validation or complete three-biome release gates.

### Configurable resident wellbeing periods — implemented

Authors can require either every founding resident or one stable cast-member identity to meet chosen energy, fullness, connection and wellbeing thresholds for 1–10,080 consecutive observed game minutes. The native simulation observes actual residents every step, including those asleep or away; names, roster order, looks and outside commitments do not change the scope. Each activated goal owns its own bounded observation records. A failing need or an unobserved clock gap resets the period; earlier healthy time cannot complete a later chapter. Pauses add no time. Event thresholds and guards observe from scenario start and preserve one-shot effect accounting. Completed goals and events retain their final observation without forcing residents to keep those needs forever.

The ordinary journal editor exposes scope, threshold and duration choices, with fullness converted correctly to native hunger. Removing a referenced friend retains an actionable invalid draft rather than retargeting their goal. Undo restores the original identity. Player journals show native elapsed progress and matching-person counts, distinguish the latest observation from a frozen final one, and link to the selected friend or the community. The ordinary resident detail sheet also displays native wellbeing alongside energy, fullness and connection, so every requested threshold can be inspected. Saves validate the exact expected observation keys, population, timestamps and continuous-period bounds, plus consistency between current incomplete observations and saved residents. Missing, extra, backdated, future, stale and inconsistent records are rejected atomically; completed historical observations remain valid when needs later change.

The 712-test native regression run passes. The final focused run passes ten wellbeing checks, including lapse/recovery, absent observation time, later-chapter activation, one/five/twelve-person rosters, reordered stable identities, live cosmetic edits, sleep/off-site scope, all four need thresholds, malformed references and timers, nested event conditions, once-only funding and exact partial/final saves. Strict all-target linting and release compilation pass. Release-WASM checks cover actual population-aware periods, paused time, one-time support and exact 16× continuation. The public browser journey authors and exports a scoped period and a wellbeing-triggered funding event with explicit scenario-start observation, repairs invalid thresholds and a removed friend, reopens the project, earns a real partial period at the ordinary manual speed, reloads its exact checkpoint, opens the correct resident and matching native wellbeing meter, and observes completion plus one-time funding. Existing native and player-journal regressions pass; creator, partial-period and completed-goal full-screen captures were reviewed. **Why:** a story about friends building a home needs goals about sustained comfort and rest, with trustworthy timing and saved progress. Configurable accessible-service quantities/reliability, challenge deadlines and continuation eligibility, resident-bound dialogue/roles and the official Story adapter, modular resident artwork, sharing/recovery, long-horizon validation and complete three-biome release gates remain required.

### Quantified service periods — implemented

Scenario goals and event conditions can now sustain a chosen minimum of accessible edible portions, accessible owned fresh water, residents with reachable assigned homes, usable reachable ecological toilets, or fully illuminated streetlights after dark. Targets and 15–10,080-minute periods are bounded; food needs located material logistics, water/sanitation/lighting need local utilities, and a housed-person target cannot exceed the founding cast. Conservative unlock checks reject housing, toilet or lamp goals requiring their own still-locked building. These service conditions can be combined with the separate wellbeing conditions when a chapter should require both working infrastructure and comfortable friends.

Access uses native routes and approaches. Food counts actual dated edible portions after claims; eggs requiring cooking, cargo, pending orders and committed ingredients cannot inflate the reserve. Fresh water excludes irrigation, sealed processing contents, carried loads, incoming water and the neighbouring service. Shared access policy checks residents on the property, using a visitor from the arrival clearing when all are away. Housing uses actual assignment and excludes homes closing for resident work; a shed does not become a bed. Toilets require local handwashing water and real greywater/sewage room. Night lighting uses actual enabled connections, charge and illumination, and cannot earn time in daylight. Opening assets may establish availability, without fabricating work or output history.

The shared observation owner and period-clock helpers now serve wellbeing and service conditions. Route-based services are observed every fifteen game minutes; known supply losses, structure/layout changes and participation changes prompt earlier checks. Lighting is checked each native step. Native metadata and both editor/player wording expose this cadence, latest/final availability and readiness. Periods begin with their first qualifying observation after activation, reset on failed or late checks, preserve pauses and save exactly; completed observations remain historical credit. Per-step readings are shared across matching goals/events, and signature ordering uses stable resident identities. The journal offers ordinary food, water/sanitation, homes and power shortcuts. Changing service types selects that type's default target with normal Undo, while invalid quantities, population targets and rule combinations remain repairable drafts.

All 725 native regression tests pass, including twelve focused service checks and the existing wellbeing tests after the refactor. Coverage includes real stock sales and water consumption, food ageing before meal arrival, inaccessible reserves across the river, all-away access, native bed counts and resident demolition, local sanitation buffers, disconnected/disabled/daylight lighting, later chapter activation, unsupported rules/targets, invalid or stale saved periods, one-time events and exact continuation with a reordered twelve-person cast. Strict all-target linting and release compilation pass. Release-WASM checks prove all five services, declared cadences, no hidden resource/time grants, completed periods and exact partial/final 16× continuation. The public browser journey authors all five conditions and a service event, repairs invalid targets and rules, saves/exports/reopens, earns genuine mixed partial/completed periods, reloads an exact checkpoint, follows every management shortcut and observes completion plus one-time support. Existing native-journal and player wellbeing journeys pass; full-screen authoring, partial and completed captures were inspected. **Why:** an inherited building count does not prove that friends can reach supplies, wash safely, sleep at home or use charged lights. Wider operational building conditions, challenge deadlines and continuation eligibility, resident-bound dialogue/roles and the official Story adapter, modular resident artwork, sharing/recovery, long-horizon validation and all three biome release gates remain unfinished.

### Challenge deadline foundation — incremental implementation

Authored journals can now declare an optional absolute game-minute deadline and a bounded failure message. Native validation requires a finite deadline after arrival. The simulation shortens its final step to the exact boundary, evaluates that observation before declaring failure, and freezes subsequent simulation steps after an unmet deadline. Successful challenges continue simulating normally. The native journal report exposes active, succeeded and failed outcomes; saves validate the deadline result against the immutable authored definition and exact clock.

The 57 journal tests pass, including inclusive-boundary completion, exact failure freeze, successful post-deadline play, failure save/reload and rejection of fabricated failure timestamps. Strict all-target linting passes. **Why:** browser frame timing and accelerated playback must not change whether a player meets a challenge. This is a native foundation only: ordinary creator controls, a visible failure result, restart and explicitly separate unrestricted continuation, eligibility tracking and browser verification remain required before challenge delivery is complete.

### Player-authored timed challenges and free continuation

The ordinary journal editor now offers an optional deadline using day/time controls and a custom failure message. These edits participate in native validation, Undo, saved drafts and portable exports. A failed challenge automatically opens its journal result when no other dialog is open, with separate Restart challenge and Continue freely actions. Restart compiles the original frozen scenario in the existing session, covering both scratch playtests and independent village saves. Free continuation records the original failure permanently, releases authored building locks, resumes simulation and allows rule changes. Later chapter completion remains visible as village progress but cannot display original challenge success. Timed runs retain their authored simulation rules until success or explicit free continuation; native setters and save validation enforce this boundary.

The 729-test native regression run passes. Additional focused checks pass for the final rule guards and once-only effects at the deadline: an event's payment cannot cascade into a goal in that same observation, and reloading/continuing cannot repeat it. Strict all-target linting and release compilation pass. Release-WASM checks cover paused time, actual 16× playback, inclusive success, exact partial/failed/continued saves, late goal completion without challenge eligibility and atomic rejection of fabricated continuation decisions. Public browser journeys exercise authoring and invalid-date Undo, export/reopen, automatic failure, exact frozen time, restart, free continuation and checkpoint reload in both scratch and independently saved runs. Existing engine-journal and public player-journal regressions pass. Full-screen creator, failure and free-continuation captures were inspected; result/chapter spacing was corrected. **Why:** a missed deadline should offer a clear player decision without discarding the village or misrepresenting a later recovery as winning the original challenge.

Wider operational-building objectives, resident-bound dialogue/roles and the official Story adapter, modular resident artwork, complete sharing/recovery and library presentation, long-horizon validation and the complete three-biome release gates remain required. This milestone does not complete the overall builder plan.

### Required story roles and resident-bound dialogue

Custom journals now support up to sixteen named required roles, each assigned to an authored friend or a supported starting skill and minimum level. Capability roles require resident learning and resolve from the immutable founding setup: highest declared starting level first, then stable authored identity for ties. They do not wait for future learning or change speakers as people rest, commute, learn, rename themselves or change appearance. One friend may hold several roles. Native validation reports missing friends, unfilled capabilities, duplicate/oversized roles and missing speaker roles without silently substituting an original cast member.

The ordinary event editor exposes role authoring, the actual native assignment preview and a message-speaker selector covering village narration, a particular friend or a role. Removing a referenced role/person keeps the broken reference visible for repair; Undo restores it. Changing to a non-message effect clears the inapplicable speaker. Role definitions and speaker references remain in draft history, exports, immutable scenario provenance and playable saves. The player journal attributes applied messages to the selected live person's name and portrait, shows their role and opens the correct person sheet. Names, titles and messages are escaped. Portrait painting reuses prepared frames, skips already-painted frames and scopes journal refreshes to journal canvases.

All 736 native tests pass, including six role checks covering default/one/twelve-person casts, repeated templates, reordered identities, highest-skill selection and deterministic ties, missing-person/role/capability repair, bounds, incompatible effects, live learning/cosmetic changes, exact saves and atomic invalid-source rejection. Strict all-target linting and release compilation pass. Release-WASM checks verify actual source/runtime identities, read-only reports and pause, one-shot attribution, reordered casts and exact 16× continuation. The public browser journey authors roles and dialogue, repairs invalid skill targets and removed roles/friends with Undo, exports/reopens the draft, opens the actual speaker, changes their live name/appearance and restores an exact checkpoint. Existing native event/unlock verifiers and the public journal/ordinary Story journey pass. Creator, player and renamed-speaker captures were inspected. **Why:** a player-created story needs to belong to its own friends rather than depend on a hard-coded original resident slot.

The official Story adapter, wider operational-building goals, complete modular character artwork, full sharing/recovery and library presentation, long-horizon validation and complete three-biome release gates remain required. This milestone does not complete the overall builder plan.

### Official Story opening adapter and run-wide milestones

The official Story's opening chapter now delegates its requirements to the shared authored-condition evaluator. The bridge has a stable chapter identity and five stable objective identities. Its authored definition is exposed in the native content catalog and checked by the shared scenario compiler; the same definition also compiles as an ordinary custom journal. New bounded milestone conditions expose the simulation's actual founding arrival, cooked meals eaten and mornings reached, with explicit run-wide credit including activity before activation. The ordinary creator supports all three milestones, native bounds, Undo and export.

The bridge deliberately keeps one simultaneous All requirement and the existing permanent campaign receipt. A cistern, one home and a kitchen must still be standing when arrival, first harvest and a cooked meal qualify. Visiting those requirements separately and removing an asset before the final requirement cannot earn the chapter. After completion, the existing receipt survives asset/resource loss and old saves retain their achievements. The chapter still opens food forest, storage and orchard possibilities and advances to Roots for Tomorrow once; a second home and next morning remain optional guidance. The other twelve campaign chapters stay behind their existing adapter. The player journal reads first-chapter measurements by stable objective identity from the native report rather than reproducing its completion conditions in JavaScript. Campaign pacing scripts now pass that report explicitly.

The 740-test native regression run passes. Focused checks compare all 64 requirement combinations across four building-progress boundaries, simultaneous versus permanent credit, unchanged unlocks/next chapter, source compilation and exact saves. An additional sunrise test passes: authoring a later start date grants no historical morning, paused time grants none, and crossing the actual sunrise boundary earns the milestone with exact reload. Strict all-target linting and release compilation pass. Release-WASM play checks complete the opening through real arrival, a requested harvest and a cooked meal at 16×, compare legacy/native/UI measurements, preserve exact partial/final continuation, prove objective-order independence, and validate one-home/no-extra-morning completion, one-shot celebration, existing unlocks and the standalone authored bridge. The public browser journey authors milestones, repairs an invalid target with Undo, exports/reopens, imports an authored Story property, requests actual harvesting/cooking at 8×, observes the correct chapter completion and restores an exact checkpoint. Scenario compiler, custom engine journal, ordinary player journal and thirteen-chapter objective/route regressions pass. Creator and initial/completed Story journal captures were inspected. **Why:** proving one real campaign chapter against shared creation rules catches semantic differences before migrating more Story content, while preserving existing village progress.

The required one-chapter Story parity bridge is now implemented. Wider operational-building goals, complete modular resident artwork, full sharing/recovery and library presentation, long-horizon feasibility validation and complete three-biome release gates remain required. Converting the remaining twelve Story chapters is an incremental campaign task, not a replacement for those builder deliverables.

### Native operating-building objectives

The ordinary condition catalog now supports bounded counts of buildings in a supported current operating state, optionally requiring the entire footprint inside a named region. Ready production covers kitchens and workshops with an unfinished supplied/committed batch that can accept work. Production under way additionally requires actual work already completed on that unfinished batch, without claiming current worker attendance. Treatment under way covers advancing reed-bed and biodigester batches and active banana beds with locally delivered sewage. Generation covers solar farms and river generators using the actual per-building electrical rate, with weather, shade and equipment effects. Night lighting requires an enabled, connected, fully supplied streetlight during darkness. This gives eight building types explicit operating criteria; growing plots, orchards, livestock and material sites retain their existing living-land conditions, while population services retain their separate accessible-service goals.

The evaluator reuses production readiness, passive-process opening/phase checks and the electrical engine rather than inventing an operating flag. Empty, paused, closing, output-blocked and power-starved sites do not qualify merely because construction finished. Generation does not mistake stored charge for current output. The source validator checks supported building/state pairs, real rule prerequisites, count limits, named regions and direct unlock deadlocks, including nested event conditions. Completed goals remain credited if operation later stops. The creator exposes the supported combinations, state descriptions, rule errors, Undo, event thresholds and portable draft persistence. The player journal opens the actual existing building for review. A discovered description replacement bug was corrected so translating a building label does not also rewrite an authored region name containing the same word.

The 749-test native regression run passes; the final focused run passes nine operation checks, including the additional biodigester feed-toggle and exact-save check. Coverage includes actual delivered inputs and attended partial work, empty/paused/manual/powered workstations, owned passive inputs, banana sewage, charged solar batteries at night, connected/disabled/daylight lamps, full regional footprints, resident demolition, source bounds/rules and unlock loops. Strict all-target linting and release compilation pass. Release-WASM checks exercise all four operating states through real production, treatment, river generation and night supply, without fabricated opening output or treatment history, plus one-time funding and exact partial/final 16× continuation. The public browser journey authors all states and a threshold event, repairs invalid counts/rules with Undo, selects a region, exports/reopens, follows the correct building link, observes real 8× village operation and one-time support, and restores an exact checkpoint. Existing native and public player-journal regressions pass. Final creator, partial and completed journal renders were inspected; operation copy was shortened and engine terminology removed from player-facing descriptions. **Why:** a scenario about useful infrastructure needs evidence that a finished building has its inputs, can work, is processing, or is supplying power, rather than rewarding an idle sprite alone.

Complete modular resident artwork, full sharing/recovery and library presentation, longer-horizon feasibility validation and all three biome release gates remain required. The overall builder plan remains in progress.

### Frozen-release storage and package foundation

Native scenario parsing now accepts a strict self-contained release envelope with its own identity, matching content/map revisions, title/description, author display name, bounded source credits and explicit game/rules/art compatibility versions. Releases require resolved terrain, vegetation, deposits, biome, weather and cast, then pass through the ordinary compiler. Unsupported, malformed, unresolved or inconsistent packages leave the running village unchanged. Three focused native package tests, strict linting and release compilation pass.

The IndexedDB repository now separates immutable releases from drafts, presets and playthroughs. Freezing reads the expected durable draft inside one transaction, validates the complete package with the native compiler and allocates an independent increasing release number. A durable counter prevents version reuse after deletion; concurrent tabs receive distinct versions. Imports create fresh local library entries while preserving original package identity and authorship. Release-backed runs retain their own definition and release metadata when drafts/releases are removed. Browser repository checks prove these properties, failed-write atomicity, stale-draft conflicts, exact run reload and physically equivalent resolved opening worlds in all three biomes. Existing content-library recovery/conflict/playtest checks pass. **Why:** player-facing release controls need storage that cannot accidentally turn later draft edits into changes to a shared scenario. Public release controls, complete library presentation, migration/recovery/export journeys and broader sharing QA still need implementation and verification.

### Player-facing frozen releases and portable sharing

The regular creator now offers Save release with an author display name and clear separation from later draft edits. It resolves any implicit map, weather and cast data through an undoable draft transaction, compiles the complete package natively and saves an independently numbered immutable release. The library lists releases separately from drafts and saved villages, with version/author, Play, Edit a copy, Export and explicit deletion controls. Play compiles the full package, not just its embedded definition. Running villages retain and display their release version/author. Removing a draft or release leaves those villages usable. Busy controls prevent repeated save/launch actions, and the release dialog retains the game's field, button and focus styling.

Release imports create separate local entries. Editable copies receive new creation/map identities and keep the original package's source credits; ordinary draft copies preserve those credits as well. New releases from a copy add its own author without discarding the source attribution. Unsupported imports leave the current draft and saved data unchanged. Retained releases requiring another game/rules/art version keep an original-export action while play/copy are disabled. Even a stale compatible library header cannot bypass the native package compatibility check. Native errors now appear inside the library instead of a toast behind its modal.

All 753 native regression tests pass, along with strict native linting and release compilation. Browser repository checks additionally prove exact opening-world equivalence when resolving implicit legacy cast/biome/weather and each of the three explicit biomes, transaction/version conflict behaviour and independent frozen content. An actual version-2 IndexedDB fixture upgrades without rewriting its draft, view, revision, headers or village snapshot; that original village still restores exactly and the migrated draft can make its first release. The public browser journey creates two versions, proves that an old export survives draft edits, plays the original with its author/version, continues after draft/release deletion, imports into a fresh entry, makes a credited editable copy and rejects a newer unsupported file without changing the current project. Recovery fixtures prove unsupported retained data stays exportable, stale headers cannot create an incompatible run, and existing releases remain untouched. Existing library recovery/conflict/playtest and public custom/ordinary Story journal regressions pass. Release dialog, library, running village and recovery screens were inspected. **Why:** players need to share a stable version of their creation and keep playing their own village without depending on another editable draft or losing original authorship.

This completes the basic scenario-release save/share/play loop, not the entire storage/library release gate. Library thumbnails/search and richer navigation, standalone map-package handling and package-version coverage for resident presets, wider damaged-record recovery/export paths, complete modular resident artwork, longer-horizon feasibility validation, curated biome scenarios and all three biome release gates remain required. The overall plan remains in progress.

### Library discovery and collection navigation

The regular library now provides accent-insensitive, case-insensitive word search over available title/author/version headers, collection filters for drafts, frozen releases and saved villages, name/recent ordering within each collection, a clear-filters action and explicit empty/no-match feedback. Search and sorting reuse lightweight headers without loading or writing map/release/save bodies. Release-backed village headers retain their release author and version when created or refreshed, allowing those villages to be found alongside their source releases. Existing collections collapse the new-village chooser by default; its ordinary biome and starting-template choices remain one explicit expansion away. Newly saved/imported releases clear stale filters so their resulting entry is visible.

The public release journey now checks uppercase unaccented author search against accented names, matching release and saved-village entries, positive and combined collection filters, no matches, clearing, name ordering, accessible starting templates and discovery resets after import/new release. It also re-exports the original release after filtering to prove content remains unchanged. The full release/create/copy/play/delete/recovery flow still passes. Unsupported retained-release recovery/export and the existing custom/ordinary Story journal browser journey pass; JavaScript syntax and whitespace checks pass. The compact desktop library render was inspected. **Why:** finding an existing village should stay manageable as players accumulate editable drafts, shared versions and independent playthroughs.

Thumbnail generation/presentation, richer metadata and older-header enrichment, standalone map packages, resident-preset package version coverage, broader damaged-record recovery, full modular resident artwork, longer-horizon feasibility checks, curated biome scenarios and complete biome release gates remain required. This is a discovery increment, not completion of the entire library or builder plan.

### Library metadata upgrade

The version-4 library upgrade enriches older release-backed saved-village headers with valid author, content revision and compatibility metadata. It reads one candidate body at a time and changes only missing or invalid discovery fields, preserving existing valid header values, custom fields, revisions, timestamps and all saved bodies. Unknown future compatibility versions remain explicit rather than being treated as supported. **Why:** existing villages should become discoverable by author without requiring players to reopen and save them.

Browser verification covers a real version-3 database with incomplete, complete, malformed, missing-body and playtest records, byte-equivalent saved-body reads and stable reopening. The existing version-2 upgrade still preserves an exactly restorable native village; incompatible-release recovery/export controls also pass. Thumbnails, standalone map packages, resident-preset versioning, broader recovery, modular resident art, long-horizon validation and the complete three-biome release gates remain required.

### Painted library previews

Draft saves, new scenario villages, frozen releases and imported supported releases now receive locally derived 480×300 WebP opening-landscape thumbnails. A detached mode of the existing world renderer uses the actual painted terrain, vegetation, buildings, crops and residents while excluding editor grids, authored region labels, camp/camera markers and selection. Draft previews regenerate once per changed document revision; ordinary camera-only saves reuse them. **Why:** players should recognize a property visually while choosing between editable work, a frozen version and an independent village.

Version-5 IndexedDB stores bounded previews separately from headers, content and snapshots. Saves/deletions update their own preview atomically; releases and runs retain independent images. No shared package embeds an image, no external image URL is accepted, and missing/failed previews leave library actions available with an explicit placeholder. Older entries gain previews when edited/saved or newly launched; old frozen releases are not rewritten solely to add artwork.

`verify-creation-thumbnails.mjs` verifies distinct deterministic previews across Mata Atlântica, Cerrado and Caatinga, unchanged definitions/native snapshots, image dimensions/size bounds, separate persistence, stale-write atomicity, ordinary-save retention, remote-image rejection and deletion cleanup. The public release journey verifies displayed draft/release/run/import previews and preservation of the original release image after draft edits. The library and three-biome screenshots were inspected. Existing content-library recovery/conflict/playtest checks, version-2 exact village restoration and version-3 header enrichment pass with the new store. This completes the basic opening-preview path; lazy previews for untouched historical entries, standalone map packages, resident-preset package versions, broader recovery, full modular character art, long-horizon validation and complete three-biome release gates remain open.

### Versioned resident sharing and discovery

New `.solar-resident` packages use format 2 with a stable package identity, positive content revision, independent title/description, author display name, explicit game/rules/art compatibility and bounded source-credit metadata. The native validator requires these fields, rejects unknown or incompatible versions before cast insertion and retains the existing no-runtime-history/no-village-identity and skill-budget rules. Format-1 presets remain supported without silently inventing their historical compatibility metadata. **Why:** shared people need the same explicit compatibility and authorship boundaries as shared scenarios.

Saving a resident now opens a themed metadata form. The resident collection displays version/authorship, searches accent-insensitively by resident name, package title or author, provides no-match feedback and creates independent local copies preserving the exact original package. Imports clear stale search filters; unsupported retained artwork remains losslessly exportable. Package metadata stays separate from the actual person definition and cannot change their skills or appearance.

Focused native package tests, strict all-target lint, release WASM compilation and 320 generated-character checks pass. The public browser journey verifies authored metadata, distinct title/name searching, escaped authorship, exact duplicate exports, independent deletion, all three compatibility rejections, durable cross-project reuse, undo/reload, skill budgets, actual playtesting and export of retained unavailable artwork. Save/library screenshots were inspected. The full native regression run passes all 754 tests (297.79 seconds). Resident portrait thumbnails, attribution propagation when residents are embedded and subsequently reshared, standalone map packages, broader recovery, complete modular artwork, longer-horizon feasibility validation and complete three-biome release gates remain required.

### Resident collection portraits

The resident collection now displays locally derived portraits using the same approved standing frames as the character creator. Both surfaces share a crop-and-fit renderer that preserves painted proportions rather than stretching faces to different canvas aspect ratios.
