Current resident headers carry their lightweight appearance recipe and package schema, so modern portrait rows do not load complete preset bodies. Historical headers resolve their recipe with a read-only fallback, including the original founding look when no cosmetic override was authored. **Why:** players should recognize saved people while choosing whom to bring into a village, without a portrait silently changing or replacing their character.

Unavailable artwork versions, unknown appearance parts and incompatible package versions show an explicit portrait-unavailable placeholder; original exports and existing use-time native validation remain available. Drawing portraits does not rewrite packages, revisions or historical records. `verify-resident-portraits.mjs` checks all five distinct bundled looks, default-look resolution, nonempty rendered pixels, exactly one historical body read, unchanged records and absence of unsupported-art substitutions. The library and five-look contact sheet were inspected. The existing character-creator browser journey passes animated poses, facing, day/night, shared scale, reduced motion, keyboard tabs, editing/undo/reload and native playtesting; the full preset-sharing browser journey also passes with real portrait/placeholder assertions. This is a JavaScript-only increment; the previous native baseline remains 754 passing tests.

Attribution propagation through embedded and reshared cast members, standalone map packages, wider recovery, complete modular resident artwork, longer-horizon feasibility validation and all three biome release gates remain required. The overall plan remains in progress.

### Resident source credits across casts and resharing

Authored residents now retain bounded source-credit metadata alongside their scenario definition, separate from gameplay identity, skills and runtime history. Adding a versioned resident preset merges its earlier credits with the package author's credit. Cast duplication preserves independent copies of those credits; legacy presets do not receive invented authorship. Resaving a resident carries the earlier credits into the new package, with the new author recorded separately. The save form displays the retained sources as escaped text in a bounded scrolling area. **Why:** sharing a village or adapting a neighbour should not erase the people who authored its founding cast.

Exact duplicate credits are combined; conflicting author entries remain distinct. More than sixteen unique credits blocks addition with visible feedback instead of truncating attribution. Malformed source metadata is rejected by the native compiler and the public scenario importer before replacing a draft. Scene exports, frozen scenario packages and independently saved villages retain credits inside their embedded cast definitions; source packages are never rewritten.

Focused native checks prove exact save/restore and physically identical opening worlds when only attribution differs. Pure JavaScript checks cover source chains, immutable inputs, independent cast copies, exact deduplication, conflicting credits, unknown formats, legacy handling and bounded refusal. The public browser journey verifies reuse/duplication, an overlong chain leaving the cast unchanged and original export intact, visible source credits, resharing under a new author, draft reload/export, malformed-import rejection, frozen release export, actual play and saved-village reload. Existing resident-sharing/recovery controls, strict native lint and the release build pass; the full native regression run passes all 755 tests (265.43 seconds). The visible save form was inspected.

Standalone map packages, wider damaged-record recovery, complete modular resident artwork, longer-horizon feasibility validation and all three biome release gates remain required. The overall builder goal remains in progress.

### Standalone map package and storage foundations

Authored-land validation is now shared between scenario compilation and an independent native map-package validator. It covers regional biome/catalog revisions, dimensions, terrain cells, vegetation, finite deposits, river geometry, camp/camera bounds, clearings and named regions. Standalone `solar-friends-map` packages contain resolved land, explicit seasonal weather, independent package identity/content revision, title/description, author, compatible game/rules/art versions and bounded source credits. They require an explicit river or river-free setting and reject settlement, cast, quest, runtime and executable fields. A map package does not implicitly start a scenario. **Why:** reusable land must remain valid independently of another scenario's residents, resources and objectives, while sharing exactly the same physical rules.

Native-backed JavaScript package creation/export/import preserves the source and enforces the same 2 MiB bound. Version-6 library storage adds immutable maps and independent map-version counters. Map and scenario freezing now share one atomic expected-revision transaction; their content/version counters remain separate. Imports receive new local identities while retaining the original package and author, and deleting a source draft/map cannot delete an independent import or scenario release. Valid land can be frozen even when its source draft has unrelated invalid settlement or quest settings. Map previews have a separate storage key; no images or mutable library links enter shared files.

Three focused native tests cover all launch biomes, shared physical constraints, exact native map round trips, unchanged running villages, explicit water data, unsupported versions, unresolved fields, byte limits and hostile metadata/content. All 758 native regression tests pass (243.27 seconds), along with strict all-target lint and the release WASM build. `verify-map-packages.mjs` proves exact physical opening worlds after export/import in all three biomes. Browser repository checks cover immutable records, independent counters, concurrency, deletion without version reuse, failed-write atomicity, stale drafts, invalid-scenario independence and import/source-deletion isolation. Existing frozen-release and public creator journeys pass. Actual version-2 and version-5 databases upgrade while retaining exact draft/view/revision/run data and native village restoration, then freeze independent map and scenario packages.

This is the map validation/package/storage foundation. Regular-player Save map controls, a map collection with original export/import and independent reuse into new scenarios, authorship propagation through those public flows and full map-preview/recovery QA still need implementation. Complete modular resident artwork, wider damaged-record recovery, long-horizon feasibility validation and all three biome release gates also remain required; the overall builder plan remains in progress.

### Player-facing standalone map sharing

The regular creator now offers **Save map** with an independent map title/description and author. It resolves supported legacy land/weather omissions in an undoable draft transaction, validates only the map before freezing, and can save valid land despite unrelated invalid starting resources or scenario goals. Explicit weather remains authored data; an implicit legacy biome preserves its original default weather, including the beginner-weather schedule, before pinning a supported regional identity. **Why:** players should reuse a property without inheriting another community's cast, buildings, supplies or quests.

Reusable maps have their own creation-library collection, accent-insensitive title/author/version discovery, locally painted land-only previews, original `.solar-map` export, new-entry import and explicit removal confirmation. **Create scenario** opens a separate draft with an independent copy of the map/weather and the normal Sandbox authoring defaults, empty buildings/roads and camp-owned starting supplies. It retains map authorship in source credits; later frozen scenario releases embed their own land and credit the map source. Source deletion never follows a mutable link into those drafts/releases/villages. Incompatible retained maps stay exportable; disabled header controls and use-time native validation protect against both unsupported versions and stale compatibility metadata.

`verify-map-controls.mjs` exercises the complete public save/search/export/import/reuse/playtest flow in all three launch biomes, separate map titles, valid land from an invalid settlement, version numbering, immutable old exports after source river edits, new-scenario isolation, retained credits, independent deletion, rejected incompatible imports and the desktop toolbar at 1366×900. The map dialog, three-biome library previews and desktop workspace were inspected. `verify-map-sharing.mjs` proves exact physical opening-world preservation for implicit legacy climate, explicit-weather retention, unsupported-default refusal, independent copies and bounded source-credit handling. Recovery browser fixtures prove exact original export and unchanged records for newer retained maps, rejection behind stale headers and no accidental draft/run creation. Existing scenario-release, immutable-map storage and content-library recovery/conflict/playtest browser checks pass. This is a JavaScript/UI increment; the native baseline remains 758 passing tests with strict lint and a release build from the preceding foundation.

This completes the basic player-facing standalone map save/share/reuse loop. Broader damaged-record recovery, previews for untouched historical entries, complete modular resident artwork, longer-horizon feasibility validation, curated examples and the complete three-biome art/balance/performance release gates remain required. The overall builder goal remains in progress.

### Complete neutral-body pose prototype

Modular resident art production now has a neutral-body reference across the three required sheets: the existing base atlas plus newly generated idle and activity atlases, totaling 48 extracted poses. The built-in image generator used the neutral body for identity/materials and the current Bento sheets for pose/camera reference. Activity tools and seating remain intentionally absent from this body-only source. **Why:** compatible face, hair, clothing, accessory and equipment layers need a complete measured body pose set rather than a standing portrait or an isolated hair cap.

The first idle candidate failed the outer-margin gate: its bottom-left pose left only one pixel below the silhouette. A separate generated revision corrected the margin; the failed candidate is retained as a reference and is not selected by the review. No acceptance threshold was lowered. Prompts, exact input/output paths, source hashes, verdicts, remaining limits and inspected boards are recorded in `assets/art-direction/character-prototype-v1/body-set-generation.json`.

`web/artwork/character-body-lab.html`, linked from the artwork studio, compares each body pose with current Bento and uses the actual animation sampler, resident width, native opening land, projected home/roads, shared world rendering and unlit night filter. Its verifier exercises all supported clips and both facings, extracts all 48 poses, checks outer-edge and normalized-frame bounds, measures equal 220-pixel standing heights across sheets, binds measurements to reviewed body/reference hashes and confirms the playable catalog still contains only the original five approved full looks. The three pose/scene boards were visually inspected. Existing rejected-part checks still pass and continue rejecting both unregistered hair attempts.

These are body-production prototypes, not a finished appearance system. No candidate is enabled in gameplay or the player creator, and no existing resident sprite was replaced. Co-authored per-pose masks, semantic head/neck/hand attachment landmarks, face/hair/clothing/accessory variations, genuinely different body types, equipment and seating with front/back occlusion, final animation-loop review, compatible recipes, portraits, bounded composition caches and complete gameplay integration remain required. The wider recovery, feasibility, curated examples and three-biome release gates also remain open; the overall goal remains in progress.

### Registered body material composition prototype

The neutral body lab now supports separate skin, shirt, shorts and shoe palettes across all 48 poses, with source-hash-bound pose regions and a shared 256-pixel coordinate space. Connected garment highlights dye with their material while small isolated facial whites remain protected. Original artwork files are unchanged. **Why:** character customization needs independently controlled materials without moving body geometry or recolouring unrelated parts.

A trusted frame provider composes frames lazily through a bounded per-model LRU; the default recipe reuses original frames. The existing image-sheet renderer path remains supported. The lab exposes diagnostic regions and palette controls; regular gameplay still offers only the five existing approved full looks. Review boards, source bindings, limits and the corrected initial classifier finding are recorded in `assets/art-direction/character-prototype-v1/material-mask-review.json`.

`verify-character-materials.mjs` passes 48 masks and 192 isolated material changes, source/mask immutability, unchanged alpha, protected facial whites, connected collar highlights, default pixel equality against the legacy renderer for 13 clips in both facings, canonical recipe caching, bounded eviction/clear and unsupported source/palette refusal. Character-creator and body-set browser regressions pass. JavaScript syntax and diff checks pass. No native code changed; the preceding 758-test native baseline was not rerun for this increment. Cache byte figures describe logical composed RGBA buffers, not total browser or GPU memory.

This remains a one-body material prototype. Authored body diversity, attachment sockets, shape parts, hair/faces/clothing cuts/accessories, equipment/seating occlusion, native recipes, portraits, player integration and global maximum-roster performance remain required. Wider recovery, feasibility, curated examples and complete three-biome release gates remain open.

### Original draft and village recovery exports

The creation library now offers Backup beside every draft and independent saved village. It downloads a versioned `solar-friends-recovery` JSON envelope containing the retained record, including unknown fields, draft history and previous checkpoint or village snapshot. Export deliberately bypasses native content validation and does not open or rewrite the record. **Why:** damaged or newer-format content must remain recoverable even when Edit or Continue cannot use it. These are recovery records, not published scenario packages; restoring them is not yet supported by the ordinary import control.

`verify-record-recovery.mjs` drives the public Backup buttons against deliberately damaged/future draft and run records in real IndexedDB, compares downloaded JSON with the retained records and proves the database is unchanged. Browser errors, JavaScript syntax and diff checks pass. Missing bodies, damaged discovery headers, recovery import/repair and broader previous-checkpoint recovery still require work; this increment does not complete the recovery gate or the overall builder goal.

### Independent draft recovery import

The regular creation import control now understands version-1 project recovery envelopes. It tries the current definition, then the previous durable checkpoint, using native scenario validation; a valid checkpoint becomes a separate draft with fresh project/map identities and retained attribution. The original file and stored source are unchanged. **Why:** a usable earlier checkpoint should rescue authoring work without replacing the evidence needed to diagnose a damaged original. Backup envelopes have a bounded 32 MiB read limit; ordinary scenario/package imports retain their existing smaller limit, and large backup envelopes are not passed wholesale into native scenario validation.

The public browser recovery check imports a damaged-current/valid-previous backup, waits for the new draft to save, confirms fresh identity and unchanged original storage, and rejects unsupported envelopes. Original backup export checks continue to pass, along with JavaScript syntax and diff checks. This is definition recovery: imported undo history/view state, unplayable-but-repairable draft recovery, saved-village restoration, damaged headers and explicit checkpoint-choice UI remain unfinished. The full recovery and builder gates remain open.

### Independent saved-village recovery import

The ordinary creation import control now restores version-1 saved-village backup envelopes as independent villages. A fresh native game validates the current snapshot, then the previous snapshot if necessary; neither usable snapshot means refusal without a new record. Scratch playtests and unsupported envelope/record versions are refused. The new record has a fresh local identity and no mutable project link, retains source-release credits, and uses the frozen scenario definition inside the native-accepted snapshot for restart. A conflicting outer definition cannot change the restored community or its restart. **Why:** recovering progress must not overwrite the original or silently pair a village with another scenario.

The real-browser recovery verifier imports a damaged-current/valid-previous village through the public file control, proves exact snapshot retention, independent ownership and unchanged original storage, opens it through PlayerSession, and checks exact native restart using the embedded definition. Fully damaged village imports leave the library count unchanged and report a recovery error. Existing export/draft recovery checks, JavaScript syntax and diff checks pass. This increment changes no Rust code. Damaged discovery headers, missing record bodies, explicit checkpoint selection, imported draft history/view preservation and incomplete-but-repairable draft recovery remain open, as do the other builder release gates.

### Recovery retains unfinished current drafts

Draft backup recovery now shares ordinary draft import's structural/version refusal policy. Native gameplay validation errors can remain editable review issues; a structurally supported current draft is retained even when it is not playable. Recovery falls back to the previous checkpoint only when the current definition cannot safely enter the editor. A separate EditorDocument construction check protects the authoring boundary. **Why:** an intentionally unfinished cast or scenario should not silently lose newer edits in favour of an older playable checkpoint.

The browser recovery verifier imports a current empty-cast draft with an older playable checkpoint, confirms the current title and empty cast in durable storage, reloads it and verifies Play remains disabled. All existing recovery/export/village checks continue to pass. Focused policy checks reject unsupported structures, appearance/credit versions and malformed validator reports while accepting ordinary incomplete-draft issues. Syntax and diff checks pass. Imported history/view preservation, explicit checkpoint selection, damaged discovery metadata and wider recovery gates remain unfinished; the overall builder goal stays in progress.

### Player-initiated library index repair

The library's **Missing a saved creation?** section now offers **Repair library list**. A single IndexedDB transaction scans drafts, villages, resident presets, frozen releases and maps with cursors and reconstructs missing/stale discovery headers from each retained record. It never writes the content stores, previews, release counters or map counters, and does not delete orphan headers. Entries without a usable string identity are skipped and reported; missing titles receive a discovery-only label. **Why:** a missing index entry must not hide otherwise retained authoring work from its backup/export controls. Repair is explicit rather than adding full-snapshot scans to every ordinary library opening.

The public browser recovery verifier removes a draft header and corrupts a village header, reloads to prove the draft is initially absent, uses the visible repair control, observes both entries restored, and repeats repair to prove zero additional changes. Original record equality is checked independently and all existing backup/import/recovery journeys still pass. Syntax and diff checks pass. ### Explicit checkpoint recovery choice

The public browser verifier covers unavailable/current states, default previous selection, Escape cancellation with unchanged library entries, deliberate older-checkpoint selection when both are supported, incomplete-current recovery, native village recovery/restart, index repair and original exports. The overall builder goal stays in progress.

### Recovered draft history and editor view

Draft backup import now preserves a supported selected checkpoint's undo/redo journal and editor view. Undo cannot return the new project to its source's identity. Unsupported/corrupt history is cleared while retaining the current definition and reporting that recovery to the player. The existing attach boundary validates/clamps recovered view settings. **Why:** rescuing a draft should retain the player's editing context and reversible decisions without reconnecting it to the original project.

The native-backed document verifier covers a fork with both past and future edits, complete undo/redo, unchanged source checkpoint, stable new identities, valid compiled content and damaged-journal recovery. The public browser verifier imports history and a camera/zoom/grid view, exercises Undo/Redo controls, saves, reloads and verifies future edits remain available under the new identity. Existing index, backup, checkpoint-choice, incomplete draft and village recovery checks still pass. Syntax and diff checks pass. This completes the basic supported history/view import path, not all recovery or release QA: missing bodies, all-collection damaged metadata, historical previews, full modular residents, longer-horizon feasibility and three-biome release gates remain open.

### Historical library preview backfill

Opening the library now fills missing landscape thumbnails for retained drafts, frozen releases, reusable maps and saved villages. It reads one record at a time, uses fresh native instances and the existing painted thumbnail renderer, yields between rows and stops when its rows disappear or the library closes. Saved-village previews show the opening landscape from the snapshot's accepted frozen scenario; they do not simulate or change the village. Unsupported or damaged content keeps its placeholder and remains available to existing actions. **Why:** older creations need the same visual discovery as newly saved work without resaving or advancing their content.

Preview writes use a separate derivative-cache transaction and require the source record to exist at the expected storage revision. They change no content records, timestamps, history, package versions or counters. `verify-library-previews.mjs` seeds twelve thumbnail-free records across all four types and three biomes, observes the public library fill all twelve, compares exact source records before/after, reloads to prove cache persistence and rejects stale/missing/invalid-image writes. Existing comprehensive recovery checks also pass. The desktop list was visually inspected; JavaScript syntax and diff checks pass. Broader damaged-record/all-collection recovery coverage, complete modular resident artwork, longer-horizon feasibility and complete three-biome example/art/balance/performance gates remain required; the overall builder goal stays in progress.

### Source-bound attachment authoring foundation

The neutral body lab now has an attachment-guide editor for all 48 poses, with head-centre, neck-base and near/far grip points in the body's shared prepared-frame space. It supports pointer placement, exact coordinates, mirrored preview and keyboard nudging, explicit hidden hands, clear/undo/redo, bounded import/export and a fixed body ground line. Raw source hashes plus SHA-256 digests of every prepared RGBA frame bind the coordinates to the actual normalization output; a crop/scale/render change cannot silently reuse old points. **Why:** independently fitted hair/clothing/tools drift between poses, so modular artwork needs co-authored attachment coordinates before compatible layers can be reviewed.

### Working poses and source-bound foreground ownership

### Missing saved-data recovery across collections

Library repair now checks record-key existence for project, village, resident, release and map headers. If a body is missing, it preserves the header and all remaining unknown metadata while marking the entry unavailable; it never fabricates replacement content or deletes the orphaned entry. Ordinary library/resident rows explain the limitation, disable unavailable content actions and offer **Save entry details**, a separately identified metadata export that explicitly cannot restore missing content. Restoring a body and repairing again clears the missing flag from the derived header without changing the restored record. Missing draft/map/release actions report the problem before repair instead of silently doing nothing; missing village continuation stays in the library.

The public browser journey verifies missing-data rows, metadata downloads and safe disabled actions for the main collections, resident recovery-row availability, exact retained source/unknown-metadata preservation across all five stores, idempotent repair, persistence after reload, and status clearing when a body returns. Existing checkpoint recovery/import/backup and content-library ownership/stale-tab checks pass. Visual review found the sticky close control overlapping row actions while scrolled; a dedicated horizontal margin now keeps it separate, with an actual element-overlap assertion and inspected desktop capture. Syntax/diff checks pass.

This handles missing bodies and remaining entry metadata, not reconstruction of content that no longer exists. Broader malformed-package/raw-backup recovery remains to audit, alongside the original feasibility, character artwork/integration, biome/balance/usability and global performance release gates. The full builder goal remains in progress.

Raw backups are preservation files and may require a compatible version; recovery/import support for all package backup kinds remains a separate required step.

### Validated package-backup restoration

The creation library now recovers supported raw map, scenario-release and resident backups into independent local entries. A shared recovery reader accepts only understood envelope/record versions and validates the extracted package through the existing native map, scenario or resident validator before any write. It preserves package identity, revision, authorship, credits and content while allocating a new local library identity. Unknown outer metadata remains in the original stored record/backup rather than being interpreted or copied as executable behaviour. Unsupported envelopes, artwork revisions, missing content and incompatible outer records fail without partial writes.

Resident presets also accept their own raw backups through the ordinary resident-file input and direct other backup kinds to the creation library. A fixture with over 80 KiB of outer metadata verifies that a valid small resident can recover without weakening its inner limit. Import progress replaces stale prior status, and recovered residents include directions to Founding cast → Resident presets.

### Two-checkpoint backup size boundary

Fixed a verified transport mismatch: each stored village snapshot may reach 16,777,216 JavaScript string code units, and a raw backup contains current and previous snapshots plus its frozen definition. JSON escaping can push the resulting file beyond the former 32 MiB upload cap. Backup upload readers now share a separate 96 MiB envelope limit; snapshot storage/restoration still uses the unchanged 16 Mi-code-unit limit, and ordinary creations/resident packages retain their 2 MiB/64 KiB limits. This supersedes the earlier progress note's 32 MiB backup envelope ceiling.

`verify-backup-size-boundary.mjs` creates two maximum-length native-valid snapshots, exports an actual 66,695,941-byte backup, compares the exported record digest against the untouched stored original, opens both recovery choices through the ordinary import control, and recovers a separate native village with the expected canonical checkpoint. Reload preserves both original and recovered village. A sparse file one byte above the upload cap is refused by both import readers; oversized ordinary creation/resident files and over-limit snapshot writes remain refused. Temporary large files are cleaned up. Package-backup recovery and content-library ownership/continuation regressions pass; syntax/diff checks pass.

This verifies backup transport boundaries using valid checkpoints padded with JSON whitespace, not maximum-complexity gameplay or runtime memory/frame-rate budgets. Feasibility feedback, complete modular character art/native/player integration, remaining recovery auditing, all-biome balance/usability and global performance gates remain required. The full goal remains in progress.

### Bound pending appearance composition

A reusable appearance cache now limits active composition as well as completed results. The draft combined appearance provider shares concurrent requests for the same validated clip/recipe, composes at most two groups at once and queues at most 48 groups. Its existing 12-group completed LRU remains in place. Extra requests receive a recoverable busy error rather than allocating unbounded concurrent frame work. Queue entries contain recipes/providers, not pre-rendered frame groups. Cache inspection reports active, queued and pending work separately from completed-frame RGBA estimates.

Clearing starts a new cache generation: already accepted callers still settle, but old active or queued work cannot repopulate the cleared cache or supply a new-generation request. Failed composition releases its slot and pending key before notifying callers, allowing immediate retries. These limits apply to this cache instance; they do not establish a whole-game memory/performance budget.

**Why:** limiting retained frames alone still allows duplicate or rapidly changing preview requests to create unbounded transient work. This foundation is needed before exposing complete modular appearance editing to players.

Deterministic scheduler checks cover duplicate requests, the active/queued limits, overload, FIFO scheduling, LRU reuse, cache clearing with active and queued jobs, and immediate failure retry. The actual browser composition check also requests one recipe sixteen times concurrently and verifies one shared four-pose result, alongside the existing 48-pose colour/layer checks, all animation controls, matching portraits and rapid-control latest-result handling. Draft day/night/world/portrait output was inspected. Production character artwork, equipment, native recipes, player integration and global maximum-roster performance remain unfinished; draft art remains excluded from player choices.

### Conditional life-after-arrival outlook

The ordinary creator now offers **Life after arrival**, with one-, three- and seven-day windows, a cancellable worker, and results kept separate from the draft and saved playthroughs. It reuses the game's existing isolated native forecast, including actual autonomous work, current plans, paid deliveries, outside occupations, weather and resource consumption. Native hourly observations add owned opening-summary fields, reachable food/water, assigned housing, usable ecological toilets, and counts of residents with high hunger or low energy. The report includes its actual start/end times, lowest observed service quantities, final quantities, food/water route-gap observation counts, projected funds and unfinished construction count.

Changing the draft or window discards stale results; editing during computation terminates its worker. Cancellation, failure and the existing 60-second worker limit leave the draft untouched and permit retry. Disabled finite-supply rules omit the relevant service rows. Reports explicitly distinguish hourly observations from continuous guarantees and long-term sustainability; a zero-low or a completed outlook is not release approval. The window does not invent player purchases, new projects or future interventions.

**Why:** initial inventories and completed buildings alone cannot show whether the authored arrival remains viable after food is eaten, work competes with needs and deliveries arrive. A real conditional replay gives authors useful evidence before a manual playtest.

The browser check exercises the actual worker and public controls, compares all 25 one-day observations against a separate native replay, checks source snapshot preservation, invalidation after edits, cancellation and retry. Deterministic controller checks also deliver obsolete callbacks after cancellation/editing, refuse invalid drafts and inspect escaped worker errors. Opening housing/sanitation regression checks pass. This is incremental feasibility feedback: continuous shortage detection, seasonal sustainability, material/utility-specific explanations, remaining character production/integration and original biome/balance/usability/global-performance release gates remain open.

All three public windows completed successfully with 25, 73 and 169 observations respectively. All 762 native tests pass (282.00 seconds), including the extended first/final observation comparison with real play. Strict all-target linting, release compilation, changed-file formatting, JavaScript syntax and diff checks pass. The full goal remains in progress.

### First source-bound activity equipment layer

The body lab now includes a separate watering-can equipment review for **activities pose 4 only**. The built-in image generator produced two candidates using the unchanged prepared resident as the placement/style reference. The first was rejected for excessive scale, low placement and texture. The second remains a static draft, registered in the original 1254-square coordinate space without silhouette fitting, local warping or body repainting. Exact prompts, input roles, candidate paths and visual findings are retained in `assets/art-direction/character-prototype-v1/water-equipment-generation-review.json`.

A reusable equipment composer validates the exact equipment file identity/dimensions and body pose binding. It partitions the authored hand foreground from the existing coloured/hair appearance, paints the equipment, then restores the foreground using the original hand pixels. Conflicting appearance pixels in the hand region are refused. Unsupported poses fail explicitly. Equipment stays attached to the activity rather than being placed in a character appearance recipe, and no draft equipment is added to player choices.

**Why:** modular residents need their activity tools to read naturally without regenerating bodies, overwriting colour choices or painting tools above fingers. A registered first pose establishes this composition path before producing the rest of each activity.

The actual renderer review shows the unchanged Bento reference and the layered draft beside the home, shed, storage and terrain, with both facings and day/night lighting. The revised can reads at world scale and is held near the hands; its metal highlights still need a full-clip style review. The initial hold pose is not evidence of a pouring animation. Mechanical checks confirm unchanged appearance pixels outside the equipment, hand-over-tool occlusion, transparent frame margins, exact source/pose refusals and incompatible-body/appearance refusal. The existing 48-pose combined appearance/portrait browser checks pass with the new lab installed.

Other watering poses, pouring/temporal continuity, cooking/tending/feeding equipment, additional body/face/hair/clothing/accessory artwork, final style review, native recipes and regular-player integration remain required. This static draft does not complete the character creator or the full builder goal.

### Whole painted pose presentation for modular drafts

The animation compositor now supports an explicit, opt-in **keyframes** presentation policy for independently painted poses. It chooses the strongest complete pose at full weight, aggregating duplicate pose weights and resolving ties deterministically. The default path for existing sheets/providers retains crossfades. Combined modular appearance drafts opt into keyframes, and the equipment review offers **Whole painted poses** and **Crossfade comparison** on the same source artwork. No body, hair, hand or equipment pixels are repainted, stretched or regenerated for this change.

**Why:** opacity blending cannot interpolate the position of independently painted silhouettes. It produced duplicated/translucent heads, arms and tools even when the layer registration was correct. Whole-pose presentation removes this renderer artifact while keeping authored art, timing data and legacy rendering available for comparison. It does not manufacture missing in-between artwork or establish that the current cadence is final.

The equipment review adds bounded, three-second **Play cycle / Pause cycle** playback, stops on manual pose/time/presentation changes, and cancels on page hide. Browser coverage verifies the public controls, paused clock preservation, interruption by pose choice and complete cycles. Real canvas footage is retained for both modes, with 75 successive sampled video frames per mode. Those frames and the 19-sample transition board were inspected: the keyframe recording has intact silhouettes, while the comparison still shows the previous crossfade ghosts.

Pure checks cover all thirteen animations, activity transitions, duplicate weighting, stable ties and unchanged legacy layers. An independent browser pixel check verifies 104 actual compositor samples equal one exact source pose; the existing 48-pose colour/layer/portrait and appearance-cache checks pass. Source hashes and footage identities are recorded in `assets/art-direction/character-prototype-v1/appearance-temporal-v2-review.json`.

The watering movement still has visible discrete pose changes. Pose spacing/cadence, the lowest grip, final tool highlights and water effects remain production review items. Additional modular artwork, remaining activity equipment, native appearance recipes, regular-player integration and the full builder release gates remain unfinished. This resolves the ghosting mechanism in the opted-in draft path, not the full character animation gate.

### Feeding equipment, shared painted parts and precise hand ownership

The body lab now reviews all four feeding poses through the actual three-second animation. Eight built-in image-generator candidates preserve the unchanged body framing. The first set had chunky basket weave and oversized glossy grain; a second set uses an enlarged original Bento feeding pose as a style reference. Exact prompts, references, retained outputs and findings are recorded in `assets/art-direction/character-prototype-v1/feed-equipment-generation-review.json`.

**Why:** feeding needed its own basket, scoop and release stages. Independently repainting the basket in every frame introduced changes in size, weave and lighting even when each pose was registered correctly. Reusing the same painted part is a stronger consistency guarantee than accepting those variations.

A versioned equipment-part recipe now reuses one painted basket region across all four poses, with explicit integer placement offsets and separately registered grain regions. Part sources retain exact SHA256 identities. Per-part scaling, rotation, arbitrary fields, external filenames and clipped regions are refused. The review shares one request and decoded basket image across the four poses. A source-space assembly followed by one whole-frame normalization fixes resampling differences found when cropped regions were scaled independently. Source PNGs are copied unchanged; this is renderer composition, with no sprite warping, repainting or automatic fitting.

Hand polygons also restored small shirt/shorts patches over the basket. A second foreground-mask format stores exact prepared-body pixel ownership as ordered, disjoint runs. An authoring script starts with source-bound polygons and excludes registered garment material pixels, then saves fixed masks. Runtime performs no colour segmentation. Original body RGBA values remain unchanged, including antialiased edges, and no pixel belongs to both foreground and background. Garment foreground is zero in all four final feeding compositions.

All four poses pass an independent full-frame equipment/hand composition, supporting-hand contact, unchanged-body, margin and incompatible-input checks. Additional coverage refuses sixteen malformed part recipes, five mismatched-source cases and eighteen malformed pixel masks; invalid recipes fail before fetching artwork. All 48 combined appearance poses, portraits, controls and bounded cache regressions pass, as do existing watering/cooking/tending static checks and shared partial/full/wrong-row controls. Cooking and tending retain their two-second cycles in the duration-aware runner. Regression captures use separate temporary output directories, preserving historical evidence.

The feeding cycle checks nineteen boundary samples, four distinct poses, exact return to the opening frame, play/pause/interruption and both facings in day/night scenes. Recordings now start synchronously with the public playback handler, force a final canvas capture and decode seventy-five successive frames plus the endpoint per presentation. This fixes a recording-start delay that could omit the visual loop return. The final transition board, recordings and rendered scenes were inspected: the shared basket stays consistent; whole-pose presentation remains discrete, and the legacy crossfade comparison still ghosts.

All sixteen daily-activity equipment poses now have review drafts. This does not complete modular residents: loose-seed scale/count and palm semantics, final style/temporal review, other body/face/hair/clothing/accessory choices, portraits, native appearance recipes and regular-player integration remain unfinished. These drafts remain outside player choices. The original recovery, feasibility, three-biome, usability and global performance release gates remain required; the full builder objective stays in progress.

### Progress: per-step welfare and owned-stock outlook

The conditional replay now records hunger and low-energy exposure at every native simulation boundary (at most 0.75 game minutes), including each resident’s accumulated duration, longest spell, first onset and episode count. Stable resident IDs preserve attribution independently of query order. Bounded summaries retain no frame history. Finite food and fresh-water supplies report lowest owned stock and empty-stock duration; carried supplies remain owned, while automatic-supply rules omit these rows. The player-facing outlook shows the summaries, explains their resolution and offers direct navigation to cast, food and water editing.

**Why:** hourly observations can miss a need or stock shortage that starts and recovers between readings. These summaries expose those spells without running expensive route searches at every step or confusing owned inventory with accessible supplies.

Focused native coverage includes a real hunger spell absent from both adjacent hourly observations, recovery, horizon boundaries, changing resident iteration order and automatic-supply omission. Browser verification uses the rebuilt release WASM, compares all 1,921 per-step resident readings and owned-stock minima/empty durations against a separate replay, preserves the source checkpoint, exercises the 1/3/7-day worker and cancellation/edit invalidation, and checks repair navigation. Desktop report layout was visually inspected. All 766 native tests, strict all-target linting, release-WASM compilation, scoped formatting, JavaScript checks and diff checks pass. Route-dependent services remain hourly observations; continuous route/service detection, seasonal sustainability, detailed material/utility guidance, modular resident production/integration and the original recovery, biome, usability and performance gates remain open. The overall goal remains in progress.

### Progress: actionable construction outlook

The player-facing conditional outlook now lists each projected construction outcome instead of only counting unfinished projects. It shows the building type and grid location, the native completion window or end-of-window blocker, and a **Show building on map** action that selects and centres the exact authored structure in the inspector. Worksite metadata comes from the same frozen checkpoint as the replay; repeated building kinds and reordered authored lists cannot retarget the action. Draft edits invalidate these actions with the rest of the report. Native construction and material rules remain authoritative.

**Why:** a count of unfinished projects cannot tell an author which starting plan is held up, which supplies it needs or where to repair it. Existing native replay explanations now lead directly to the corresponding editable worksite.

Deterministic controller checks cover escaped names/blockers, completion windows, unknown and stale action refusal, frozen metadata and stable checkpoint bindings. The browser journey authors a Home blueprint, removes starting inventories through the public supplies dialog, runs the actual replay, displays its earth/stone shortage and navigates back to the correct blueprint without changing edit history. The full outlook browser regression also passes, including separate per-step replay comparisons and cancellation/edit invalidation. This is a presentation of existing native construction outcomes; broader continuous service feasibility, seasonal sustainability, complete modular resident production/integration, recovery auditing and the original biome/usability/performance release gates remain open. The full goal remains in progress.

### Progress: electrical outlook for authored villages

Hourly native outlook observations now include the real electrical service probe and owned energy accounts. The editor shows shared battery charge/capacity at arrival, lowest observation and end; peak observed shared generation and lighting/pump demand; native counts of buildings with reduced or missing light; and energy generated, consumed and discarded during the replay. Private roof batteries remain excluded from shared capacity, while actual network account totals include them. Automatic-energy rules receive an explicit explanation rather than a false finite-power warning. Repair actions open utility-route and equipment-condition editing.

**Why:** a village can own charged roof batteries while shared lights and pumps remain disconnected or unpowered. Authors need electrical consequences of their layout and equipment choices alongside food, water and construction, with the limits of each estimate visible.

The lighting probe estimates the next minute with pumps first, excludes attended tool demand, and is sampled hourly. The actual replay still runs tools and their competing demand. This is neither continuous outage detection nor a per-circuit sustainability guarantee. Native coverage checks a disconnected night light, daylight, a disabled light and automatic rules; all nine forecast tests, strict all-target lint, release-WASM compilation and scoped formatting pass. Controller checks cover minima, native reduced-light counts, account deltas and automatic-energy presentation. Browser checks compare hourly electrical data against separate native replay observations, including nonzero private-roof generation with zero shared capacity, and exercise both repair actions; construction outlook regression passes. The final desktop layout was visually inspected. Continuous service feasibility, seasonal validation, complete modular residents and the remaining original release gates stay open. The full objective remains in progress.

### Progress: solo residents can finish work while lonely

Full-cycle biome testing exposed a roster-dependent autonomy defect: once a lone resident's social need fell, work was reconsidered every tick even though no conversation was available. Re-selecting the same harvest reset its timer, and the shared work queue also treated that resident as unwilling. The chooser, work interruptions, meal-finishing check and queue now consistently require another resident before social need can trigger a conversation break. A lone resident retains the actual low social value and its consequences; no social contact is fabricated. Multi-person social thresholds remain unchanged.

**Why:** one-person authored scenarios are explicitly supported. An unavailable social action must not prevent their resident from harvesting, hauling or completing useful work.

The native regression authors a one-person scenario with zero social need, an empty pantry and a mature garden, then completes an actual harvest through normal ticks. A standalone browser regression imports the same kind of authored setup through the public library and observes a real harvest at normal 8× play, without task/need patches; social remains zero. All 768 native tests, strict all-target linting, release-WASM compilation, scoped formatting and JavaScript/diff checks pass. The first post-fix 42-day diagnostic produced 17 harvests versus one before the defect trapped work; it also revealed that the seasonal harness must use cumulative village harvests, not the resettable per-plant counter. The corrected six-case biome matrix remains under validation. Complete biome balance/art review, modular residents, remaining recovery, usability and performance gates remain open; the full objective stays in progress.

### Progress: six complete regional seasonal-cycle checks

`node scripts/verify-biome-cycles.mjs` now authors and runs two properties per launch biome, using seeds 42 and 73. Every case starts from a generated regional map plus an explicit soil edit and a one-person settlement with a home, planted garden, cistern and solar farm. River cases include a native-eligible hydro generator; the other seed removes the river. Each Mata Atlântica/Cerrado case runs 42 days and each Caatinga case 56 days, covering a full wet/dry cycle at its actual regional season length with introductory weather disabled.

**Why:** short opening tests cannot establish that frozen biome profiles, forecasts, soil/crops, water and energy remain coherent across season changes. The same long run also exposed the solo-resident social/work interruption defect fixed in `05aada7`.

All six cases pass: **280 simulated days, 537,600 ordinary native ticks, 286 daily conserved checkpoints and 40 exact one-hour continuations from restored checkpoints**. Checks compare saved seven-day forecasts with later actual days, actual rain windows, real cistern collection, higher dry-period evaporation, solar output, lower dry-season hydro output, no hydro sites/output without a river, material/liquid/electrical conservation and continued harvesting after the solo resident's social need reaches zero. All cases retain their frozen regional profile. Across the six controlled runs, cumulative harvests range from 16 to 24, sampled peak hunger stays below 41 and sampled energy remains above 78; transient empty pantry readings are retained rather than hidden.

Durable results, exact script/WASM hashes, source revision and reproduction commands are recorded in `.github/builder-review/biome-cycles.json`; detailed definitions and boundary saves are generated under `artifacts/biome-cycles/`. The report matches the current harness and built WASM. Syntax and diff checks pass. These are controlled one-resident environmental/accounting runs, with maintenance disabled and no market variation. They do not establish maximum-roster performance, equipment-lifecycle resilience, example difficulty, all-biome artwork approval or unfamiliar-player usability. Those gates, complete modular residents and remaining recovery/service-feasibility work remain required. The complete objective stays in progress.

### Progress: dense-editor compiler latency and measured baseline

Added `scripts/benchmark-builder.mjs` to measure public editor fund changes, undo/redo, zoom and minimap pan at one, five and twelve residents, plus the 256-building bound. The dense fixture deliberately contains two starter buildings and 254 streetlights around a clear arrival area; native preflight validation refuses invalid fixtures before measurement. Measurements capture synchronous handler cost, two-frame presentation latency and native/map-render phases. Original preset appearances are used. The final comparison runs without competing test processes; five samples are recorded per control.

**Why:** a valid 12-person, 256-building draft stalled for almost a second on every fund edit because compilation repeated whole-grid route searches and worksite geometry for each resident/building pair. Slow native review, rather than map painting, dominated the delay.

Navigation now indexes nearby obstacles for adjacent edges and fractional grid links while retaining exact open-footprint intersection and river checks. Opening review lazily reuses one shortest-path search per starting resident and computes each building's valid approach geometry once, preserving individual slots and occupancy claims. The shared contexts borrow the unchanged village, remain bounded by the cast and exist only during the review; they do not enter saves. Ordinary gameplay still evaluates approach candidates lazily. The editor also avoids a redundant map repaint when no utility preview exists.

On the recorded desktop setup, median dense fund-edit latency fell from **948.7 ms to 118.3 ms**, with native compilation falling from **888.8 ms to 68.5 ms**. Final camp fund edits measured about 49–53 ms across the three population sizes; short zoom/pan samples measured about 4–7 ms. `.github/builder-review/editor-performance.json` preserves the baseline revision, machine/browser, fixture scope, source/WASM hashes and per-control results. These are small-sample measurements, not universal timing promises. The dense handler still exceeds the proposed 100 ms target.

All **772 native tests** pass, including exhaustive adjacent/fractional-link comparisons with the original exact segment checks, batched versus individual route quotes, and twelve-person approach/claim comparisons with the original algorithm. Strict all-target linting, release-WASM compilation, scoped formatting, JavaScript and diff checks pass. Browser route authoring/preview and opening service/housing/sanitation regressions pass. The dense editor layout was inspected. Mixed-building maxima, pointer strokes, save throughput, sustained frame pacing, 1×/16× simulation and shared/unique modular-character memory/preview budgets remain required. Complete modular residents, remaining service/recovery work and the original art/usability release gates also stay open; the full objective remains in progress.

This incomplete hairstyle cannot enter the complete prototype recipe format, native looks, resident packages or player choices. The shared preview UI now accepts an explicit recipe factory and clip list instead of assuming every review is the curls prototype.

All four candidates retain 100% of alpha≥32 painted pixels inside their registered head bounds and compose over the exact source-bound body pose. `node scripts/verify-sidepart-appearance.mjs assets/art-direction/character-prototype-v1/sidepart-breathe-review-v1` checks four poses in three palettes, 44 unsupported-pose refusals, unsupported-clip/recipe refusal, complete-recipe isolation, pending-work reuse, six-entry/24-frame cache limits, exact matching portraits, pause, palette interruption and actual five-second playback. Final art approval and the remaining 44 poses, hand/head occlusion, other hair/body/face/clothing/facial-hair/hat/accessory choices, native/player integration and all original builder recovery, service-feasibility, biome, usability and global performance gates remain required. This is four partial hairstyle drafts, not a completed character creator. The full objective stays in progress.

### Shared player appearance path refactor

**Why:** the map editor, creator, saved preset library, relationship editor, live identity dialog and normal gameplay previously selected resident sheets separately. Extending those independent paths with modular parts would risk portraits and world poses disagreeing, duplicate decoding and accidental substitution. `web/resident-appearance-library.js` now supplies one stable provider per supported appearance within an art session, sharing the existing prepared frames for all poses and the portrait. The public catalog owns keys, revisions and avatar accents; strict validation refuses unknown revisions or parts. Sheet decoding is lazy and shared, failed loads can retry, caller array mutation cannot retarget a look, and the fixed five-look catalog bounds loaded artwork to fifteen sheets and 240 prepared frames. This does not establish the future modular-character memory budget.

The creator and map/relationship/preset previews use the editor art session's library. Normal gameplay, live cosmetic preview and resident/speaker portraits use the game's library. The portrait crop is shared and preserves proportions, including relationship thumbnails and live avatars that previously stretched the crop. Live portrait canvases resolve the selected person's current appearance through stable resident IDs. The relationship editor no longer derives artwork from preset-list positions or ignores unsupported revisions; unavailable artwork gets an explicit marker while the creation remains intact.

`scripts/verify-resident-appearance-library.mjs` passes 240 exact original-frame selections, 520 animation comparisons across both facings/day-night, ten portrait comparisons, native/renderer catalog parity, one decode per sheet, strict appearance/pose refusal, failed-load retry, input isolation and relationship portraits with reordered presets and duplicate names. The public creator, live appearance edit/checkpoint, saved portrait library, scenario speaker/role and category lock/randomization journeys pass. Live portrait pixels are compared with the shared crop before and after editing. Desktop creator, live identity and preset captures were inspected; JavaScript/diff checks pass. `.github/builder-review/resident-appearance-library.json` records the commands, scope, source/WASM hashes and capture locations.

This refactor still exposes the original five public looks. Complete modular artwork, production part recipes, creator controls and final art/cadence approval remain unfinished, as do the original builder recovery, service-feasibility, biome/example balance, unfamiliar-player usability and maximum-scale performance gates. The full objective stays in progress.

### Initial playable custom looks

**Why:** reviewed modular artwork must reach the regular creator and the actual village, not remain a portrait or development preview. A strict schema-2 `resident-parts-v1` recipe now describes bundled body, hairstyle and material keys. The native appearance type accepts either an original preset look or this explicit recipe; it refuses unknown revisions, parts, palettes, mixed preset/part fields and extra properties. Cosmetic edits share one native operation, preserving needs, skills, work, relationships, cargo and identity. Authored characters, portable resident packages, frozen scenario definitions and live saves carry the same recipe.

The creator's Appearance tab and the live Name and appearance dialog now offer **Custom look**. The initial set combines the registered neutral body/outfit, short curls or swept side part, three skin tones, four top colours, three bottom colours and two shoe colours: 144 supported combinations. Category randomization honours custom looks and locked identity/personality/experience. Undo/reset restore complete recipes. The interface states the current fixed body, clothing style and hair colour; additional variants remain a required deliverable.

The runtime compositor loads bundled source-bound artwork directly, without installing development-tool UI. It verifies hashes, body/frame bindings, head containment, underlays and tool-owned hands; it preserves whole-frame registration and feeding recovery. One provider covers all 48 poses, cross-activity transitions and the matching portrait. Shared pending composition has two active slots and 24 queued slots; an LRU holds at most sixteen complete appearances (800 frames including feeding returns, at most 200 MiB of prepared RGBA). Up to twelve active resident recipes are protected from preview eviction. Raw decoded artwork and body-material caches are separate from that bound: the two-hairstyle test loads 85 source images. Full browser/GPU memory and twelve-person performance remain unverified release gates.

Player and editor loading paths prepare custom artwork explicitly, show loading/error states instead of substituting a founder, and guard obsolete callbacks. Village time does not advance while a scene waits for its artwork. Normal mode Continue, scenario/checkpoint reload, live editing, map placement, relationship portraits, preset portraits and thumbnails use the shared appearance library. Detached thumbnails cannot change the active cast's cache protection. The live preview now has explicit canvas dimensions; visual review caught and corrected a CSS stretch introduced by its two-column layout. Both dialogs keep palette controls and actions visible at a 1440×900 desktop size.

All **775 native tests**, strict all-target linting, scoped formatting and release-WASM compilation pass. Native checks cover all 144 recipe combinations, atomic unsupported-part rejection, cosmetic-only edits and exact continuation of a reordered custom cast. The runtime browser comparison passes 288 complete pose/palette/hairstyle comparisons against the reviewed artwork, twelve feeding return states, native catalog/palette parity, isolated custom randomization, source reuse and cache bounds. The asynchronous-cache checks also cover active-cast protection, synchronous reads and retention-failure cleanup.

The public custom-look journey authors and previews every activity, changes facing/lighting/scale, uses Undo/Redo, saves/exports/imports a resident preset, adds the imported copy as a separate sixth person, reopens the draft, launches a playtest, cancels then applies a live edit to the original authored person and reloads the exact checkpoint. The imported person's state stays independent. A separate normal Sandbox journey verifies cosmetic-only editing, its durable mode save, Story-slot isolation and Continue after a fresh page without custom artwork decoded. Original-look creator, shared-library/portrait, category controls, palette masks, creation thumbnails and resident-animation regressions pass. Captures and the portable fixture are retained under `assets/art-direction/character-prototype-v1/playable-parts-v1/`; `.github/builder-review/playable-resident-parts.json` records scope, commands and source/WASM hashes.

Ordinary Settings import is also exercised from a fresh five-person village. A synthetic artwork outage leaves the live world unchanged and exposes retry/cancel; retry imports the exact six-person custom checkpoint. Importing the original five-person checkpoint again removes stale People, portrait-strip and outside-job rows. This check exposed and fixed startup-only roster widgets and missing job controls. Asset preparation pauses scene updates before replacement; developer scene switches also refuse competing loads. The existing authored-speaker/live-rename and Story/Sandbox unlock/save regressions pass after these changes. These targeted import checks do not close every original recovery gate.

Visual inspection covers the compact desktop creator, corrected live preview, matching portraits, the custom original and imported neighbour beside the existing cast, terrain, shed and storage building. The selected source pixels also retain the prior all-pose, both-facing day/night reviews. This accepts the existing sources for an initial playable customization increment; it does not approve the final appearance range, every biome's artwork or temporal polish. Additional body/face/hair-colour/clothing/facial-hair/hat/accessory sets, final grip/cadence, remaining recovery/service-feasibility work, biome/example balance, unfamiliar-player usability and maximum-scale performance remain required. The complete objective stays in progress.


### Hair-colour customization increment

**Why:** players need hair colour independent of hairstyle and skin/clothing choices. Both the creator and live appearance editor now offer chestnut, espresso, black and silver for both supported hairstyles (576 complete recipes). Randomization includes this choice. The optional saved `hairColour` field defaults to chestnut and omits that default on serialization, preserving existing recipes; unknown values are rejected by native and browser validators.

Tinting uses only the registered hair layer at its final sprite resolution, retaining painted luminance, original alpha and foreground hands. The first raw-resolution tint experiment exposed a resampling edge mismatch; the final implementation passes exact silhouette comparisons across 384 colour/style poses and 16 feeding returns. All 288 existing default-colour pose comparisons and twelve returns remain pixel-identical to reviewed artwork. Native catalog parity and randomization cover the new choices. Normal Sandbox editing, durable saving and cold-art Continue preserve a silver hairstyle without changing noncosmetic state. The comparison board and numeric report are retained under `assets/art-direction/character-prototype-v1/hair-colours-v1/`; inspection confirms painted shading and unchanged body/tool proportions at two scales. This does not close the remaining body, face, clothing, accessory, animation or full-biome art requirements.

Final verification for this increment: all 775 native tests, strict all-target Clippy, scoped formatting and release-WASM compilation pass. The public creator journey also passes with black hair through preset export/import, independent duplication, draft reload, cancelled/applied live edits and exact checkpoint continuation.


### Maximum custom-cast performance probe and bounded interactive simulation

**Why:** the supported twelve-person roster must remain interactive with distinct custom looks. A new browser benchmark runs a normal 64×64 Sandbox arrival with twelve shared appearances and then twelve unique part recipes at a deliberately selected 16× test speed. Source-bound appearance loading completes (about 3.2 seconds for the first shared look and 11 seconds for twelve unique looks in this run); all twelve unique providers remain cached. Their prepared-frame RGBA bound is 150 MiB, separate from 85 decoded sources, raw/temporary canvases and GPU resources. Coarse browser JS-heap readings do not establish total memory safety.

The first probe exposed a simulation catch-up spiral: slow frames asked the next frame to advance more wall time, causing repeated 1.3–1.6-second gaps. The interactive clock now has an 8 ms work budget (checked between indivisible native ticks) and a maximum quarter-second catch-up window. It drops excess **wall time**, never skips native simulation steps or fabricates simulated outcomes. Pause, inactive scenes, artwork waits and scene replacement clear pending wall-time debt. Requested speed is a target under overload; offline deterministic advancement remains unchanged. Read-only diagnostics expose debt and appearance-cache bounds; a sandbox-only validated test hook selects 16× without adding player speed controls.

The final five-second samples observe roughly 25 ms p95 frame gaps, maximum 26–33 ms, about 93–98 village minutes advanced, and successful pause interactions. This is around 6.2–6.5× effective throughput using the engine's three-village-minutes-per-real-second baseline, **not** a passing full-16× or 60-fps result. Cold initial art loading, dense mixed-building scenarios, sustained panning/editing/compilation/saving, longer windows and full source/GPU memory remain performance gates. The final twelve-person capture was inspected for actual authored looks and matching portraits. `.github/builder-review/custom-roster-performance.json` retains before/after measurements and source hashes; `scripts/benchmark-custom-roster.mjs` reproduces this limited probe. Targeted checks cover catch-up bounds, automatic speed transitions, pause reset, equal unconstrained advancement at all five speeds, existing performance-history accounting, authoritative frame-state agreement and exact continuation across scenes at 1×/16×, and the ordinary custom-look Sandbox save/Continue journey.


### Profiled destination-search optimization

**Why:** bounded frame work removed stalls, but maximum rosters still could not sustain their requested speed. A named release-WASM CPU profile of 1,000 twelve-resident arrival ticks identified exhaustive navigation searches and their heap operations as the dominant cost. Single-destination requests now stop when the next settled grid cost exceeds the best complete destination cost, including its fractional approach. Equal-cost nodes remain processed, preserving endpoint/predecessor tie choices. Shared source-to-many-destination trees remain exhaustive, and geometry/crowd rules are unchanged. `scripts/profile-custom-roster.mjs` retains the profiling path; normal stripped release output was rebuilt after the temporary named-symbol profile.

Differential tests compare 900 sampled routes plus 30 explicit fractional, identical, enclosed, cross-river and invalid-endpoint cases against exhaustive search, including roads, foundations and stationary crowds. A nearby-destination check proves that fewer than 100 cells are reached rather than over 1,000. All 777 native tests, sixteen focused navigation tests, strict all-target Clippy, scoped formatting and release-WASM compilation pass. Engine roster checks pass across Mata Atlântica, Cerrado and Caatinga with 1, 2, 5 and 12 residents, shared/mixed original looks, 1×/16×, material conservation and exact checkpoint restoration. Frame-state/exact-continuation regression checks also pass.

Two short browser probes with twelve shared and twelve unique custom looks now advance about 241 village minutes in five real seconds—approximately the requested 16× rate at the engine's base rate of three village minutes per real second. The final probe observes p95 frame gaps of 17.1–17.2 ms, no pending clock debt at the sample end, and maximum gaps of 18.5–24.7 ms. Simulation p95 falls from roughly 12.6 ms to 3.7 ms in this arrival case. Twelve distinct providers remain cached and their portraits/world render were inspected. Cold artwork still takes several seconds (about 4 seconds for one look and 14.5 seconds for twelve in the final probe); these are not latency-release passes. Measurements, profile summary and hashes are in `.github/builder-review/destination-search-performance.json`. This improves the limited arrival benchmark; dense buildings, sustained editing/panning/saving, long sessions and complete source/canvas/GPU memory remain unverified performance gates, alongside the other unfinished plan requirements.


### Shared prepared hair layers and faster custom-cast loading

**Why:** preparing twelve distinct looks repeatedly audited and resampled identical 1254-pixel hair artwork for each material/colour recipe. The compositor now captures each validated source and copied registration into a private 256-pixel layer once per hairstyle/pose. Every final composition still validates the supplied body hash, body binding, colour key, foreground ownership and coloured-body alpha. A returned canvas, a changed source canvas or a mutated registration cannot modify the captured layer. The ordinary single-frame review function remains available and follows the same pixel pipeline. Temporary audit canvases are released explicitly; production pose records retain the normalized composer rather than the large extracted hair frame.

Both styles retain 96 shared normalized hair layers (24 MiB of RGBA), now reported separately from the bounded full-appearance cache. This is not total browser-memory accounting: original decoded sources, activity underlays, body-material caches, transient canvases and GPU storage remain separate. In the twelve-person probe, full appearance frames still account for at most 150 MiB and 85 original images are decoded.

The same sequential benchmark's twelve-unique-look preparation step drops from about 14.5 seconds to 3.2 seconds after its initial shared-look case. This comparison has partially warm source art in both runs. A separate fresh-browser, unique-only case loads all twelve looks in about 5.7 seconds; it sustains approximately 16× over the short five-second play sample with p95 frame gaps around 17.7 ms. Cold loading remains a multi-second operation and does not pass an ordinary-edit latency gate. This does not close dense-map, sustained editor/panning/save or total-memory requirements.

All 288 reviewed pose/palette comparisons and twelve feeding returns pass, as do 384 colour/style poses and sixteen colour feeding returns. A separate verifier compares 400 exact final-frame RGBA fingerprints against a fixed pre-optimization commit (`7aeced031158d6f32cd59156d14a7ce8f2005cec`), covering both hairstyles, all four colours, every pose and the feeding returns with one clothing/skin combination. Its reference capture executes that old renderer rather than generating expectations from the optimized implementation. Seven adverse-input checks and source/registration/output/tint isolation checks pass. Public creator undo/redo, activity previews, portrait, preset export/import, independent duplicate, draft reload, playtest and exact checkpoint checks pass; ordinary Sandbox save/Continue and artwork-failure import/retry/roster replacement checks also pass. The desktop creator capture was inspected. Evidence and source hashes are in `.github/builder-review/prepared-resident-hair.json`, with the fixed fingerprints and capture under `assets/art-direction/character-prototype-v1/prepared-hair-v1/`. ### Straw-hat accessory prototype and visual fit gate

The existing 288 playable pose comparisons, twelve feeding returns and 400 frozen pre-change final-frame fingerprints also pass; no playable appearance changed. Exact prompts, input roles, rejected sources, the accepted standing candidate's registration/hash and inspected captures are retained under `assets/art-direction/character-prototype-v1/straw-hat-prototype-v1/`, the candidate and registration are under `web/art/characters/prototype-v1/`, and `.github/builder-review/straw-hat-prototype.json` records the limited gate. ### Shared body-specific timing and brief neutral-body blink

**Why:** motion review found that the neutral body's closed-eye breathing pose lasted about 1.25 seconds. The new `neutral-v1` presentation profile holds the four existing breathing poses for 2.0, 0.15, 1.35 and 1.5 seconds, retaining a five-second loop with a brief blink. Cross-activity transitions retain the previous profile, mirrored facing and pause behaviour. A read-only visual-state diagnostic verifies that actual gameplay receives the custom-body profile. The hat breathing registration explicitly names/refuses unsupported timing profiles; the accessory still has only four review poses and is not a player option.

A one-millisecond sampling sweep measures exactly 150 ms of closed eyes per custom loop. Tests cover 169 profiled mode-pair cases, existing 156 transition cases, unchanged other clips, original-cast timing boundaries, pause invariance and unsupported profile refusal. Controlled browser time verifies the actual public creator and live preview before/during/after the blink and across the five-second seam, plus reduced-motion freeze/resume and unchanged resident state after cancel. Actual gameplay profile use, ordinary Sandbox save/Continue and the full custom creator/preset/export/import/playtest/checkpoint journey pass. The original appearance library passes 240 exact pose checks, 520 facing/lighting comparisons and ten portraits; all 400 frozen playable-frame hashes remain unchanged.

No runtime optimization is claimed in this attribution increment.

The named module’s final state equals the production module after all 600 ticks. Function weights are profiler attribution with overhead, not latency measurements. ### Cancellable village preparation

Starting a draft playtest or a library scenario/release now prepares its native opening state in a disposable worker. A modal names the current preparation stage and offers Cancel; Escape also terminates the worker. Cancellation, compilation errors and worker failure/timeout close preparation without creating a run. Only a successful result reaches the existing run-storage installation, and the worker is terminated on every terminal path. The ordinary pre-test draft save remains intentional; cancelling leaves its definition, view and undo checkpoint unchanged. Keyboard focus returns to Test after it is enabled again.

The actual-browser verifier covers button/Escape cancellation with delayed worker startup, no late-created run, unchanged draft checkpoint/view, focus restoration, exact worker/direct-native snapshot and report parity, invalid native compilation without library writes, and successful playtest navigation. The existing full creator save/reload/test/restart/return journey and frozen-release launch/storage-isolation journey pass. The preparation dialog capture was inspected. Evidence: `assets/validation/scenario-preparation-v1/`; repeat with `node scripts/verify-scenario-preparation.mjs`.

**Why:** authors must be able to stop preparation without freezing the editor or creating an unwanted saved village. This covers launch compilation, not every editor refresh, thumbnail, map export, durable IndexedDB write or release-publication operation. Their remaining responsiveness/cancellation gates and the original art, biome, sustainability and usability requirements remain open.
