### Cancellable draft export preparation

The ordinary editor Export action now resolves frozen landscape fields and serializes the creation in a disposable worker. A shared preparation dialog shows stages, supports Cancel/Escape, restores focus and terminates the worker on success, failure or cancellation. Only successful preparation creates a download; cancellation cannot produce a late file. Valid drafts retain the previous frozen export bytes, and incomplete drafts export their authored definition without inventing resolved land. The operation does not save, change the undo history or mutate a draft. Playtest preparation uses the same lifecycle helper while preserving its existing messages and worker contract.

`node scripts/verify-creation-export.mjs assets/validation/creation-export-preparation-v1` passes actual button/Escape cancellation during delayed worker startup, responsive UI timers, no late download, focus restoration, byte-exact valid/incomplete exports, a real downloaded file, oversized/structured-clone failure cleanup and unchanged library records/checkpoints/views. The maximum supported stress fixture (64×64, 256 buildings, twelve custom residents) produces a byte-identical 671885-byte export; one local sample observed 46.3 ms including the 25 ms observation wait and a 10.7 ms maximum UI timer gap. These are short local observations, not application-wide latency promises. The desktop preparation dialog was inspected.

`node scripts/verify-scenario-preparation.mjs assets/validation/scenario-preparation-shared-dialog-v1` passes cancellation, native-worker exact opening/report parity, invalid compilation, unchanged storage and successful playtesting after the helper extraction. The normal creator save/reload/test/restart/return journey also passes with Story/Sandbox storage unchanged. JavaScript syntax and diff checks pass.

**Why:** preparing a sharing file should leave the interface responsive and let the author stop before download without risking the saved creation. This completes draft export preparation only; thumbnail generation, durable save transactions, map/release publication and their remaining responsiveness/cancellation requirements still need work. Facial-art consistency and every other original builder release gate remain open; the complete objective stays active.


### Cancellable frozen-release compilation and freezing

Frozen-release confirmation now compiles a disposable native simulation in a worker, freezes resolved landscape/weather/resident defaults and validates that definition before publication-related saves or draft resolution begin. The shared preparation dialog reports stages and allows Cancel/Escape. Cancellation terminates the worker, leaves the author form open, restores confirm-button focus and does not advance the release counter or change saved draft/history/view records. A revision check refuses installation if the active draft changed during preparation. When the draft is unchanged, cancelling avoids an unnecessary synchronous editor refresh/compilation.

`node scripts/verify-release-preparation.mjs assets/validation/release-preparation-v1` passes button/Escape cancellation with no late publication, unchanged full library records, focus restoration, invalid-compilation cleanup and successful first publication at version 1. Worker output exactly matches direct native freezing for the 64×64, 256-building, twelve-custom-resident fixture. The final local sample observed 183 ms including a 25 ms observation wait and an 11.9 ms maximum UI timer gap; these are short observations, not full publication-latency promises. The nested desktop preparation dialog was inspected.

Existing release controls pass versioning, immutable exports after draft edits, author escaping, independent play/checkpoints, deletion independence, imports, credited copies and unsupported-version rejection. Draft export cancellation/parity/failure regressions also pass. Syntax and diff checks pass.

**Why:** authors must be able to stop expensive release checks before those checks cause saved changes. This covers compilation/freezing preparation, not the entire publication path: final draft writes, thumbnail rendering and native validation inside the existing atomic library write still need responsiveness/cancellation work. Map sharing, other saves, facial-art consistency and every remaining original builder release gate stay open; the full objective remains active.


### Cancellable shared-map checks and preview compilation

The player Save map flow now resolves valid-draft land, validates map/climate metadata and compiles its optional land-only preview in a disposable worker. Invalid settlements still share valid land through the existing independent map rules. Cancel/Escape stops preparation before draft resolution or map publication; the form stays open and confirm-button focus returns. The installation step rejects an active-project or draft-revision change. Busy-state controls no longer trigger a full synchronous editor refresh just to begin or cancel preparation. The checked preview report is rendered on the main thread without a second native preview compilation.

`node scripts/verify-map-preparation.mjs assets/validation/map-preparation-v1` passes button/Escape cancellation with no late map, unchanged saved records/history/view, first successful map version 1, saved optional preview and invalid-climate rejection. A forced draft revision change while the actual worker starts is refused before installation. The nested desktop preparation dialog was inspected.

`node scripts/verify-map-controls.mjs` passes all three biomes through normal save/search/export/import/reuse/playtest, land-only previews, invalid-settlement independence, versioning, immutable prior exports, authorship, deletion independence, incompatible import rejection and desktop toolbar fit. `node scripts/verify-map-sharing.mjs` also passes physical-world/legacy-weather preservation, explicit weather, unsupported defaults, independent copies and credits. Syntax and diff checks pass.

**Why:** expensive map checks should be stoppable and cannot publish a stale draft or require a finished village. This completes map preparation only. Main-thread thumbnail painting, durable writes and native validation inside the final atomic write still need the remaining responsiveness/cancellation work. Facial-art consistency and every other original builder release gate remain open; the full objective stays active.


### Cancellable final publication validation and atomic commits

Final map/release package validation now accepts asynchronous native checks, and the player forms run those checks in a disposable worker. Their Cancel save action and Escape remain available during this phase; metadata is locked until it finishes. Cancelling final publication retains the checked draft already saved by preparation and clearly says that no map/release was published. This differs from cancelling initial preparation, which still precedes draft resolution/saving.

The library builds and validates candidate bytes before opening its write transaction. The transaction rechecks the expected source revision, exact source definition/credits and publication counter before atomically installing the package, header, preview and counter. Concurrent counter changes retry with newly generated and revalidated version bytes (bounded to eight attempts); source changes reject. Abort signals reject promptly during even an uncooperative validator, ignore late completion and abort an open transaction before commit. A committed record remains a committed success. Existing synchronous package APIs remain available; import/export formats do not change.

`node scripts/verify-publication-transactions.mjs assets/validation/publication-transactions-v1` passes both map/release cases: pre-abort, asynchronous validation rejection, cancellation during held validation, ignored late success, cancellation at the actual write-transaction boundary, no partial package/header/preview/counter, concurrent versions 1/2 with fresh version-2 validation, rejection of source edits, same-revision content replacement and malformed source replacement. Failed/cancelled attempts consume no versions. Other library writes complete while validation is held, proving no write lock spans the asynchronous check.

`node scripts/verify-publication-cancellation.mjs assets/validation/publication-cancellation-v1` passes actual form button/Escape cancellation in the final native-check phase, no late saved package, focus/metadata restoration, version 1 on the next success, and worker/native report parity for valid and incompatible packages. Both desktop final-check forms were inspected. Existing content-library persistence, map-library concurrency/versioning, native package checks, full three-biome map controls and release controls pass, including immutable exports, independent play/imports/deletion and authorship. Syntax and diff checks pass.

**Why:** an expensive validator must not hold the storage write lock, and cancellation must not leave an orphaned package or silently consume its version. This closes the final native-validation/write cancellation gap for map/release publication. It does not close main-thread draft checkpoint serialization, thumbnail painting, all maximum-scale latency/memory benchmarks, facial-art consistency or the other original builder release requirements. The complete objective remains active.


### Reuse thumbnail terrain between ordinary draft saves

Measured warm manual saves after funds edits in both a five-resident/two-building starter and the maximum 256-building/twelve-custom-resident fixture. Checkpoint creation and recovery encoding/storage were each around a millisecond; almost all blocking time came from constructing a fresh detached preview renderer and repainting its entire landscape on every save.

Thumbnail generation now retains one detached preview renderer per loaded art library in a WeakMap. It reuses the existing ground key and rendering code, so roads, soil/terrain, rivers and biome changes still invalidate ground, while camera/resident/building output is repainted normally. It adds no observers or event listeners and does not cache a stale final image. A separate art library receives its own renderer.

Three local samples per case changed median synchronous save callbacks from 594.7 to 12.5 ms (starter) and 643.8 to 15.9 ms (maximum fixture). End-to-end observations include storage and acknowledgement; raw per-component results, renderer/editor/WASM source hashes and machine metadata are retained under `assets/validation/draft-save-performance-v1/`. Repeat `node scripts/benchmark-draft-save.mjs <output-directory>`. These are warm funds-edit/manual-save samples, not cold terrain regeneration, maximal undo-history serialization, sustained throughput or full memory acceptance.

`node scripts/verify-thumbnail-cache.mjs assets/validation/thumbnail-cache-v1` proves byte-identical cached versus fresh WebP output in twelve cases: first rendering, funds/title edits, camera changes, roads, terrain, river removal, all three biomes, a silver-haired custom resident wearing a hat, land-only preview and returning to the scenario. It checks expected terrain invalidation and one renderer per art library. The resulting thumbnail was inspected. Existing historical draft/map/release/run preview backfilling and durable storage across all three biomes passes with unchanged source records, as does the ordinary creator save/reload/playtest/restart/return journey. Syntax and diff checks pass.

**Why:** changing funds should not repaint the same 64×64 landscape just to update a library preview. Cold preview painting, worst-case checkpoint/history work, broader performance/memory requirements, facial-art consistency and every other original builder release gate remain unfinished. The full objective stays active.

### Restore visible authored roads and avoid empty road layers

The cold-terrain comparison exposed an existing compositing error: painting texture tiles with `source-in` repeatedly erased earlier tiles, leaving the tested road layer entirely transparent. The shared game/editor painter now clips once to the union of authored road cells and paints normally inside that clip. River painting still follows roads. Properties without roads skip the road surface and its texture work entirely, avoiding one 4096×4096 RGBA surface (64 MiB of nominal pixel backing, not a measurement of total browser memory).

`node scripts/verify-terrain-empty-roads.mjs assets/validation/terrain-empty-roads-v1` compares the pinned pre-change painter with current output across Mata Atlântica, Cerrado and Caatinga. Full-resolution pixel hashes prove unbuilt terrain is unchanged. Three adjacent road cells now visibly change 10,740 pixels in each biome, with no changes outside their cell boundaries plus the antialiased edge. The fixture includes soil and resource-edge masking. The three-biome road contact sheet was inspected. Three alternating local samples per case include full 4096-square pixel readback: median no-road times changed from 607.4/1764.7/1829.3 ms to 423.8/458.3/470.2 ms respectively; with roads, from 597.6/1771.1/1803.4 ms to 420.2/472.3/478.5 ms. These are isolated renderer measurements, not sustained frame rate or complete editor latency acceptance.

**Why:** player-authored roads must actually appear on the land, and empty land previews should not pay to texture an invisible full-size road layer. Cold terrain painting still blocks for hundreds of milliseconds; asynchronous painting, maximum history/save latency, sustained dense simulation and total memory gates remain open alongside resident art, biome campaign/art review, recovery and unfamiliar-player usability requirements. This increment does not complete the builder plan.

The existing twelve-case thumbnail-cache verifier also passes against the corrected painter, including road/terrain/river/biome invalidation, fresh-render equivalence, resident/camera changes and land-only previews (`assets/validation/thumbnail-after-road-fix-v1`). The ordinary player menu → create → save → reload → test → restart → return journey passes, preserving Story/Sandbox storage. Its complete 1280×800 desktop editor capture was inspected. JavaScript syntax and whitespace checks pass.

### Paint interactive terrain away from the interface thread

Interactive builder maps, including the biome-change preview, now paint terrain in a disposable worker. The worker uses the shared painter, bundled terrain sources, authored soil/roads/river and regional biome registration. Each map owns at most one pending request: a newer land definition terminates the old request, undo can immediately reuse the displayed terrain, and late results cannot install. Completed bitmaps transfer to a canvas and close; workers terminate on success, cancellation, malformed response, startup/message/clone error or timeout. Unsupported worker/offscreen capabilities retain the synchronous path.

The painter accepts a surface factory and regional texture registration so the worker does not duplicate landscape geometry. Both paths use offscreen surfaces when available. Road texture clips directly into the ground, removing the remaining transparent 4096-square road surface. This changes road-edge antialiasing from the prior DOM-canvas path; the changed pixels remain inside the authored cells and their antialiased border. The last completed interactive terrain is retained per live art library and shared by matching thumbnail renders. Ground input keys are prepared once per document/snapshot update rather than re-encoding soil on every pointer render.

Draft saving does not wait for painting. Pending paints preserve the existing stored preview while saving the actual authored checkpoint immediately. Completion schedules an ordinary preview update; it does not alter the definition. The map keeps its existing terrain while painting and displays an updating notice below the coordinate/zoom controls, with busy state and an accessible description. Painting errors leave editing available and explain the failure; changing land retries the paint.

`node scripts/verify-terrain-worker.mjs assets/validation/terrain-worker-v1` verifies full 4096-square worker/synchronous pixel equality in all three biomes, with roads and river and without either, soil changes, actual interactive supersession, latest-land installation, undo cancellation, late-result rejection, shared thumbnail ground and synchronous capability fallback. Lifecycle cases cover pre-abort, abort during worker construction, normal abort, invalid/empty response, worker/message/clone failures and timeout, including worker termination and bitmap closure. Local timer samples during painting stayed around 6–7 ms while paint requests took roughly 0.4–0.5 seconds; these are not whole-editor frame-rate, response-transfer latency or sustained-memory acceptance.

`node scripts/verify-terrain-save.mjs assets/validation/terrain-save-v1` exercises the real Road tool and Save button with a deliberately delayed terrain worker. The draft and new road persist before the paint finishes; the prior preview remains, then updates automatically. Preview completion leaves the definition unchanged, and reloading preserves exact saved content. Pending and completed full desktop captures were inspected; the first capture exposed a notice/coordinate overlap, which was corrected and recaptured.

The original pinned terrain comparison still proves unchanged no-road pixels in all three biomes, visible road coverage confined to its cells and two full-size surface allocations rather than three (`assets/validation/terrain-offscreen-surface-v1`). The twelve-case thumbnail invalidation/fresh-render verifier passes (`assets/validation/thumbnail-after-terrain-worker-v1`). Ordinary create/save/reload/playtest/restart/return, public biome-change cancel/apply/undo/redo/reload/playtest, and all three player-selected biome creation/planting/playtest journeys pass without browser errors. The biome verifier now opens the existing creation disclosure when the library already contains projects, and visual captures wait for terrain readiness.

**Why:** shaping a 64×64 property should leave the player able to pan, edit and save while the landscape is painted. This closes the interactive terrain-painting block and the matching draft-thumbnail repaint, not all synchronous map-only/import/publication previews or the remaining maximum-history serialization, sustained dense simulation, total memory, resident body/face/clothing art, biome campaign/art sweep, recovery and unfamiliar-player usability gates. The full builder objective remains active.

These static sheets do not establish continuous-playback or in-village neighbour/ground acceptance. The public appearance catalog and gameplay recipes remain unchanged.

Older clients still reject the unknown optional part rather than silently substitute artwork.

It prepares one shared forty-eight-frame transparent overlay set, yields between small batches, and applies it to normal and equipment-return frames before the hat. It checks shared concurrent work, one download per facial source, strict part refusal, old bare recipe bytes, six malformed/late-failure cleanups and overlay disposal without damaging a borrowed body. The shared overlay set stays at forty-eight frames/12,582,912 nominal RGBA bytes; completed appearance cache remains capped at sixteen entries/fifty frames each. These are cache ownership/accounting checks, not total browser/GPU memory acceptance.

The public journey `node scripts/verify-part-creator.mjs <output> --hat --beard [--biome=cerrado|caatinga]` passes independently in all three launch biomes. It uses actual creator controls for all thirteen pose previews, facing/night/in-world scale, undo/redo, resident preset save/export/import and duplication, durable draft reload, full scenario export and import/play in clean browser storage, native playtest, cancelled/applied People edits and exact checkpoint reload. Noncosmetic state and the independent copied resident remain unchanged. Captures and portable scenario/resident files are retained under `assets/validation/facial-hair-creator-v1`, `facial-hair-creator-cerrado-v1` and `facial-hair-creator-caatinga-v1`, with native/renderer/WASM hashes. The normal village import verifier supports a facial-source outage and current Village → Town navigation. The previously failing job-row assertion inspected deliberately deferred hidden Town content; it now opens the player interface before checking. ### Avoid re-encoding retained undo history on every edit

The editor now caches byte sizes for internally owned journal entries in a document-local weak map. Adding a small edit no longer serializes every earlier retained edit to enforce the existing 64-step/8-MiB limits. Imported checkpoint entries are still validated and measured afresh before cloning; no caller-owned object receives a trusted cached size. Weak keys do not keep discarded entries alive. Save format, history limits and replay rules are unchanged.

`node scripts/benchmark-editor-history.mjs assets/validation/editor-history-cache-v1 --baseline=/tmp/solar-document-before-history-cache.js` compares the preceding document source with the changed implementation. Both source hashes are recorded. A synthetic 64-entry journal occupies 7,950,361 bytes; sixteen subsequent edits have median CPU times of 11.62 ms before and 0.69 ms after on this M3 Pro. Final checkpoint hashes match exactly. A separate byte-limited sequence retains 59 steps/8,272,403 bytes, below the unchanged 8-MiB cap. Exact undo/redo, reopening, independent branching, and rejection of a subsequently mutated external journal pass for both implementations. This is a Node journal CPU measurement with a synthetic structurally valid draft, not native scenario feasibility, maximum-history browser save latency or total memory evidence.

The existing document transaction/recovery verifier and 700-sequence compact-journal verifier pass. The isolated browser save benchmark also passes ordinary warm manual saves for the starter property and 256-building/12-custom-resident property, without browser errors. Their median synchronous save calls are about 12.9 and 15.9 ms, respectively (`assets/validation/draft-save-after-history-cache-v1`); these browser cases do not carry the near-limit synthetic journal. JavaScript syntax and whitespace checks pass.

**Why:** a long editing session should not make each new edit repeatedly encode megabytes of already-owned history. This reduces one identified source of edit latency while preserving strict recovery boundaries. Maximum-history browser serialization/save responsiveness, sustained simulation and total memory, body/face/clothing art, biome campaign/art sweeps and the remaining recovery/usability gates remain open. The overall builder objective remains active.

### Exercise near-limit durable history and reduce checkpoint-copy cost

`scripts/verify-large-history-save.mjs` now constructs a native-validated property with repeated whole-property fertility edits. Its journal holds 8,078,050 bytes/sixteen steps, close to the existing 8-MiB byte limit. The fixture is seeded through the content library, then the ordinary desktop editor opens it, changes the shared fund, saves, reloads, and uses public Undo/Redo controls. The durable checkpoint restores exactly without history recovery or browser errors. This verifies the public save/reopen path with a near-limit journal; seeding does not claim that ninety-six fertility changes were made through the brush interface.

The first run identified checkpoint structured-cloning as a main-thread cost. Journal entries already contain only validated JSON data, so checkpoints now use an independent JSON clone for history. External checkpoint mutation cannot change the live journal, and the imported-history validation rules remain unchanged. Existing document transaction/recovery tests and the compact-journal verifier's 700 sequences pass, including plain-data special keys. Added an explicit nested external-checkpoint mutation assertion.

The comparison runs the preceding `805f47c` document source through an isolated server override, preserving current game/storage/UI sources and recording their hashes. To reproduce: extract `git show 805f47c:web/editor/document.js` into a temporary file, then pass `--baseline-document=<that file>` to the verifier. The reference run (`assets/validation/large-history-save-v1`) measures 67 ms checkpoint copying, 88.9 ms synchronous Save, 276.6 ms until the saved status, and a 155.5 ms maximum five-millisecond timer gap. The changed run (`assets/validation/large-history-json-clone-v1`) measures 27.6, 48.5, 224 and 113.9 ms, respectively. Both preserve exact durable content and functioning reopened history. Opening including initial terrain readiness takes about 2.3 seconds. These single local Chromium samples are not sustained frame pacing or total memory acceptance. The complete desktop editor capture was inspected.

**Why:** saving near the journal limit must preserve usable history without unnecessarily copying every change object through the slower structured-clone path. This closes the missing near-limit durable-save/reload evidence and reduces copying cost. The observed timer gap still exceeds the proposed 100-ms feedback target; remaining save/storage responsiveness work, sustained performance/memory, body/face/clothing art, biome campaigns/art and recovery/usability gates stay open. The full builder objective remains in progress.

### Yield checkpoint preparation while keeping save revisions isolated

Ordinary editor saves now prepare checkpoints asynchronously, copying at most four journal entries between yields. The document captures its raw definition, revision and both history branches before yielding; later transactions/undo/redo cannot mix revisions into that checkpoint. Returned history remains independently mutable. The synchronous checkpoint API remains for immediate unload recovery and existing transport/recovery callers.

Save displays `Saving…` immediately and retains the captured view, validation result, world snapshot and terrain readiness. Thumbnail generation and durable writes remain ordered by the existing save queue. A preparation failure is observed even while earlier saves are queued, preserves live work and the prior durable record, and follows the existing retry/export error flow. A completed older save cannot acknowledge a newer edit or paint the newer world into its older checkpoint.

The document verifier covers edits and redo during the first yield, exact captured past/future branches, independently mutable returned data, older save acknowledgements leaving new work dirty, and preparation rejection preserving the document. Existing transaction/recovery and 700-sequence compact-journal regressions pass. The ordinary player create/save/reload/test/restart/return journey passes with Story/Sandbox slots unchanged. Saving while a delayed terrain worker is active still persists the authored road first, preserves the previous preview, updates the completed preview automatically and reloads exact content (`assets/validation/terrain-save-after-yielding-checkpoint-v1`).

The near-limit browser verifier now deliberately holds checkpoint preparation, changes the shared fund again through the actual editor, then releases preparation. Durable revisions preserve the old captured fund followed by the later autosaved fund; the newer edit stays visible. Injected preparation failure preserves both the previous durable fund and unsaved live edit; retry succeeds. Exact saved checkpoint reload, public Undo/Redo and no browser errors pass. The complete saved editor capture was inspected. The 8,078,050-byte history case has a 0.4-ms synchronous Save callback, 28-ms elapsed yielding preparation and 232.3 ms until the saved status (`assets/validation/large-history-yielding-save-v1`). Its sampled maximum timer gap is still 118.8 ms, attributable to the remaining overall save path rather than proof that all storage work yields. Reference-script overrides now accept both `--baseline-document` and `--baseline-editor` when reproducing older save API versions.

**Why:** a player should see saving begin and continue editing while a large checkpoint is copied, without newer edits being acknowledged as part of an older save. This closes blocking checkpoint preparation in ordinary saves and verifies the new concurrency/failure boundaries. Remaining storage serialization responsiveness, sustained maximum-map simulation/total memory, character body/face/clothing production, biome campaign/art sweeps and recovery/usability gates remain required. The overall objective stays active.

### Walking shirt poses, foreground arms and an inherited gait finding

Authored four separate walking shirt silhouettes from the actual native body rows, with independent collar, sleeve and waist geometry. The guide tool now records body/cream-garment row spans to support tracing. Body/crop/source envelopes were frozen before four built-in imagegen calls; exact prompts, input roles, original output paths, unmodified paint and hashes are retained in `short-sleeve-walk-single-0-v1` through `-3-v1`. All four sources pass 100% bounded coverage, approximately 99.85–99.93% guide overlap, near-unity area ratios and zero undeclared paint. No source fitting, warping, trimming or threshold relaxation was used.

The composed review exposed a forearm edge painted over by the shirt in one walking pose. Added independent body-bound foreground ownership to the garment layer: remove the owned arm pixels from the body background, draw the garment, then draw those original arm pixels once in front. This preserves the proper alpha blend where an arm edge overlaps the shirt; drawing the original arm twice would give the wrong edge. The shared foreground validator moved to `character-foreground-mask.js`, with the existing export retained, so garments and hair use the same exact pose/body/run validation without circular painter dependencies. `prepare-clothing-foreground.mjs` constructs immutable masks from authored native forearm regions and the original body binding; it never reads generated garment paint. Source-intent, region-spec and mask hashes are recorded separately. This changes ownership, not source paint or body proportions.

The body-preservation check initially treated a transparent gap beside the torso as a forearm; a one-alpha shirt fringe there is legitimate new silhouette. The check now preserves opaque forearms exactly, allows separately authored volume in empty gaps, preserves all pixels at row 150 and below, and independently checks source-over alpha at owned arm edges within one quantization unit. The combined four-pose review passes twenty-four outside-crop comparisons, twenty-four protected-body checks, four foreground recombinations, four failed-composition cleanups and twenty-eight invalid requests. The 106 actual arm/shirt intersections each blend once. Forearm ownership contains no opaque old shirt/short pixels, and measured walking top-core coverage is 100% with zero collar leaks.

`review-clothing-poses.mjs` now accepts `--clip=walk` as well as breathing and supports multi-source comparisons. Inspected walking body/detail boards for both hairstyles × all three skins, sixty-four day/night/facing/48/120-pixel samples, and sixteen decoded samples from a native-timed daylight/mirrored-night recording. The 0.5-second walk loop repeats for three seconds per lighting case; independent decoding reports a 5.97-second 500×300 VP9 recording with 180 frames. The 722 render calls are not encoded-frame or performance evidence. A separate eight-pose walking/breathing comparison passes forty-eight outside-crop and protected-body checks, eight foreground recombinations/cleanups, forty-eight invalid requests and fifty-two native transition updates. Its sixteen walk↔breathe transition samples were inspected. Evidence is under `short-sleeve-walk-complete-v1` and `short-sleeve-walk-breathe-comparison-v1`.

This is inherited body artwork, not a defect a shirt layer can fix. Walking garment fit/occlusion is accepted as an internal prototype, but walking motion acceptance remains open. Correct/review the underlying body cycle and all affected part bindings before expanding or publishing this clothing family; do not silently rebaseline the source fingerprints or claim the recording itself proves a good gait.

### Rigid walking hair registration and corrected side-part crown

Added an explicit source/target socket path to the shared hair compositor. All ninety-six preserve body pixels below the head and the protected lower face. Nineteen invalid placement/source/body/geometry requests reject, including the actual-paint clipping/cleanup probe. Source/body proof and palette isolation are mechanical gates, separate from style approval. Inspected the body/head/palette boards, sixty-four day/night/facing/48/120-pixel samples and sixteen independently decoded samples of the native-timed walk loop. The decoder verifies the movie hash and preserves its aspect ratio; visible animation clocks disambiguate requested seek times. Render calls are not encoded-frame or frame-pacing evidence.

Also inspected twenty-four shared-renderer world scenes: four poses in daylight and mirrored night across Mata Atlântica, Cerrado and Caatinga, beside Bento, a home, shed, warehouse and roads. The hair stays attached and proportional, faces remain readable, and feet keep their body-owned ground anchor in those views. No fresh native simulation suite is claimed for this JavaScript/art increment.

### Shared complete-family provider and preview lifecycle

Added `prepareFamilyAppearanceLibrary` as the shared internal composer for the complete registered hair/material/tool/stool family. It copies authoring registrations before asynchronous preparation, requires both complete hairstyle owners and all sixteen equipment slots to match the exact family, shares source/preparation work, and publishes each appearance only after its forty-eight normal and two feeding-return frames are ready. Sources and the body family remain borrowed. Tint canvases and other intermediate appearances are released pose by pose; normalized caps and deduplicated seating partitions remain owned by the library.

An acquired appearance has an explicit preview lease. Duplicate callers share prepared frame buffers; releasing one preview cannot invalidate another. The bounded least-recently-used cache evicts only completed records with no active leases or waiting callers. When all slots are in use, preparation rejects rather than destroying visible characters. Composition concurrency and queue length are bounded separately. Closing rejects new/queued work, waits for in-flight work to unwind, clears completed frames and prepared parts, and preserves the borrowed body/source assets. Disposal is idempotent. This is an internal provider contract; initialization itself is not claimed to support aborting an unresolved source request.

Prepared hair parts now expose explicit disposal and recheck their lifetime after asynchronous body/socket and foreground validation. Disposing while either hash boundary is suspended rejects composition and releases newly allocated layers. The single-frame convenience composer releases its private prepared cap after returning the independent result. Existing long-lived public cap callers retain their previous behaviour and pixels.

`verify-family-appearance.mjs` compares all 192 complete provider poses against the independently retained full-pose review hashes and verifies eight distinct feeding returns. Thirty-one invalid/released/capacity operations reject. It exercises duplicate callers, lease-safe eviction, mutable-input isolation, a sixth-source outage after several prepared caps, queue backpressure, queued/in-flight closing, and two direct hair-disposal races. All owned canvas buffers are explicitly empty afterward; all borrowed body frames remain intact. The report pins provider/hair source hashes and the reference report hash. Existing prepared-hair checks pass seven source/body/alpha/colour/bounds refusals and source/output isolation, and all 400 frozen public appearance fingerprints still match. JavaScript syntax and whitespace checks pass. These tests do not establish a sustained total-memory or frame-pacing result; no fresh native simulation suite is claimed.

**Why:** character creation needs complete reusable appearances and predictable cancellation/ownership, not a collection of independent prototype renderers. This provides the shared provider foundation without exposing incomplete hat/beard/clothing support or silently substituting another anatomy. ### Avoid redundant recovery-history parsing after save

Ordinary editor saves now retain the exact recovery-envelope bytes they wrote and remove that recovery copy after durable commit when the stored bytes still match. This avoids parsing the unchanged multi-megabyte checkpoint again. A different recovery copy still follows the existing revision/view reconciliation, preserving edits made during an earlier save. Before-unload recovery continues to use the same synchronous writer.

The near-limit browser journey passes on an 8,078,050-byte history: zero large recovery-envelope parses, exact checkpoint reload, public Undo/Redo, isolated old/new concurrent save revisions, preserved durable/live state after preparation failure and successful retry. No browser errors. The latest local sample records 0.3 ms synchronous Save handling, 28.8 ms yielding checkpoint preparation, 228 ms to saved status and a 119.7 ms maximum timer gap. The remaining stall is not resolved by this change; storage/serialization responsiveness remains open. Evidence is retained under `assets/validation/large-history-recovery-byte-save-v1`.

**Why:** completing a durable save should not parse an unchanged large recovery copy solely to delete it. This removes redundant work without weakening recovery or claiming full responsiveness acceptance. The inactive seated artwork and all other unfinished plan requirements remain active.

### Large-save synchronous phase profile

Extended the real-browser near-limit save verifier to record synchronous JSON serialization, structured cloning, storage access and IndexedDB put durations while Save is active. One 8,078,050-byte local sample records 53.2 ms in structuredClone, 72 ms in IndexedDB.put and 7.2 ms in recovery JSON serialization, with a 117.7 ms maximum timer gap and 226.8 ms until Saved. These measurements are sampled individual calls, not additive whole-operation or sustained frame-pacing claims. Zero large recovery-envelope parses, concurrent revision isolation, failure/retry, exact reload and Undo/Redo continue to pass. Evidence: `assets/validation/large-history-save-phase-profile-v1`.

**Why:** phase evidence identifies storage-side object copying rather than recovery JSON parsing as the next responsiveness target. Move ordinary prepared editor saves through a worker-backed storage path with compact completion metadata, preserving conflict checks and recovery; do not merely yield around the existing large put or remove snapshot isolation. The storage responsiveness requirement and complete builder goal remain open.

### Prepared editor saves use worker-backed storage

Ordinary editor saves now serialize the already captured checkpoint and perform ContentLibrary's existing revision-checked transaction inside a dedicated worker. The worker returns compact durable metadata, preserving the editor's captured checkpoint for markSaved without transferring an 8 MB object graph back. The main library publishes the local/broadcast change after acknowledgement; the worker is silent so its own commit cannot be mistaken for another tab's edit. Transport completion validates draft identity and revision, terminates workers on every path and leaves recovery copies available on failure or uncertain timeout. Existing direct library callers retain their full-record API.

Final near-limit journey passes exact reload, Undo/Redo, concurrent old/new revision isolation, failure/retry and zero browser errors on 8,078,050 bytes of history. This local sample observes 0.2 ms synchronous Save handling, 29.4 ms yielding preparation, 312.8 ms to Saved and a 35 ms maximum timer gap, compared with the prior instrumented 117.7 ms gap. Overall completion takes longer; storage object cloning/put no longer appears in the main-thread phase trace. Separate real-browser tests pass synchronous snapshot isolation, compact acknowledgement, stale LibraryConflict identity, local/remote notification distinction, five transport failure cases and worker cleanup. Public scenario preparation cancellation/native parity and successful playtest navigation also pass. Evidence: `assets/validation/large-history-worker-save-v1` and `assets/validation/prepared-project-save-v1`.

**Why:** large browser-storage object copies should not stop rendering. This improves the measured ordinary-save path without claiming sustained frame pacing, total memory/storage acceptance or completion of the wider plan. Seated artwork, the remaining outfit poses and all other original release gates remain open.

### Coalesce identical pending editor saves

The editor now shares an in-flight save promise when document revision, captured view, artwork revision and terrain readiness match. Entries leave the pending map on success or failure. New artwork/terrain readiness increments an explicit preview revision, so it cannot be accidentally coalesced with an older preview save. New document edits retain their own captured preparation and serialized durable operation.

The near-limit browser journey holds checkpoint preparation, clicks public Save twice more and observes zero additional preparations, then changes the fund and confirms distinct durable revisions preserve old/new values in order. Exact reload, Undo/Redo, preparation-failure preservation and retry all pass without browser errors. The local timer-gap sample remains 34.2 ms; this is request deduplication, not evidence of a further overall latency improvement. Retained results under `assets/validation/large-history-coalesced-save-v1`.

**Why:** repeated clicks or coincident callbacks should not prepare and store the same large checkpoint twice, while new edits and newly ready previews must still be saved. Full performance/memory/storage, artwork and broader builder gates remain open.

### Reuse delivery quotes within immutable planning scans

Four alternating engine-only batches average 843.6 ms versus 1081.2 ms previously (22.0% less simulation time). Short dense browser runs pass exact reloads and show 16× simulation-cost p95 around 22–25 ms versus roughly 32–34 ms in the prior run, while requested throughput remains unmet. Evidence: `delivery-quote-engine-parity-v1` and `mixed-play-delivery-quotes-v1`.

**Why:** deterministic planning repeatedly requested values that cannot change during the scan. Scoped reuse avoids stale long-lived caches and preserves material allocation. These measurements do not close sustained 16×, total memory/storage, remaining artwork or broader builder release gates.

### Isolate queued night-light rendering work

Added an isolated fixed-state Canvas profiler with real rendering calls, source/served-main hashes and explicit instrumentation limits. A first passive paused sample contained no Canvas work because the game correctly stops drawing when paused; discarded that sample. The retained profiler uses a test-only served hook to call the actual render function on animation frames, verifies actual Canvas calls and unchanged simulation minutes, and records method totals separately from whole-frame durations.

The dense night scene paints at about 27 ms versus 8 ms by day. About 16 ms is charged at the final cross-canvas screen copy, but attempts to tile that copy show this attribution does not isolate blending from prior queued drawing. Both broad and tight emission tiling preserve the captured pixels but remain around 27 ms. A software-backed light-canvas hint also remains around 27 ms. Removed all experimental production changes. The rejected tight patch and verifier sources are retained for review, with six exact world comparisons per standard/Retina desktop scale and eight synthetic cases; standard-scale captures exactly match the previously retained three-biome night captures. The archived verifier's later pan extension was not run and does not supply moving-camera equivalence.

A deliberately incorrect test-only bypass of depth occlusion reduces the same night profile to about 13.2 ms while leaving day cost around 8 ms. This diagnostic must never ship: foreground silhouettes must continue to block rear lights. It identifies depth-mask work as the next target, including conservative rejection of masks that cannot intersect earlier emissions, rather than accepting a different appearance. Profile wrappers add overhead and the scene remains paused; none of these results closes sustained 16× or memory/storage acceptance.

**Why:** retain correct lighting and use measurements to reject attractive but ineffective optimizations. Evidence: `world-paint-profile-v2`, `world-light-tiles-profile-v1`, `world-light-tiles-tight-profile-v1`, `world-light-tiles-tight-dpr1-v1`, `world-light-tiles-tight-dpr2-v1`, `world-paint-software-light-v1` and `world-paint-no-occlusion-diagnostic-v1`. The full map/scenario/resident builder goal, unfinished artwork and all original release gates remain active.

### Reject depth masks that cannot touch earlier light

The night pass now tracks conservative regions emitted earlier in depth order. It skips masks before any emission and masks proven disjoint from all earlier regions. Unknown mask bounds retain the original paint path; an emitter without known bounds disables rejection for the rest of that frame. Regions are copied, never shrunk after masking, and discarded each frame, so stale state can cause extra work but cannot leak a rear light. Tree bounds follow each species' actual sprite registration and contact shadow. Building bounds use the previously checked broad envelope; resident bounds follow the current frame's facing and breathing scale. Lamp emissions include the lantern halo. Colour, depth order and the final light blend remain unchanged.

Focused checks contain 1,080 traced sprite/shadow corners across twenty vegetation species, three sizes and three scales, plus empty/unknown/mutated/reset cases. Browser comparisons pass 24 exact world PNG comparisons against unconditional masks: all three biomes, day/night, fixed views and views after real pan/zoom input, at standard and Retina desktop scale. Stable baselines bracket each comparison. A strengthened separate randomized check passes 1,200 exact intermediate light layers, exercising rejection before unknown emitters force fallback. Existing emission/alignment, building-envelope, JavaScript syntax and whitespace checks pass. No native Rust code changed in this increment.

In the fixed-state instrumented profile, the bounded pass reduces night mask image draws from 373 to 261 per frame and median paint from 27.1 to 25.6 ms; day medians are approximately 8.1 versus 8.0 ms. This is a local diagnostic comparison, not sustained 16× acceptance. The profiler now has an asserted test-only unconditional-mask baseline and refuses a lighting override when its source call no longer matches. Longer uninstrumented simulation/panning observations are running separately. Evidence: `depth-light-bounds-dpr1-v1`, `depth-light-bounds-dpr2-v1`, `depth-light-bounds-layer-sequences-v1`, `depth-light-bounds-profile-v1` and `depth-light-unbounded-profile-v1`.

**Why:** a silhouette cannot erase light outside its complete draw envelope. Conservative rejection removes unnecessary mask work while preserving the actual captured image. Sustained simulation throughput, continuous moving-camera/motion review, total memory/storage, remaining character artwork and every other original release requirement remain open.

### Advancing-state follow-up for bounded depth masks

All four uninstrumented thirty-second phases finish successfully with zero browser errors and exact checkpoint reloads retaining twelve residents and 256 buildings. 1× remains about 3.00 game minutes per second. Requested 16× averages 25.57 still and 24.44 while panning, below the 48 target; p95 gaps remain about 83 ms, with maximum observations of 334 ms still and 279 ms panning. These separate advancing-state observations do not demonstrate an overall throughput improvement from the fixed-state mask reduction. Retain the pixel-preserving removal of unnecessary masks, without closing the performance gate.

**Why:** advancing weather, simulation and rendering costs must be measured together; the paused-scene improvement cannot substitute for this result. Next inspect work specific to the advancing scene, including repeated weather-site/building exclusion scans, while retaining the complete builder scope and all remaining art, motion, memory/storage, recovery and usability gates. ### Reuse weather foundation exclusions for unchanged layouts

Weather-site selection now caches only the last building layout by kind and coordinates, comparing values rather than snapshot object identity. In-place edits, additions/removals and reordering invalidate it; progress/metadata changes do not affect the footprint rule. A changed layout still runs the original full exclusion scan. Every call returns an independent array so callers cannot corrupt the next frame's cached selection.

Exact comparisons pass 635 deterministic layout cases, including movement, kind changes, removals, reordered arrays, fresh snapshot objects, edge-touching foundations and returned-array mutation. Twenty alternating local Node samples for 256 buildings/2,508 sites measure about 0.003 ms cached versus 7.5 ms uncached; one changed-layout rebuild takes about 10.5 ms. Existing rain/drying/particle/reduced-motion checks pass. Twelve real-world canvas comparisons are exactly PNG-equal across three biomes, forced rain/wet visual conditions, day/night and views after actual pan/zoom input, with stable bracketing baselines and zero browser errors. These visual fixtures do not replace native weather-balance acceptance.

**Why:** wet-ground rendering should not repeat thousands of unchanged foundation tests every frame. The bounded cache preserves the original selection rule and avoids relying on stable native snapshot references. The runtime recorder now offers an asserted `--uncached-weather-sites` source override and hashes the selected weather source for comparisons. Longer uninstrumented 1×/16× gameplay observations are running separately; the local selector timing does not establish overall throughput or complete the broader builder release gates. Evidence: `weather-layout-cache-v1` and `weather-cache-browser-v1`.

A second build passes frozen-file equality checks and produces the same bundle hash.

The registration file is 649,942 bytes and PNG transfer size is 19,073,941 bytes; this is not decoded memory or performance acceptance. The bundle is separate and not loaded or offered publicly yet. Its source provenance and byte-pinned index support the next loader and saved-recipe integration.

### Faster exact part-region auditing

A CPU profile of real twelve-resident preparation identifies `auditCharacterPartRegion` as the dominant sampled function. The auditor now inspects every alpha pixel in the same order but updates painted bounds once per painted row, using its first/last painted positions. It retains the exact alpha threshold, authored-region bounds, painted/inside counts, coverage ratio and 98% gate; it neither caches mutable input nor skips validation. Source decoding, tinting, PNG preparation and sprite resampling are unchanged.

The independent shipped-function reference matches 1,804 differential cases, covering 60,381,948 channels with threshold-edge alpha, empty/full rasters, fractional regions, offset typed-array views, immutable borrowed inputs and invalid arguments. Existing region-boundary checks pass. All 48 shirt poses across eight complete appearances pass integration checks, and all eight 48-pose PNG boards are byte-identical to the previously reviewed originals. Grain returns, cache reuse, release refusals and colour-source cleanup pass.

An unprofiled maximum-map run reduces initial twelve-outfit preparation from the earlier 35.4 seconds to 28.1 seconds; all four 1×/16× still/panning checkpoints restore exactly. These are short observations, not a statistical or sustained-performance release claim. Profiling itself adds overhead and its 46.2-second preparation time is kept separate from the unprofiled comparison. The benchmark now supports explicit loading profiling. Reports, source hashes, original CPU profile and exact-board hashes are in `part-region-fast-v1`.

**Why:** repeated full-resolution source auditing was costly during complete outfit preparation. This improves real loading time while retaining exact artwork and validation outcomes. Preparation latency, sustained performance, total memory/storage, broader appearance/sleep artwork and all other original builder release gates remain open.

### Staged resident preparation, progress and safe cancellation

Resident artwork preparation now stages at most twelve providers through two workers while preserving the live cast's cache pins. It installs the complete set atomically only after success. Cancellation immediately stops scheduling further residents; already-running shared work may finish, but cannot update progress, install the cancelled cast or evict the protected live residents. The completed appearance-cache limit stays sixteen. Prepared-provider installation rejects forged/mismatched providers and supports a single-use rollback that cannot overwrite a newer installation or a cleared cache.

The game loading screen now shows a resident count and progress bar. Before replacing a village, the ordinary import/new-village path offers **Cancel loading** and Escape in a proper modal above existing dialogs. Cancelled imports remain retryable; current village state and saved bytes remain unchanged. If saving an import fails after preparation, the previous prepared cache/pins are restored before returning to the old village. Cold startup and refreshes of an already installed scene show progress without offering a rollback they cannot perform; moving every remaining session transition's artwork preparation before native installation remains a separate release gate.

Controlled stateful checks cover scheduling limits, copied queued inputs, abort-before-start, mid-batch abort, late completion, preparation failure, active-cast retention, atomic install, rollback and stale rollback refusal. The real player flow imports twelve unique classic residents, partially prepares twelve unique short-sleeved replacements, cancels by button and Escape, drains in-flight work, retries successfully, then injects a storage quota failure while replacing the cast again. Exact native state, primary saved bytes and all twelve portrait PNGs survive both cancellation and failed-write rollback. Current loading/cancel/recovery captures were inspected. Existing library regressions pass 240 original-pose and 520 facing/lighting comparisons; resident-part checks pass 288 reviewed poses, twelve feeding returns and forged-install refusal. The complete Caatinga creator/file/playtest/live-edit/checkpoint flow also passes. Evidence: `resident-artwork-staging-v1`.

**Why:** a long preparation step must neither trap the player nor evict the artwork needed to return safely to their current village. Staging temporarily retains up to twelve candidate providers beyond the ordinary cache; existing cache counters do not represent that full transient memory cost. Total memory measurement, remaining transition cancellation, loading latency, wider appearance/sleep artwork and every other original builder release requirement remain open. No native simulation or source artwork changed.

### Prepare scenario restarts before native installation

`PlayerSession` now compiles a restart in an independent native candidate and awaits resident preparation before restoring it into the existing live Game object. It refuses concurrent restarts, accepts cancellation before installation, frees candidates on every outcome and aborts pending preparation when the session is disposed. Late noncooperating preparation cannot install a cancelled candidate; its returned artwork rollback is consumed. The restart uses the run's frozen definition, preserving the existing project/run ownership boundary.

If saving the installed restart fails, the previous native snapshot, resident artwork and recovery bytes are restored; a later save/restart can retry. Player playtest/challenge restart controls now use this preparation transaction with the same count/progress/Cancel/Escape dialog. The current run is paused during the operation. Once preparation completes, cancellation is disabled for the short native-install/checkpoint commit boundary. A cancelled restart returns to the current village without resetting selection or camera; a successful restart begins paused.

Native-backed lifecycle checks cover pre-start/mid-preparation cancellation, independent compilation, invalid definitions, artwork failure, overlapping restarts, disposal, late completion, failed checkpoint/recovery rollback and save retry. The public twelve-resident test starts from live cosmetic changes that differ from the frozen scenario, cancels partial preparation, preserves the exact native/checkpoint/portrait state, retries to the exact fresh compilation, injects an IndexedDB checkpoint failure and retries again. Loading/cancel/recovery captures were inspected. Existing IndexedDB/library/frozen restart checks and the full public challenge authoring/restart/free-continuation/checkpoint journey pass. Evidence: `session-restart-preparation-v1`.

**Why:** a restart must not erase the current village before its residents can be displayed or its checkpoint can be written. This closes the restart transaction gap; cold startup and other remaining mode transitions, full memory/storage stress, loading latency, wider resident/sleep art and all other original builder release gates remain open. No native simulation code or source artwork changed.

The final restart checks additionally hold an earlier checkpoint write open, cancel the waiting restart promptly without invoking resident preparation, then complete that legitimate save without a latent restart. Leaving a session while its restart is pending is refused. The final public twelve-person cancellation/write-failure/retry journey passes after these queue guards.

### Invalidate old artwork rollbacks after cached cast switches

A reproduced lifecycle gap allowed an old prepared-cast rollback after `setActive` switched to a different already-cached cast. Because no new installation occurred, the cache-install revision alone did not detect that newer village activation; restoring the old cache could remove a provider needed by the current cast.

Part-provider rollbacks now require their exact installed pin generation. The shared appearance library also tracks activation of the complete normalized appearance set, including original-character presets when no part library exists. Actual appearance-set changes invalidate older rollbacks; switching away and back cannot revive them. Equivalent refreshes, duplicates and reordered appearances keep valid rollback working, preserving normal repeated UI updates and storage-failure recovery. Original-character rollback is single-use too. No native identities, save format, source artwork or completed-cache limit changed.

A real-browser reproducer fails before the fix and passes afterward with actual prepared part and original-character providers. It verifies cached switches, away/back changes, reordered/duplicate equivalent sets and exact provider identity. Both twelve-resident public import and scenario restart cancellation/write-failure/retry journeys pass, including exact checkpoints and portrait PNGs. Existing regressions pass 288 reviewed part poses, twelve feeding returns, 240 original poses and 520 facing/lighting comparisons; bounded staging/cache checks also pass. Reports, source hashes and baseline failure: `resident-artwork-activation-v1`.

**Why:** late cleanup from an older transaction must not restore an outdated village's artwork, including switches requiring no loading. Broader session-transition cancellation, transient/total memory, loading latency, wider resident/sleep art and all other original builder release requirements remain open.

### Five-person wet/dry cycles with real upkeep and market effects

The regional cycle harness now supports `--settlement`, retaining the original one-person default. The new controlled five-person fixture adds three homes, three planted/replanting gardens, a kitchen and a workshop, uses actual regional seasons and finite Sandbox opening supplies/funds, enables maintenance and market effects, and gives one resident their ordinary outside job. Inherited workshop condition is authored at 65 percent without invented wear history. Opening service tools are deliberately absent; the test follows actual shortage reports and buys parts through the ordinary town-order API, checking the exact money deduction against quoted prices. It never injects funds, parts, needs or task state during the run. Biome/seed selectors support diagnosis without changing the full default matrix.

All six settlement cases pass: both seeds 42/73 in Mata Atlântica, Cerrado and Caatinga, including generated rivers and explicitly river-free properties. They cover 280 simulated days, 537,600 primary ticks, 286 daily conserved checkpoints and forty exact restored continuations. Each run observes completed resident upkeep and one real parts order; the six orders cost 46,854 cents in total. Sampled tool prices vary during these runs. Sampled group hunger peaks at 42.13 and energy stays above 68.69. Food production, actual forecast/rain windows, soil moisture, solar output, reduced dry-season hydro, no phantom no-river hydro, and material/liquid/electrical accounting remain checked throughout.

The six original solo cases also pass on the current harness/WASM: another 280 days, 286 daily checkpoints and forty exact continuations. Together the two matrices cover 560 primary simulation days and 1,075,200 primary ticks, plus their mirrored continuation steps. Updated source/helper/WASM hashes, definition/end-state hashes and reproduction commands are in `.github/builder-review/biome-cycles.json` and `.github/builder-review/biome-settlement-cycles.json`.

**Why:** a climate-only solo run cannot establish that a group still eats, rests, maintains equipment and pays for missing parts across changing regional seasons. These remain controlled, well-funded inherited-settlement checks using default Sandbox funds, not acceptance of example difficulty, every hardware lifecycle, maximum-roster performance, complete biome artwork or unfamiliar-player usability. Wider resident/sleep artwork, remaining session transitions and every other original builder release requirement remain open. No native game code or artwork changed.


### Reclining resident renderer foundation

The resident renderer accepts optional native-frame contact coordinates from a frame provider. It composites whole 256-square poses into a bounded scratch canvas, preserving reclining limbs and aligning blended poses at their declared ground contact. Existing providers retain their original path and anchor. Invalid contacts are refused; zero-weight poses do not request artwork or contact metadata.

`node scripts/verify-resident-pose-contacts.mjs` compares existing rendering against commit `d4a8453` in 32 exact pixel comparisons, independently verifies four full-frame custom-contact compositions, rejects eight malformed contacts and checks scratch restoration. Existing animation and profile checks pass. These synthetic rendering checks do not establish artwork acceptance.

**Why:** a lying resident needs room below the standing foot anchor without shrinking or clipping the body. Two unchanged generated sleep studies, exact prompts, reference hashes and scoped reviews are retained under `assets/art-direction/character-prototype-v1/sleep-body-study-v1` and `sleep-body-study-v2`. The second study improves the head-to-feet camera direction but still needs measured registration, material masks, layered hair/clothing, accessory and shadow policies, and gameplay-scale lighting/transition review. No production provider enables custom contacts yet. Dedicated sleep artwork and the overall builder plan remain unfinished.


### Sleeping visibility follows engine-assigned homes

`web/resident-presence.js` hides a sleeping resident only after reaching the completed home identified by the engine's `activity_building`. Resident IDs and building array positions no longer determine indoor visibility. Travelling residents, approaching sleepers, missing/unfinished homes and non-home assignments remain visible under the ordinary presence rules.

**Why:** custom residents use stable identities that are unrelated to bed slots. Dividing an ID by three hid or exposed the wrong residents. `verify-resident-presence.mjs` covers arbitrary IDs, reordered buildings, invalid assignments and travel. Actual WASM departure, overnight, individual-sleep and occupation checks pass. The occupation check now observes departure within a bounded interval rather than assuming the old map's 90-minute commute. This fixes presence classification; dedicated sleeping artwork and the other builder release gates remain open.


### Dedicated sleep source registration

`character-contact-frame.js` validates an exact hashed single-pose source, native/source contact landmarks and one uniform scale. It checks every nonzero alpha pixel with resampling margin before drawing the unchanged PNG. Body-family ownership now supports `contact-v1`, includes its registration in attachment identity, exposes the registered contact and releases its owned canvas on disposal. Existing sheet and waist sources retain their previous transforms and ownership rules.

The internal sleep study replaces only base pose 15. `verify-contact-body-family.mjs` proves that all other 47 pose hashes remain unchanged, the output equals a direct uniform source transform, eighteen invalid registrations and five inconsistent ownership declarations are refused, and failure/disposal releases only owned canvases. Existing body-family, registered-frame and renderer contact checks pass. The twelve-view raw-body board was inspected at 192/96/48 pixels, both facings, daylight and diagnostic night tint. Its exact sources, registration, board hashes and scoped findings are retained with the study.

**Why:** dedicated sleep artwork needs an explicit ground contact without shrinking limbs to the standing foot anchor or silently changing existing poses. Actual alpha bounds rejected the pre-generation scale/contact intent; the recorded registration retains faint source pixels as well as the visible body. The source remains byte-identical. No public bundle or appearance revision activates this study. Material masks, layered hair/clothing, accessory/shadow rules, measured camera/anatomy, actual terrain and recorded transitions remain required before accepting it, along with every other unfinished builder requirement.


### Reclining body material regions

Body material registration supports explicit shirt/shorts polygons for poses whose garment seam cannot be expressed as a horizontal waistline. Those poses declare their shoe regions explicitly; the sleep study uses an empty set because it has bare feet. Existing standing/seated registrations keep their previous classifier. Material-only providers retain source-bound pose contacts when present.

The sleep draft has source-bound material regions and an inspected diagnostic/palette board. `verify-sleep-materials.mjs` compares all 192 existing body palette outputs against commit `c2dfb72`, checks four isolated sleep material toggles, unchanged alpha and source pixels, default-frame reuse, defensive mask copies, five semantic points and ten malformed region refusals. The shoe toggle changes no sleep pixels. Existing full material-lab and complete-family appearance checks pass, including original animation output, cache limits, leases and failure cleanup.

**Why:** screen-space rows cannot distinguish the shirt and shorts of a resident lying diagonally. Explicit garment regions preserve the painted anatomy while supporting independent palettes. Only the new sleep region is authored; the other 47 regions are reused after exact body-frame identity checks. This remains an internal draft: waistband refinement with clothing layers, both hairstyles, short sleeves, accessory/shadow policy, terrain/lighting/motion review and public versioned integration are required. No bundled public appearance changes, and the overall builder plan remains in progress.


### Sleeping hairstyles and uniform close-up source mapping

The dedicated sleep study has separate short-curl and side-part layers, generated with the built-in image tool and retained byte-for-byte with prompts, source identities, reference roles and rejected-study findings. The corrected layers use a scalp viewport declared before generation: native rectangle `[8,140,88,88]` represented on a 1254-square source. `character-hair-source.js` validates its one uniform scale and offset, requires the complete source canvas to remain inside the native frame and refuses combination with existing cap-reuse placements. Existing hair sources keep their original normalization path. Bounds remain an audit, never a fitting rectangle.

`verify-sleep-hair.mjs` prepares the actual hair composer against the exact sleeping-body binding and a measured supported-arm foreground region. Eight hairstyle/colour compositions pass. Both chestnut results equal independently drawn direct source mappings; fourteen invalid mappings are refused. Body/source pixels remain unchanged, checked eye/mouth/arm points remain visible and disposed composers reject reuse. Existing complete-family checks pass 192 original appearance comparisons, eight feeding return states, cache/lease limits and failure/disposal cleanup. The corrected runtime board was inspected with alternate body palettes, both facings and diagnostic night tint.

**Why:** the initial small-corner generations repeatedly enlarged or displaced the caps. A predeclared close-up body viewport gives the source artist a readable anatomical guide while preserving one body-bound uniform transform. Earlier oversized or misaligned candidates remain rejected and excluded from runtime. These two hair layers are internal single-pose drafts. Full-family rebinding, cross-pose volume continuity, short sleeves and seam refinement, optional accessory policy, actual ground shadows/terrain/lighting and recorded transitions are still required before public activation. The overall builder plan remains in progress.


### Dedicated short-sleeve sleeping garment

The internal sleep pose has a separate short-sleeve cotton layer under `short-sleeve-sleep-v2`, using the existing fixed-crop garment contract. Its silhouette was authored against the exact sleeping body and frozen before paint generation. The built-in image generator produced the unchanged PNG; the earlier upright-neckline and incomplete-collar studies remain rejected under `short-sleeve-sleep-v1`. No generated paint was retouched. Body-derived foreground ownership protects the head and exposed arms, while explicit lower-body ownership restores the original trousers/legs and their edge alpha.

The corrected source passes the frozen outline audit with 99.94 percent guide coverage. `verify-sleep-shirt.mjs` verifies all 1,094 original shirt pixels meeting the declared 240-alpha criterion, twenty-four compositions (two hairstyles, three skin palettes, four shirt colours), 2,626,560 exact channels outside the crop, 1,609,872 exact preserved lower-body channels, 1,179,648 final-alpha comparisons, 120 independently composed protected-point edge blends and seven invalid registrations. The original body stays unchanged. The palette, cuff/waist and eight facing/diagnostic-lighting views were inspected; the foreground cuff contour was refined from those views without changing source paint or frozen geometry.

**Why:** a diagonal sleeping body cannot reuse an upright seated shirt. A collar revision was required after strict runtime coverage exposed four original-cloth pixels; the coverage criterion was retained. Antialiased body points are checked against an independent single source-over blend, rather than incorrectly requiring their RGBA to remain identical over cloth. The static garment is an internal draft. Full-family rebinding and provider contact forwarding, persistent facial-hair artwork and sleep accessory rules, actual terrain/shadows/lighting, recorded transitions and public versioned integration remain required. Every other unfinished builder requirement remains in scope.


### Complete internal sleep families and retained pose contacts

The internal family definition combines the dedicated sleeping body, both hairstyles and the short-sleeve garment with all existing activity drawings. Existing providers without custom contacts keep their previous shape and normalization. Invalid contacts or incomplete frames release all partially copied canvases.

Frame copies and contact copies now share an explicit retention boundary. No public asset descriptor, recipe revision or save format changes. Persistent facial-hair artwork, sleep hat rules, optional accessory integration, actual ground shadows and biome terrain/lighting, recorded transitions and player creation/save/import checks remain required before public activation. Every other unfinished builder requirement remains in scope.


### Internal sleeping accessories and complete family composition

The dedicated sleeping body now has its own source-bound chestnut beard drawing. Facial silhouette and crop guides were frozen before generation; the original generated PNG remains byte-identical. ### Live gameplay scratch compatibility for reclining poses

The actual gameplay resident surface starts at 128×128, while native contact composition needs complete 256×256 frames. The contact renderer now accepts that existing gameplay surface and upgrades it when the first nonstandard ground contact is drawn. Legacy-only rendering keeps its original path and scratch size. Invalid scratch shapes still refuse input.

`node scripts/verify-resident-pose-contacts.mjs` passes 32 frozen legacy comparisons, four whole-frame contact comparisons, eight invalid-contact refusals and four gameplay scratch lifecycle comparisons across both facings. Those lifecycle checks draw an ordinary pose, introduce a custom contact on the same 128-square surface, then switch back to a legacy appearance and compare exact rendered pixels.

**Why:** internal previews all allocated native-size scratch canvases and therefore missed the smaller live-game allocation. This removes a real activation failure without globally changing existing gameplay normalization. Ground shadows, world/biome/environment review, recorded transitions and public versioned integration remain open.


### Source-bound reclining ground shadows

The dedicated sleeping source now has an explicit native shadow ellipse aligned with its 2:1 ground axis. Its registration is keyed to the exact source hash, uniform scale and source/native contacts; different drawings or transforms do not inherit it. Complete-family leases forward shadow metadata, and retained providers copy and validate it with their frames and contacts. Both live gameplay and the player character preview draw through the shared shadow helper, preserving each scene's existing shadow defaults for earlier artwork. Metadata stays separate from painted source pixels.

`verify-resident-shadows.mjs` passes nine exact legacy comparisons (including blended poses), eight independent registered-axis comparisons across both facings and two sizes, nine malformed metadata refusals, four changed-source/transform refusals and three partial-copy cleanup refusals. The complete internal family check passes 188 unchanged poses, eight feeding returns, 200 retained frames and shadow retention after lease release, eviction and family disposal. The updated static board was visually inspected. Existing public retained-provider checks still pass 768 appearance comparisons and eight recoveries. Exact shadow evidence and reviewed board hashes are under `sleep-body-study-v2/family-v1/shadow-*-v1.json`.

**Why:** a standing oval cannot ground a reclining resident, and a valid shadow must survive the same ownership boundaries as the body. Default blended poses still draw one original shadow rather than accumulating overlapping ovals. This accepts internal static registration only; actual biome terrain/environment review, recorded transitions and public versioned integration remain required. Every other unfinished builder requirement remains open.

The desktop browser suite reaches its Story checkpoint import after menu, HUD and interface interactions at 1600/1280 widths. The next failure is the saved optional-Story checkpoint: its material UI and stock arrays contain 29 entries and the current engine refuses it. Regenerate it through `node scripts/story-all-paths.mjs`, then rerun the full browser suite. Do not treat the existing capture sequence as a completed browser regression.

**Why:** current native material schemas need freshly earned checkpoints; weakening import validation or padding old progress would hide integration errors. This fixture refresh remains in progress.

`review-sleep-world.mjs` renders the complete internal sleep family through actual native-generated regional maps and shared terrain, home, resident, contact-shadow and weather primitives.
