All nine boards were inspected: Mata Atlântica, Cerrado and Caatinga, each in daylight, shared unlit night filtering and a rain visual preset. Each board covers two zooms and both facings, with both hairstyles and sleeveless/short-sleeve outfits, selected beard and hat-off sleep rules: 144 resident draws in total.

The bodies and clothes retain their proportions and diagonal ground contact across regional backgrounds. Soft shadows sit beneath the reclining bodies without an opaque platform. The lower zoom includes the whole reference home; the closer view deliberately crops it to inspect residents. Renderer/source/WASM hashes and exact board hashes are in `sleep-body-study-v2/family-v2/world-v1/render-report.json`; inspected files, findings and limits are in `visual-review.json`. This is a static renderer review, not a simulation of outdoor sleeping, every powered light interaction, motion or public integration.

The fresh `story-all-paths.mjs` replay also passed all thirteen chapters with real resident work, purchases, resource cycles and exact per-chapter save/reload; its final stock and material UI now have all thirty entries. One paid upkeep order cost R$ 76.73. Hash-bound evidence is `.github/builder-review/story-optional-current.json`. The browser suite now passes this Story import and continues into land controls. Its next stale restoration fixtures were regenerated by `verify-restoration.mjs`, which passes real compost cargo/attendance, gradual restoration, exact working continuation, 250 placement comparisons and cancelled-cargo recovery. Other subsystem artifact directories still contain old 19/24/29-material snapshots, so full browser regression acceptance remains incomplete.

**Why:** source-level correctness does not prove that a customized resident belongs on each biome's ground, and obsolete earned checkpoints can prevent otherwise valid browser checks from running. Recorded motion, public versioned resident integration, remaining fixture refresh/browser checks and every other unfinished builder requirement remain in scope.

Cultivation and planting fixture refreshes also pass on the current engine: real gardening, soil-dependent yield/maturity, actual compost consumption, persistent soil nutrients, exact saves and matching shade/solar preview; stock/attendance-gated first planting, separate timber/bamboo inputs, real town pickup and hauling, cancelled cargo recovery and delivered seedling use. These refresh the browser prerequisites without changing saved progress or inventory by hand. Full browser acceptance still awaits the other stale fixture families.


### Encoded and decoded sleep transition review

`review-sleep-motion.mjs` records the real `advanceResidentAnimation` path and complete internal appearance providers through awake/sleep/awake changes. Both hairstyles and both outfits are shown in daylight and mirrored unlit night. The 12-second recording decodes to 360 frames. `decode-sleep-motion.mjs` independently verifies the recording hash/dimensions/duration and produces a full one-second overview plus four 20fps transition windows. All five boards were inspected. Exact movie, input, decoder and board identities are recorded under `sleep-body-study-v2/family-v2/motion-v1`.

The hat switches with the body pose, the beard persists, garment/hair layers stay intact and the reclining shadow remains stable. The visible change is deliberately recorded as a hard key-pose switch: there is no authored lowering/rising sequence. This review establishes discrete-pose/accessory coherence; it does not claim smooth or physically natural get-down/get-up motion, autonomous village sleep, physical display pacing or public creator/save/import integration. The library remains internal.

`verify-living-land.mjs` also passes and refreshes its current browser fixtures: visible seeded forest and real clearing shade, saved road/extraction disturbance, fifteen restrained soil patches, resident restoration, exact checkpoints, read-only appearance and banana-canopy solar exposure.

**Why:** a still image cannot expose a detached hat, unstable contact or activity handover. The decoded movie makes the remaining hard-switch limitation explicit before public integration. All other unfinished builder requirements remain open.


### Staged versioned sleep-family bundle and loader

`prepare-bundled-sleep-family.mjs` packages the complete internal definition as a separate `relaxed-sleep-v1` schema-3 bundle. It preserves all source hashes, copies the five new source PNGs byte-identically and references the existing immutable artwork for the other 131 entries. The bundle keeps all five body sources, forty-eight garment registrations, activity equipment and explicit sleep accessory rules. Its descriptor pins the registration bytes and bounds the response size. No native/JavaScript player appearance catalog selects this revision yet, and older bundles are unchanged.

The dedicated loader uses a closed source catalog, strict schema/field/source counts, streamed registration-size bounds and exact registration/source hashes. It prepares bounded complete-family libraries for both sleeveless and short-sleeve appearances and owns/disposes their source body family. `verify-bundled-sleep-family.mjs` loads it through HTTP and passes 192 exact reviewed-pose comparisons, eight feeding-return comparisons, four retained-provider checks after disposal, five invalid-option refusals and three corrupt/oversized input refusals. Copied sleeping contacts and shadows persist after the library closes. Exact descriptor, source packaging and verifier hashes are recorded in `sleep-body-study-v2/family-v2/bundle-{staging,verification}-v1.json`.

Native/JavaScript recipe revisions, player creator selection, saved/exported/imported residents and scenarios, public world integration and all remaining original release gates still need implementation and verification. The recorded hard pose-switch limitation remains explicit.

Sleep-preview, village and live-dialog captures were inspected for both new runs. ### Terminal resident artwork ownership and cleanup

Appearance caches now have an idempotent terminal `dispose()` distinct from their existing reusable `clear()` generations. Closing rejects active/queued callers immediately, prevents new admission/installation, waits for active work to settle and prevents late cache publication. Superseded, cleared and closed installation tokens drop their old-entry references and cannot restore stale cache generations; the current rollback remains exact and once-only.

Both public resident-part and appearance libraries expose terminal cleanup. Their preparation groups settle every branch before private resources are released, including registration/source subrequests after a sibling failure. Cleanup disposes private body-family composers, prepared hair and facial overlays, clears material/source caches and drops owned catalog references. The appearance wrapper refuses new operations and stale installation rollback, rejects waiting preset/custom callers immediately and waits for their underlying work. A close before lazy part initialization prevents creation of a new part library. Fully prepared providers already borrowed by a view remain drawable, including their copied sleeping contacts/shadows; cleanup does not erase caller-owned frames.

`verify-appearance-cache.mjs` passes its original queue/LRU/generation/active-pin checks plus terminal queue rejection, active drain, no resurrection and installation-token tests. `verify-resident-artwork-disposal.mjs` passes 150 exact borrowed frame/feeding-return comparisons across a classic preset, classic modular hat/beard and v6 resident, fifteen closed-operation refusals, preserved ground metadata, stale rollback refusal, close-before-import, blocked bundled preparation, failed parallel preparation with a blocked body source and blocked preset decoding. Hash-bound evidence is `.github/builder-review/resident-artwork-disposal.json`. Public v5/v6 renderer checks pass another 192 pose and eight feeding-return comparisons; the classic renderer regression passes 288 poses and twelve returns with native catalog parity and bounded caches.

**Why:** queued work and private source families need an explicit owner lifetime, while a finished provider borrowed by a visible view must not be destroyed under it. This establishes and verifies the cleanup API; it is not a measurement of total RAM/GPU release or acceptance of every mode/session transition. Remaining controller ownership integration, sustained memory/performance checks and every other unfinished original builder requirement remain open.


### Artwork ownership in ordinary session transitions

The real menu transition now installs the new cast's active appearance set even when all of its artwork was already loaded. Previously that fast path retained the old custom cast's pins. When the resulting cast has no custom appearances, the owner detaches and drains the inactive part library. An active custom cast is protected. Cancelled identity previews can retire their unused part generation and prepare a fresh one later; successful cosmetic/import commits and completed/cancelled restarts reconcile ownership against the actual surviving native cast. Retirement is suppressed during an unfinished cast installation.

Retired generations are tracked independently of the current part slot. A late lazy import or old preparation cannot overwrite a newer generation, and terminal cleanup waits for generations already being retired. Resident scratch canvases are zeroed and removed on world replacements and replaced cosmetic identities, rather than accumulating old resident IDs across sessions. The read-only development counters expose owner and scratch identities for verification; regular players use the existing menu, preview and save controls.

`verify-artwork-session-retirement.mjs` passes active-cast protection, detached-library cleanup, borrowed frame/contact/shadow preservation, exact new-generation preparation, stale lazy-import isolation, overlapping old/new loads and terminal drain. `verify-artwork-mode-switch-ui.mjs` passes a twelve-person shared-custom-look playtest to normal Story and Sandbox, preserving the old run's saved snapshot, returning scratch IDs to exactly the five current residents, and cancelling/reopening a custom preview twice without changing native state. Story and Sandbox captures were inspected. Hash-bound evidence is `.github/builder-review/artwork-{session-retirement,mode-switch-ui}.json`. The twelve-person unique-outfit restart regression also passes cancellation, exact restart, portrait/checkpoint preservation, failed-write rollback and successful retry; its report is `artwork-retirement-restart-ui.json`. The normal-save import/cancel/write-failure regression is running separately.

**Why:** a cleanup API cannot release session resources if the live controller never hands them back, and already-ready artwork must not bypass the new cast's ownership update. This closes the specified preset-cast/menu and cancelled-preview paths. It does not measure total RAM/GPU release, retire individual inactive family revisions while another custom cast is active, or accept every remaining session/performance/artwork/original builder gate.

The final normal-save import regression also passes with twelve unique residents: progress visibility, button and Escape cancellation, late completion isolation, exact native/saved-byte/portrait preservation, successful retry and artwork rollback after an injected storage write failure. Its current source hashes and results are retained in `.github/builder-review/artwork-retirement-import-ui.json`. No Rust or artwork source pixels changed in this ownership milestone.


### Measured artwork memory baseline

`benchmark-artwork-memory.mjs` measures the real public appearance/staging path across four shared-look and four twelve-unique-look load/retirement cycles. It draws the complete new sleeping providers, separates raw active/released snapshots from forced-GC diagnostics, and records Chromium isolate/array-buffer/embedder counters plus RSS for processes reported by that isolated browser. Actual GPU/driver status, machine/browser versions and source hashes accompany the measurements.

The first run finishes on the Apple M3 Pro using headless Chromium 151 with SwiftShader. Shared casts prepare in roughly 3.5 seconds; twelve unique mixed v5/v6 looks take roughly 21 seconds. After retirement and forced GC, JavaScript heap usage returns to about 2 MiB and backing storage to roughly 0.27 MiB. Process RSS remains much larger: approximately 1,230–1,397 MiB after shared cycles and 1,856–1,927 MiB after unique cycles, primarily in the renderer process. The isolated baseline is about 264 MiB. These are measured process totals with shared-page double counting, not unique physical RAM or dedicated GPU memory.

**Why:** empty owner counters and a small JS heap do not establish that decoded image/canvas memory has been returned. Exact baseline samples are in `.github/builder-review/artwork-memory-baseline.json`. This is diagnostic evidence requiring further investigation, not a passed memory limit or a measurement of full 64×64 gameplay/editor allocations. Broader performance, hardware/GPU and every unfinished original builder gate remain open.


### Explicit source and normalization-buffer cleanup

Decoded source records now expose idempotent disposal. The bundle owners track their own loaded images, including failed source-hash validation, drain outstanding source work and release those images when preparation fails or the bundle closes. Classic part-library closure releases its own decoded sources. Injected borrowed sources and finished borrowed providers retain their existing ownership rules.

Sprite-sheet normalization now zeroes its temporary full-source and per-pose cut canvases immediately after copying each normalized result. Failure clears partially created outputs as well. `verify-sprite-frame-disposal.mjs` compares against the frozen pre-cleanup algorithm in commit `837c926`: all 144 poses across classic Iara/Bento and neutral body sheets retain exactly identical native RGBA pixels; 153 temporary canvases are released. An injected draw failure releases all seven allocated canvases, preserves the borrowed source and allows a clean retry. Public sleep-family verification also passes 192 pose comparisons, eight feeding returns, four ground-metadata checks and four preserved top selectors. Source-loader, terminal-disposal and appearance-cache checks pass.

**Why:** decoded images and transient canvas buffers should have explicit lifetimes rather than depend entirely on garbage collection. This is a correctness improvement, not an accepted memory-performance fix. Four shared and four twelve-unique cast cycles remain comparable to the earlier high process-RSS baseline; final unique-cast retirement is approximately 1.91 GiB. A separate two-cycle critical-memory-pressure diagnostic also did not materially reduce process RSS, while DOM counters returned to one document, eight nodes and zero listeners. Full reports and comparison are `.github/builder-review/artwork-memory-{owned-release,canvas-release,pressure,comparison}.json`. Headless SwiftShader process RSS includes shared-page double counting and does not establish dedicated GPU or full-game memory. Native/renderer retention, full 64×64 performance, remaining session/artwork coverage and all other unfinished original plan requirements remain open.


### Facial-hair source release after overlay preparation

The classic resident adapter now explicitly disposes decoded facial-hair images before removing them from its shared source map. Those sources are owned by the adapter and their completed overlay pixels have already been copied. The same cleanup runs when source hash validation rejects the artwork. It awaits the exact source promise and removes only the matching cache entry.

`verify-facial-source-disposal.mjs` instruments the actual source loader and proves that all nine decoded sources are released exactly once immediately after successful preparation, an invalid-hash source is released on failure, repeated terminal closure does not release them again, and the finished borrowed pose remains pixel-identical after closure.

**Why:** deleting a cache entry bypassed terminal cleanup for these owned images. This closes that specific lifecycle gap without claiming that the unresolved overall renderer-memory retention has been fixed. Full builder release gates remain open.


### Current-camera gameplay regression coordinates

Gameplay browser checks no longer reproduce the old smaller-map camera origin and minimum zoom. The development renderer exposes a bounded cell-framing query that changes only the camera and returns its actual projected point; a shared browser helper uses that point for normal mouse interactions. Restoration, living-land, cultivation, battery moves, Sandbox placement and the general game interaction checks use the helper. Invalid or out-of-map cell coordinates are rejected before the camera changes.

The desktop regression passes its 1600/1280 interface checks, Story outcome controls, extraction/ground disturbance, compost delivery and actual resident restoration, cultivation, planting and living-land interactions. The restoration capture was inspected and its queued job correctly identifies cell 18,18. The run then reached an obsolete climate Sandbox fixture; the current native climate verifier regenerated that fixture and passed forecasts, rain collection, solar/river differences, exact saves and Sandbox season changes. The broader browser suite remains in progress and is not accepted as completely passing.

**Why:** the centered 64×64 map made duplicated old screen-coordinate formulas click different cells. Reading the real renderer projection keeps pointer checks meaningful instead of changing native expectations to match an incorrect click. This test-infrastructure correction does not complete the remaining original builder release gates.

At 1×, observed throughput is about three game minutes per real second; selected 16× reaches about 41 versus the nominal 48, with frame-gap p95 around 42 ms. This does not accept the runtime-performance gate. **Why:** benchmarks using only older resident revisions cannot establish the cost of the characters players create now. Current-artwork editing is responsive in these sampled operations, while maximum-world 16× throughput and ground rendering require further work. All other unfinished original plan requirements remain open.


### Exact viewport-cropping experiment, not promoted

A renderer-side source-cropping candidate was compared against the frozen `7f91902` ground primitive before any production change. It inverse-projects viewport corners into the existing 4096-pixel terrain source, preserves four source pixels of sampling margin and draws the same affine mapping from a bounded source rectangle. `verify-ground-crop-probe.mjs` compares full native RGBA output across all three actual biome textures, four camera positions/zooms, DPR 1/2 and two lighting filters: all 48 cases match exactly. The verifier retains the frozen reference rather than comparing an optimized renderer against itself.

The mixed-village runtime benchmark now accepts an explicit local ground-renderer source override and records its hash, permitting isolated experimental runs without altering production. The candidate completes the same maximum-current-cast 1×/16× stationary/panning workload and exact checkpoint restores. However, paint medians remain about 7.6–7.8 ms, matching the baseline, and p95 frame intervals remain around 42 ms at 16×. The modest throughput variation does not establish a ground-rendering improvement. The candidate is therefore not promoted; `web/world-renderer.js` remains unchanged. Raster cases, source hashes and both measured reports are `.github/builder-review/ground-crop-experiment.json`.

**Why:** reducing apparent work is not enough when the browser already performs equivalent clipping. Pixel-preserving experiments still require measured benefit before adding production complexity. Reusing an unchanged viewport's raster, native simulation costs, total memory and the other unfinished builder release gates remain to be addressed.


### Bounded, pixel-identical game viewport backdrop

The game now reuses one opaque viewport raster when terrain, camera, scale, pixel density and sampling state are unchanged. It caches the existing terrain draw and its solid background together, so copying the result introduces no additional alpha-edge rounding. Terrain/source revisions and camera/resize changes rebuild it; unsupported filter/compositing states and oversized viewports fall back to the original direct draw. The owner has explicit clear/dispose operations, never owns or modifies the borrowed terrain, and is cleared during normal world-visual replacement. Its default allocation ceiling is 16 megapixels (64 MiB); the measured 1440×900 viewport uses 5,184,000 bytes, and DPR 2 uses 20,736,000 bytes. This is one bounded active viewport, not a claim about total renderer/GPU memory.

`verify-world-backdrop.mjs` passes exact frozen-reference ground pixels in 48 real-terrain cases across the three biomes, four camera poses, DPR 1/2 and lighting fallback. It also verifies repeated hits, changed pixels at the same source identity with an explicit revision, bounded fallback, opaque-background validation, idempotent clear/dispose and preserved borrowed terrain. `verify-world-backdrop-browser.mjs` passes 36 complete maximum-village game views: all three biomes, day/night, DPR 1/2, centered/fractional-pan/zoomed cameras, 256 buildings and twelve unique current v5/v6 appearances. Existing-cache, cold-cache and repeated-cache hashes all equal the frozen direct rendering, with exact unchanged native snapshots. Day/night representative captures were visually reviewed. Evidence is `.github/builder-review/world-backdrop-{raster,browser}.json`.

The isolated production benchmark reproduces the improvement on the reference Apple M3 Pro with headless Chromium/SwiftShader: median paint cost falls from roughly 7.6–7.8 ms to 2.2–2.7 ms. Selected 16× advances about 46.3–46.6 game minutes per real second versus the prior 41.0–41.1, with exact checkpoints in all phases. Stationary frame-gap p95 improves in this run, while panning still has frame spikes around 42 ms. Both measured reports, actual module/main/build hashes and GPU identity are `.github/builder-review/world-backdrop-performance.json`. The benchmark supports explicit main-source overrides for isolated candidate comparisons; hashes reflect the actual selected source, including legacy UI overrides.

The complete `node scripts/dev-check.mjs --browser-only` journey also passes with the integrated cache: 200 captures, no observed browser errors, normal gameplay and terrain edits, scene/mode changes, lifecycle/save isolation, exports, artwork tools and separate Sandbox save/reload groups. Current evidence is `.github/builder-review/world-backdrop-desktop.json` and the refreshed `desktop-game-regression-current.json`.

**Why:** repeating the same affine terrain raster on every frame consumed substantial time even while the camera stood still. Reusing the exact opaque viewport reduces that work without changing artwork, projection, simulation steps or speed budgets. Native simulation cost, sustained high-density panning, broader memory/hardware checks, remaining art/customization coverage and every other unfinished original builder requirement remain open.


### Reuse the viewport buffer across camera redraws

Backdrop rebuilds now keep their existing canvas dimensions when the viewport size has not changed. Before repainting the opaque background they reset the local transform explicitly, then apply the current world transform. Real width/height changes still resize the required dimension, and clear/dispose still zero the owned buffer.

The renderer verifier instruments real HTML canvas dimension setters: twenty camera/revision redraws perform no further dimension writes; a real width change performs exactly one width update. Disposal releases the owned surface without changing its borrowed source. All 48 frozen terrain cases and 36 complete game views still match exactly, including warm/cold/reused caches, day/night, both pixel densities and camera/zoom changes. Updated current-module hashes and results are in `.github/builder-review/world-backdrop-{raster,browser}.json`.

**Why:** resetting an unchanged canvas discards its drawing state unnecessarily while panning. Reusing that buffer removes those explicit resets without changing pixels or increasing the viewport budget. This verifies the resource operation and visual invariants; it does not claim an additional measured frame-rate or total-memory improvement. The earlier full desktop regression remains evidence for the preceding integration, while these targeted renderer checks cover this buffer-lifetime adjustment. Remaining original builder release gates stay open.

### Additional body shape: walking source and atlas ground origins

`web/character-contact-atlas.js` adds versioned ground-contact registration for a separated sprite atlas. Each pose owns an explicit source and native ground origin, while the complete source retains one uniform scale. Registration validates source identity, dimensions, pose order and bounded coordinates, rejects clipped source silhouettes or native crops, and releases partial outputs on failure. Body-family ownership, recolouring and appearance providers preserve the new contact metadata. Existing sheet, waist and single reclining-contact registrations retain their behavior and pixel identities.

The broader walking source in `body-broad-v1/walk-candidate-v3.png` has four authored contact/passing poses. Earlier clipped and high-knee candidates remain excluded. The corrected low passing feet use the new atlas registration: pinning each pose to its lowest boot had created artificial torso bobbing as feet moved across the projected ground plane. Registered upper-silhouette movement relative to the ground origin spans 5.63 native pixels across the four poses, using one scale of 0.45. No pose receives an anatomical stretch or separate scale.

`node scripts/verify-contact-atlas.mjs` passes four exact source-ownership/affine comparisons, 26 malformed-registration refusals, five ownership refusals, five invalid contact-index refusals, 16 real-renderer comparisons in both facings at two scales, per-pose contact propagation through diagnostic recolouring, 44 unchanged borrowed poses, partial-preparation and failed-binding cleanup, and disposal. The integration fixture deliberately mixes old poses and diagnostic colour regions with the new gait; it does not approve a complete body or fitted clothing. Existing body-family and reclining-contact checks pass; public sleep-family checks retain 192 exact poses and eight feeding returns, and classic recipes retain 288 exact poses and twelve returns.

The final ground-aligned comparison, all six biome boards (24 static world poses), and representative decoded frames from the approximately six-second day/mirrored-night movie were inspected. The limited verdict and exact source, registration, renderer, video and review hashes are recorded in `body-broad-v1/walk-review-verdict-v1.json`. This accepts the walking source only for further internal family authoring. Continuous travel cadence, transitions to other activities, the remaining body sheets, genuine sleep, fitted parts, portrait consistency and public creator integration remain unfinished.

**Why:** body customization requires new authored anatomy and a renderer that respects each pose's projected ground origin. Using the furthest boot pixel as that origin made a plausible foot movement shift the whole body vertically. Explicit atlas contacts remove that assumption while preserving all existing resident versions. This milestone does not complete the resident creator or the broader builder plan.

### Sleep transparency review correction

The earlier sleep-source rejection was based on the image viewer rather than delivered alpha compositing. `node scripts/review-broad-sleep-source.mjs` now decodes each untouched source in Chromium and composites it over green, dry and night backgrounds. The nine resulting views were inspected: there is no visible exterior colored halo. The viewer representation showed hidden RGB outside the silhouette; source alpha counts alone did not establish a defect. The original three sources remain byte-identical. Their review record now corrects the rejection and pins the composite and alpha measurements.

**Why:** acceptance must follow actual rendered pixels. The clean compositing reopens the original v1 candidate for native scale, lying anatomy, ground placement and lighting review; it does not accept the sleep pose into a body family or public recipe. Those reviews and the original remaining creator/builder requirements remain unfinished.

They include the previous installation retained for rollback, deduplicate providers shared by current/rollback entries, and report zero cache-retained surface bytes after closure. The current-entry frame limit remains 800; the diagnostic bound including one rollback installation is 1,600 slots.

**Why:** the old fixed 256×256 estimate undercounted a real public Broad + straw-hat provider: 50 unique surfaces require 14,649,332 RGBA bytes, versus the old 13,107,200-byte estimate. `verify-broad-memory-surfaces.mjs` confirms the reported upper bound equals those actual surface dimensions and becomes zero on close. Pure checks cover margins, rollback/alias ownership, eviction, clear, disposal and malformed values. The generic cache suite and 288 historical pose/12 feeding-return comparisons pass. Catalog comparisons now use semantic object equality and include body-specific facial choices; randomization checks cover both broad clean-shaven and existing relaxed bearded recipes. These figures exclude externally held providers, in-flight work, decoded images, internal family caches and browser/GPU overhead, so this does not pass the outstanding process-memory or maximum-map performance gates.

### Public broad-build editor/runtime/memory measurements — 2026-10-28

Extended the existing benchmark fixtures to use the public Broad build. A native-validated 64×64 editor import contains 256 buildings across 21 inland types and twelve distinct appearances. Import-to-ready measured 7,056 ms after the empty editor was loaded. Across five samples per action, maximum handler/two-frame presentation times were: funds 74.5/77.1 ms, undo 70.0/72.8 ms, redo 68.3/71.3 ms, zoom 34.5/39.1 ms and pan 7.0/16.0 ms. The maximum-fixture editor capture was inspected.

**Why:** the new public body must be measured through its real preparation/cache path. Shared and unique twelve-person 64×64 arrival casts also ran sampled five-second windows at 1× and 16×. Loads measured about 2.2/4.4 seconds; windows advanced about 15/241 simulated minutes with 10.3 ms p95 headless frame gaps and no pending simulation debt at each end. The recorded clock dropped-time value is cumulative, so it is not a per-window no-drop claim. The unique 16× gameplay capture was inspected.

A separate isolated appearance-memory benchmark ran two shared and two unique preparation/release cycles. Surface/owner diagnostics drained, borrowed providers and preview canvases were released, and post-GC backing storage returned to 343,671 bytes. Browser-owned process RSS nevertheless reached about 1.9 GB and stayed near 1.85 GB after the final release. This is an unresolved native-browser memory/high-water finding, not a passed memory-release gate or proof of a JavaScript leak. Investigate decoded-image ownership and browser-native caches before accepting memory behaviour. Detailed machine, raw RSS/process, heap, ownership and source fingerprints are retained in `body-broad-v1/performance-v1/`.

These headless measurements do not prove physical-display pacing, dense gameplay, sustained brush/multi-selection, maximum utility/crop/animal configurations, cancellation, or the original complete performance/release requirements. The full objective remains open.

### Explicit decoded bitmap ownership and controlled memory comparison — 2026-10-28

Published composed bundles now decode their verified PNG responses into actual `ImageBitmap` objects and explicitly close them after library retirement. Read-only dimensions/completion metadata keep the existing registration validators usable without wrapping or repainting the source. Default image-element decoding remains unchanged for older families. Missing bitmap support fails explicitly; failed/cancelled loads close every decoded source, including a bitmap that finishes decoding after cancellation.

**Why:** the prior decoded-image path retained a high renderer-process memory watermark. With the same browser version, benchmark script and cast/release cycles, the only benchmark input-code differences are the image loader and composed-bundle loader. Peak browser-owned RSS fell from 1,911,111,680 to 1,549,910,016 bytes; last post-release/GC RSS fell from 1,847,705,600 to 1,244,889,088 bytes. This is a single controlled diagnostic comparison, not a complete memory-release pass: the remaining footprint is still far above the roughly 276 MB baseline.

All 37 published source PNGs decode to identical 1:1 canvas pixels in the two paths. The composed results are **not** pixel-identical after browser scaling: antialiasing/downsampling differs, and eight curly-hair poses retain additional faint pixels in bounded transparent margins. Native contacts remain exact in 100 pose/return comparisons across both hairstyles. All six paired base/idle/activity sheets were inspected; visible proportions, registration and tool layering remain consistent. The source transforms, encoded assets and logical body geometry are unchanged. Failure/abort cleanup, normal source-loader regressions and the representative full public creator/save/share/play flow pass. The in-app Broad/curls sprite and matching portrait were inspected with no captured warnings/errors. Raw differences, paired renders, memory samples and fingerprints remain under `performance-v1/memory-bitmap/`; no claim of unchanged resampled pixels is made. Remaining renderer-native retention, wardrobe/transitions, complete art pairings and every original release gate remain open.

### Reused clipping probe and bounded readback — 2026-10-29

Hair preparation now reuses one 1024×1024 clipping probe for each complete family and reads only the registered draw rectangle with edge padding. The entire painted extent is still checked against the same transparent-margin budget. Scratch storage is cleared on success and failure; no source pixel, placement transform or final appearance cache is changed.

**Why:** each pose previously allocated a full probe and a full 4 MiB readback, even when the registered draw occupied a small fraction of it. `verify-pooled-hair-preparation.mjs` compares against committed pre-change code at `83a45c3`: 200 normal/return frames and four portraits across both hairstyles, hats/no hat and several hair colours are pixel-identical with identical origins and contacts. Probe allocations drop from 192 to four across these variants, and inspected pixels drop from 201,326,592 to 11,422,020 (about 94%). Every owned surface is released. The composed regression retains its 48 poses, 20 equipment differences, two return differences and 25 refusal/lifecycle cases, including partial-failure cleanup.

An isolated, source-pinned benchmark rerun measured unique twelve-resident preparation at 3,759/3,562 ms versus 4,914/4,767 ms with the previous bitmap path. An initial run invalidated by a concurrent comment edit is excluded. RSS changes are modest and are not claimed as a material memory improvement; the remaining renderer-native retention is still open. The in-app sprite/portrait loaded and were inspected with no captured warnings/errors. Detailed exact-pixel and timing evidence is retained in `performance-v1/pooled-hair/`. Full process-memory, art, wardrobe/transitions and original builder/release gates remain unfinished.

### Repeated retirement, pressure and unavailable native sampling — 2026-10-29

Ran eight shared/unique twelve-resident preparation/release cycles with the current public body path, followed by terminal retirement and a critical browser memory-pressure notification. Every released sample returned to the isolated DOM baseline (one document, eight nodes, zero listeners), with no resident cache, borrowed providers or preview surface remaining. Peak observed RSS was 1,467,351,040 bytes; terminal RSS stayed near 1.13 GB. Pressure had no material effect. The installed Chromium protocol documents this notification as covering all processes, so it must not be described as a browser-process-only test.

**Why:** ownership retirement, browser allocator retention and process RSS are different evidence. A separate four-cycle renderer-native sampling run accepted `Memory.startSampling` but returned zero allocation samples even while artwork was active. Those profiles are marked unusable, not evidence of zero native allocations. `analyze-artwork-memory.mjs` now explicitly checks isolated DOM/listener recovery and cache retirement while keeping RSS/native-profile conclusions separate. Five synthetic retained-object mutations are rejected, and empty/nonempty native profiles are distinguished. Raw reports, machine/protocol limits and fingerprints are retained in `performance-v1/retirement-pressure-v1/`.

These finite runs do not prove an unbounded leak, a permanent plateau or safe native-memory reclamation. Renderer-native ownership/RSS acceptance remains unresolved; no production-memory fix or full release pass is claimed. Missing wardrobe/transitions, complete art pairings and every original builder/release requirement remain in scope.

### Pending gestures cancel consistently before history traversal — 2026-10-29

Reproduced two further input defects: Undo during an active brush drag undid an earlier edit and then committed the pending stroke on release, while a pointer-cancelled pan could leave the camera outside the map. Added one gesture-cancellation path that clears brush, route and move state, restores an interrupted pan's starting view, preserves selection, and avoids redundant paints. Tool switches and pointer cancellation use it. All editor history callbacks and the keyboard shortcut share one command: a pending gesture is cancelled first; a later Undo/Redo traverses committed history normally.

**Why:** cancelling unfinished input must not rewrite a previously completed edit. Six actual-listener/state contracts and all thirteen release-handler regressions pass. Real mouse drags cancelled through Undo, keyboard activation of its button, Redo and Escape leave the durable checkpoint and undo history exactly unchanged; the matching uncancelled paint remains an effective positive control and undoes atomically. The complete editor save/test/restart/return journey and character-creator editing/undo/reload/playtest journey pass, with normal Story/Sandbox storage unchanged. The current desktop editor was inspected. Reports and current source fingerprints are retained in `artifacts/editor-gesture-cancel-v1/`. Broader performance, recovery, full art/wardrobe, example completion and the original release gates remain open.

### Native resident-memory observations beyond RSS — 2026-10-29

Added optional `--memory-dumps` to the real public appearance-library benchmark. Chrome memory-infra captures detailed baseline, active twelve-unique-resident and terminal-release dumps, each bracketed by explicit clock markers. Broad default trace categories initially exceeded the 128 MiB diagnostic bound and were rejected; the final configuration records only explicit memory-infra dumps with no periodic triggers. Native sampling remains a separate optional mechanism and its earlier empty results are not treated as zero allocations.

`analyze-artwork-native-memory.mjs` attributes events through those timestamp brackets, because Chrome rewrites trace-local IDs relative to request GUIDs. It records separate top-level allocator roots and private footprint; nested/overlapping allocator values are not summed, and omitted roots/attributes remain unknown rather than zero. Tests prove stage attribution despite different GUIDs, non-additive hierarchy handling, precise hex byte conversion, and rejection of missing/duplicate/reversed markers, missing renderer data, wrong units and failed dumps. The analyzer accepts bounded gzip traces and verifies their decoded source hash.

Two shared and two unique twelve-resident cycles completed in the final run. App caches/borrowed providers drain and DOM returns exactly to baseline. The renderer private footprint was 19,547,136 bytes before artwork, 724,536,320 bytes with the last unique cast active, and 168,119,296 bytes after release. The active canvas root reports 233,310,792 bytes; that root is unreported after release. The released partition allocator still reports 114,327,552 bytes, requiring further native-buffer attribution. Total process RSS remains high and is a different measure; these results do not prove a leak-free permanent plateau or an acceptable full-game memory bound.

An A/B CPU-backed-copy experiment (`willReadFrequently:true`) did not clearly lower peak/released RSS, so it was reverted and is not shipped. Exact reports and a reconstructible source-hash check are retained. Compressed raw trace, completed report, analyses, limited verdict and fingerprints are committed under `body-broad-v1/native-memory-review-v1`. The gzip copy reproduces the native analysis exactly. Syntax/diff checks and native-analysis regressions pass. Long-run native ownership, complete editor/game limits and the remaining release gates stay open.

### Resident memory observer control and native-buffer attribution — 2026-10-29

The earlier growing released native buffers are now explained in a controlled study. Added an isolated raw-CDP diagnostic driver using only Page/Runtime by default, with an explicit matching `--raw-executable` and a separate `--raw-network-agent` positive control. The benchmark can capture each unique-cast release, pressure/cache-clear diagnostics and an in-process Network disable. It records observer type and whether network inspection is enabled. The driver creates its own temporary browser profile, closes only its own startup pages/process, and retains the original Playwright benchmark mode.

Final controls use identical source hashes, the same HeadlessChrome 151.0.7922.34 executable, four shared and four unique twelve-resident cycles, and the same raw driver. Without Network inspection, released partition allocation stays between 3,538,944 and 3,686,400 bytes; private footprint spans 59,967,488–65,226,752 bytes across the four unique releases. With Network enabled, released partition allocation grows from 141,541,376 to 217,284,608 bytes. Pressure and browser-cache clearing do not release it. Disabling that same Network domain then releases 213,630,976 partition bytes and 212,451,328 private-footprint bytes in the same browser. This identifies the observed growing response-buffer allocation as observer-owned in this study. High process RSS alone was not proof of retained game previews.

`compare-artwork-memory-observers.mjs` refuses mismatched binaries/versions, cast parameters and source fingerprints, missing/ambiguous stages and duplicate cycles; no-effect controls remain no effect. Native tracing still treats omitted allocators as unknown and overlapping allocator roots as non-additive. App caches and borrowed providers drain and DOM returns to baseline in both final controls, including post-diagnostic stages. The default Playwright mode also passes a separate two-shared/two-unique-cycle ownership regression.

Both compressed raw traces, full reports, analyses, exact comparison and limited verdict are committed under `body-broad-v1/memory-observer-control-v1`; analyzing those compressed copies reproduces the comparison byte-for-byte. The initial regular-Chrome run used a different executable/extra renderers and is explicitly excluded. Syntax/diff checks and observer/native-analysis regression tests pass. This resolves the sampled observer-retention finding, not the complete release memory gate: full 64×64 editor/game budgets, broader body-family combinations and long-duration limits remain required, along with the other art and usability gates. No public game artwork or runtime behavior changed.

### Interrupted resident activity transitions — 2026-10-29

Fixed a presentation defect found while reviewing the resident transition gate: when another activity arrived during the 180 ms fade, the renderer discarded the current blend and restarted from the unfinished target clip. It now carries the displayed mixture into the next transition. Identical native pose keys are merged, including sequence index so carried-equipment phases remain distinct; repeated interruptions cannot create an unbounded tree of source mixtures. Settled timing, selected interpolation, facing and simulation state remain unchanged.

The new regression fails on the old implementation and passes on the fix: 312 interrupted clip/profile combinations preserve source weights and custom keyframe selection, pause exactly and settle into the new activity. Ten thousand rapid changes retain finite normalized weights and a native-pose bound. Existing animation and profile regressions also pass (156 and 169 transition cases). Current public Classic Iara and Broad providers render identical pose pixels at the instant of interruption in day/mirrored night, with breath held fixed to isolate pose continuity.

A real native-transition movie and sixteen decoded quarter-second samples, plus the enlarged before/interruption/90 ms/settled board, were generated and visually inspected. Classic crossfade ghosting and Broad discrete painted pose changes remain visible; this fixes interruption discontinuity, not those broader art/presentation issues. A normal pointerup still commits before its ordinary capture-loss notification.

A complete ordinary stroke still creates exactly one undo step and restores the exact prior definition through the public Undo button. Removing only the capture-loss handler makes this same final regression fail; the fixed source is restored byte-for-byte afterward.

The lower-level gesture regression now passes ten cancellation/idempotence/tool-switch cases, covering pointercancel and lostpointercapture for pan, brush, utility route and move. All thirteen normal brush/route release cases still pass. Syntax and diff checks pass. Attempts to verify native tab hiding were excluded: both tested headless browser modes keep the page visible, while the visible-browser attempt lost focus before the gesture could start reliably. Native OS focus/tab-switch validation therefore remains open; it is not replaced by the synthetic-listener checks above. The broader release goal remains active.

### Durable editor preferences and committed view recovery — 2026-10-29

Fixed view-setting persistence gaps found during save/recovery review. Nature size now follows the same debounced view-save path as brush width and tool choices; grid and overlay changes also mark the view as changed. Unsaved view settings participate in the beforeunload recovery cache even when the scenario definition is clean. The guard cancels an unfinished gesture before caching. This prevents an unrelated autosave or Ctrl+S during a drag from installing a transient view. Attaching another project clears pending gestures before replacing its camera, so an old pan cannot restore its center into the new project.

Catalog sprites, versioned bundle sources and other retained character sources/studies are labeled distinctly; these inventory labels are not visual approval or a claim that every study is active. Top-level artwork still requires a catalog entry, and other character-source directories/names must be explicitly supported. Nothing is added to browser bitmap loading.

Duplicate bytes are allowed only when all but at most one named original are content-addressed `source-<sha256>.png` copies declared by a versioned character bundle, whose referenced bytes match the declared hash. Both relative-path and rooted local-URL manifest formats are validated with bounded path syntax. All nineteen actual duplicate groups satisfy this contract. Merely renaming a file to resemble a hash is insufficient; removing its bundle declaration restores the duplicate failure. An ordinary duplicate remains an error.

`verify-artwork-source-inventory.mjs` checks the complete current inventory and uses isolated fixtures to reject missing records, changed bundled bytes, unregistered files, accidental named copies, missing bundle declarations and traversal paths. `sync-artwork-source-inventory.mjs` checks by default; updating inventory requires explicit `--write` after inspecting source changes. `verify-artwork.mjs` now passes again, retaining catalog crop/ID/file, building day/night dimensions/transparency, camera projection, scale, economy anchor and house-variation checks. Syntax/diff checks pass. **Why:** versioned bundles deliberately retain copies of shared sources; treating those as accidental art duplication prevented the audit from reaching its useful checks. This resolves the previously recorded mechanical audit failure without accepting any unreviewed image or closing remaining visual, performance or builder release gates.

### Native allocation sampling during dense village preparation — 2026-10-29

Extended `benchmark-creator-playtest-memory.mjs` with optional `--loading-dump-ms=250..2000` sampling, disabled by default. It brackets detailed native memory dumps with actual editor-busy or resident-loading UI observations while opening the editor, starting its public playtest and returning. Loading samples issue no forced garbage-collection command and keep the raw driver’s Network inspection disabled. Existing readiness samples retain their explicit forced-GC behavior and are not relabeled as loading observations. A dump bracket that crosses readiness or navigation is retained but excluded from the loading-only maximum.

The final isolated reference-browser run uses the native-validated 64×64 Mata Atlântica seed-42 fixture, 256 mixed buildings and twelve public Broad residents: one shared-appearance and one unique-appearance cycle. Eighteen loading samples were captured; thirteen have loading states at both ends, with at least two eligible samples for each of the six transitions. The largest bracketed renderer private footprint was 999,870,464 bytes during unique-cast return to editor. This is a sampled lower bound on that transition's true peak, not a whole-app bound. Ready/running states can be higher: the unique running observation records 1,048,678,400 bytes. Allocator-root maxima stay separate and are never summed.

The existing lifecycle analyzer still passes exact corresponding editor DOM/listener/canvas round trips, empty-creator canvas restoration, draft/run isolation and actual public 1×/8× simulation progress. A new analyzer verifies unique stage attribution and endpoint state/URL agreement; negative controls reject forced GC, Network inspection, duplicate/missing/ambiguous samples, invented phases and forged loading flags. Missing private footprint/root evidence stays unknown. The native trace analyzer’s attribution regressions also pass. Both final game screenshots were visually inspected. Complete report, fixture, compressed raw trace, replayable analyses, scoped verdict and screenshots are retained in `assets/validation/creator-loading-memory-v1/`. **Why:** readiness-state dumps can miss temporary allocations during composition and scene transitions. One short profiled pair does not establish all-biome/body coverage, a sustained plateau, release budgets, 16× performance or physical-display pacing; these original gates remain open.

### Actual owned-browser crash recovery — 2026-10-29

Added `verify-editor-browser-crash-recovery.mjs` using a disposable persistent Chromium profile and private loopback origin. It creates a camp through the public library, saves title/grid/tool choices, paints and saves a trusted road stroke, then holds a second real stroke unfinished. Before SIGKILL it requires the owned browser's debugging session to report the exact disposable profile in its command line and one positive browser PID, excludes the test process itself, and confirms that PID has exited before relaunch. Only this owned browser is terminated; the profile is removed after the test.

Relaunching the same profile/origin and reopening through the public library restores the exact completed checkpoint/history and view. The unfinished stroke is absent, the installed beforeunload probe did not fire, and public Undo/Redo remove and restore exactly the completed road transaction. The first run exposed empty-selection fields added only on reopening; fresh map and project initialization now use the same explicit null selections as restored views, keeping serialized state consistent without changing selection behavior. The final crash run passes, as do the existing view-save/pending-pan/unload-cache check and ten gesture-cancellation cases. Before/after complete-screen captures were inspected and retained with the saved record and source identities in `assets/validation/editor-browser-crash-recovery-v1/`. **Why:** a normal reload can invoke graceful saving and cannot establish abrupt-exit recovery. This verifies one real browser-crash transaction boundary; power loss, all write phases, disk faults and native OS focus remain distinct requirements. Overall release gates remain open.

### Modular example casts and current public playthroughs — 2026-10-29

Before the dry season now demonstrates five distinct modular residents, and An inherited village mixes the original Iara/Bento/Nina with modular Sueli and Otávio. The changes were authored through the regular import, character-creator and export controls. Resident IDs and every noncosmetic scenario field remain exact; import assigns fresh map/document copy identities. Marina, Davi and Cecília were saved and exported as reusable modular resident presets through the player library. **Why:** renamed original appearance presets did not demonstrate the plan's custom and mixed-cast requirements.

All three shipped examples were replayed through the normal picker, paid Town orders and actual pointer placements where needed. Each reached its required goals, continued for at least three more in-game days, restored an exact checkpoint after reload and returned to an unchanged draft with its scratch run removed. The Cerrado challenge completed before its seven-day deadline. All seven standing creator previews and six final game/journal captures were inspected. Exact source/report/capture identities and original definitions are retained in `assets/scenario-reviews/modular-examples-v1/`; `verify-example-modular-evidence.mjs`, `verify-example-scenarios.mjs`, `verify-public-example-evidence.mjs` and `verify-public-example-continuation.mjs` check the corresponding contracts. Older playthrough reports remain historical and are not relabeled against changed sources. Concurrent runs provide gameplay evidence, not performance measurements. This does not close all animation, biome artwork, sustained performance, recovery or unfamiliar-player release gates.

### Final wetland biome/lighting review — 2026-10-29

Finished inspecting the eighteen previously pending final wetland context captures: all three launch biomes at zoom 1 in day/night/rain/night-rain, plus zoom 2 night and rain. Together with the six earlier inspected views this covers the complete 24-view static matrix. The painted bed follows its adjacent road boundary, contains the barrel, preserves the full plant silhouette and remains legible beside the original resident and home under these presentation settings. A fresh run from current code generated all 24 PNGs byte-identically to the inspected set; fetched input hashes and retained fixture bytes were also checked. All current captures, definitions, report and scoped verdict are in `assets/art-direction/wetland-square-v2/active-v2/`. The earlier active-v1 record remains historical with its then-pending status.

The broad artwork source/geometry/state audit now passes after the separate explicit source-inventory correction; that does not imply visual acceptance of every source. **Why:** generated but uninspected screenshots cannot close a visual gate. This closes this wetland static context matrix only. Synthetic visual rain is not climate simulation, and service/path completion, broader building/state review, sustained performance and unfamiliar-player validation remain separate release work.

### Source-bound starter/maximum gameplay performance comparison — 2026-10-29

Extended `benchmark-mixed-play.mjs` with launch-biome, supported population, starter-building and shared-appearance choices. Existing defaults remain the twelve-person maximum mixed Mata Atlântica fixture. Every run now retains the exact authored fixture, opening snapshot identity, benchmark/fixture-builder hashes, fetched module/asset fingerprints and an explicit completion/error record. The report distinguishes developer snapshot import/forced speeds from trusted camera input; these controls establish repeatable stress states, not public authoring or speed-control acceptance. `verify-mixed-play-evidence.mjs` independently recompiles the retained opening, checks source and fixture identities, phase/population/building contracts, positive simulation, and bounded artwork cache entries/queues.

Two sequential unprofiled seed-42 Cerrado runs cover five Broad residents sharing one recipe with two starter buildings, and twelve unique Broad residents with 256 mixed buildings. Each has ten-second still/panning phases at 1× and forced 16×, with exact native checkpoint restoration and zero browser errors. Eight final full-screen captures were inspected. The starter advances at approximately the requested simulation rate, with panning frame-gap p95 10.2 ms at 1× and 16.4 ms at 16×. The maximum fixture records 25.0 ms and 41.3 ms respectively; its 16× panning phase advances at 96.3% of the requested simulation rate. Prepared appearance RGBA/cache bytes are 14,184,964 shared and 173,852,864 unique; these are not total process/GPU memory.

Evidence and explicit limited verdict are retained in `assets/validation/cerrado-play-performance-v1/`. **Why:** a dense default-biome run cannot establish performance across different geography, population or appearance sharing. This identifies a maximum-load fast-forward issue rather than closing the performance gate. The starter is not an established typical village; broader biome/body/editor-action distributions, sustained memory/throughput, physical-display pacing and reference budgets remain open.

### Reachable-meal availability fast path — 2026-10-29

Profiled the exact maximum Cerrado fixture with a separately built, name-preserving original engine. Six hundred native quarter-second ticks replay exactly against the original shipped module. The profile identifies food-availability probes inside `workforce::eating` as substantial work: they invoke the full ranked meal chooser even though the caller needs only an existence answer. `food::can_eat` now shares the chooser's claim, dated-food, approach and travel-validity rules but returns after finding one usable portion. Actual meal assignment still collects and ranks all candidates with unchanged precedence. No new cache or serialized state was introduced.

Extended the engine comparison/profiling scripts to accept an exact retained scenario, and the comparison script to accept an independent original module. The new module preserves 100 exact snapshots over 2,400 ticks, frame/full-report parity and the existing three-biome/two-mode/one-five-twelve-resident matrix (12 accepted cases, six expected atomic rejections). All 787 native tests pass, including strengthened last-portion and unreachable-store availability checks; strict all-target Clippy and targeted formatting for both changed Rust files pass. Repository-wide formatting check still reports pre-existing differences outside these files.

Four alternating-order engine-only batches improve from 623–632 ms to 461–480 ms per 240 ticks. A subsequent unprofiled rendered run uses the identical retained 256-building/twelve-unique-Broad Cerrado definition: 16× panning frame-gap p95 improves from 41.3 to 33.4 ms, while measured simulation throughput changes from 96.3% to approximately 100% of the requested rate. All four phase checkpoints restore exactly, no browser errors occur, and all four final captures were inspected. Original named binary/bindings, compressed diagnostic trace, parity report, new rendered run, source fingerprints and descriptive comparison are retained in `assets/validation/meal-availability-fastpath-v1/`. **Why:** callers asking whether food exists should not rank every store. Short measurements show improvement, not universal 60-fps or release-performance acceptance. Earlier source-bound engine reports remain historical; full builder gates remain open.

### Player walkability and camp-connectivity overlay — 2026-10-29

Added **Walking access from camp** to the ordinary editor's land-layer selector. It distinguishes clear cell centres connected to the arrival camp, clear but disconnected cells, and blocked centres. The ground inspector supplies the corresponding text explanation, alongside the colour legend. A read-only native `navigation_overlay` query uses the same cached navigation grid, endpoint links and route search as resident travel. JavaScript does not invent a second walkability formula. The query is lazy while the layer is unused, cached by the last valid authored-land key, and repeated after relevant edits/undo. Pointer movement only repaints the completed result. Invalid drafts retain the explicitly labelled last valid preview.

The native regression compares all 4,096 clearance flags with native clear checks and samples real route equivalence on a river-free map and a property divided by a river, including an obstructing home. A separate release-WASM check compares all 24,576 cell-centre connectivity answers with actual native route quotes across six fixtures: all three biomes, absent/stepped separating rivers and eight extra completed-home obstacles. Destination endpoint links are included, matching resident routing rather than only conservative grid edges. Querying preserves the exact snapshot. All 788 native tests and strict all-target Clippy pass. Public-browser checks in Mata Atlântica, Cerrado and Caatinga verify disconnected land, river removal/undo/redo, real home placement/undo, view-only isolation, incomplete-draft handling and exact save/reload. The existing view/pending-pan recovery regression also passes. Six full editor captures were inspected; the final expanded browser run reproduces all six PNGs byte-identically. Exact fixtures, source fingerprints, report and scoped verdict are retained in `assets/validation/editor-navigation-overlay-v1/`.

**Why:** the planned walkability layer was absent, and clear-looking land across a separating river does not necessarily have a route from camp. This covers static cell-centre access, not temporary pedestrian crowding or every fractional work approach. Other original editor, artwork, performance, recovery and unfamiliar-player release requirements remain open. The introductory resident-status paragraph was also corrected to describe the current public builds/outfits rather than the superseded one-body prototype.

### Navigable player creation workspaces — 2026-10-29

Added five freely selectable workspaces above the shared map: Land, Friends, Village, Journal, and Check & share. Each exposes its relevant tools and inspector sections. Selection details remain available when inspecting an object, and review links reveal the destination workspace before scrolling. Blocking errors appear in the review tab. Arrow keys/Home/End navigate the tab rail without intercepting field input. Switching workspaces cancels unfinished map gestures; it does not create a document/history edit. The chosen workspace is saved with the editor view and restored after reload and an isolated playtest. **Why:** the original plan called for navigable editing areas, but the growing single inspector made unrelated controls compete for space.

The dedicated public-browser check covers all five workspaces, real cast/fund/journal edits, invalid-draft discovery and repair, contextual links, pending-stroke cancellation, exact document/history isolation, reload, test/return and an independent exported/imported copy. Seven desktop captures at 1600×1000 and 1280×800 were inspected. All 58 changed existing browser regressions pass against their current source hashes; syntax and diff checks pass. Tests now explicitly navigate player-visible sections and wait for the library to open before importing, avoiding hidden-file-input timing races. Final scoped evidence is retained in `assets/validation/editor-workspaces-v1/`. Concurrent checks establish functional behaviour, not performance. Native simulation was unchanged. This completes the workspace navigation increment; broader artwork, sustained performance, recovery, and unfamiliar-player release gates remain open.

### Validation links into journal repair — 2026-10-29

Journal validation notes in Check & share now include Open journal or Open events & roles. These actions reveal the Journal workspace and open the appropriate editor with its native validation message intact. Indexed chapter paths select the corresponding chapter and focus the matching editable field; indexed goal paths also expand the identified goal. Generic native paths do not pretend to identify an individual goal. Opening a repair view does not rewrite the journal or create an undo transaction. **Why:** journal errors previously blocked testing without a direct route from review feedback to the editing controls.

Public browser regressions prove an invalid deadline can be opened and repaired with Undo, an invalid title in the second chapter selects that chapter and focuses its title, and an event referring to a missing goal opens the event editor and repairs with Undo. Existing challenge boundary/freeze/restart/continuation and event order/funding/export/reload/ordinary Story checks pass, with zero browser errors. Both desktop repair captures were inspected; source identities and scoped results are retained in `assets/validation/editor-issue-links-v1/`. Syntax and diff checks pass. This is a feedback/navigation increment, not completion of the wider original release gates.

### Owned fresh-water access layer — 2026-10-29

Added Owned fresh-water access to the normal editor's overlay selector. It marks compatible owned containers, shows unclaimed fresh-water units and how many checked friends have a route, and distinguishes ready, empty, closed and unreachable sources with text as well as colour. The legend reports reachable supply, whether everyone checked has a usable source, disabled local-water rules, and the existing camp fallback when everyone is away. It excludes the neighbour's service, carried water, future rain and deliveries. **Why:** the planned water-access layer was missing; owning a filled tank across an impassable river must not imply that residents can use it.

A read-only native query shares the same tank approach/route predicate, open-container test, reservation-aware free stock and participant policy as existing water service measurements. The UI queries only when this layer is selected and its last valid authored definition changes; pointer movement and repeated layer switching reuse the result. Invalid drafts retain an explicitly labelled last valid preview. The chosen layer restores after reload without editing document history.

All 788 native tests and strict all-target Clippy pass, including an extended service-access regression comparing overlay quantities/access with existing native readings for isolated, partial and connected stores while preserving exact snapshots. Release WASM compilation passes. Public-browser checks in all three launch biomes verify the overlay, native opening agreement, river removal/undo/redo, view/history isolation, no pointer-time queries, incomplete drafts and exact reload. Existing navigation-overlay and creator/test-loop browser checks pass. Six final captures showing both the camp and distant store were inspected after correcting a label/camp-marker overlap; exact fixtures, sources and scoped results are retained in `assets/validation/editor-water-access-v1/`.

This covers owned opening storage and resident access, not future sustainability, a coverage answer at every arbitrary cell, dense maximum-label presentation or the full utility-network overlay requirement. The remaining original artwork, performance, recovery and unfamiliar-player gates stay open.

### Utility-corridor and goal-region inspection layers — 2026-10-29

Added Utility corridors and Goal regions to the ordinary map-layer selector. Utilities can be filtered to fresh water, reclaimed irrigation, greywater, sealed sewage or electricity. Counts and installed/planned styling come from the compiled native routes; the legend explicitly separates installed construction from actual flow/power. The dedicated layer redraws corridors above sprites for inspection and hides unrelated region outlines. The region layer hides utility corridors, filters a named area or shows all, redraws boundaries/labels above buildings and lists its authored chapter/goal references. These are reference counts, never a second completion evaluator. **Why:** always drawing every corridor and region did not provide the planned focused inspection layers.

Layer/filter choices save as view state without changing the document or undo history and restore after reload. Removing the selected region falls back to the remaining areas without retargeting its goals; Undo restores the region and view filter. Filtered-out corridors cannot be selected or erased by clicking their hidden cells. Normal map rendering remains available through Natural view.

The extended public route-authoring browser regression covers all five class filters, native installed/planned counts, actual region/goal authoring, exact view-only checkpoints, both filters after reload, hidden-route selection/erase isolation, referenced-region removal/Undo, existing route import/edit/play and exact utility checkpoints. The three-biome water-access regression and existing custom-journal/region gameplay regression also pass. Two final desktop captures were inspected. The exact saved scene draft, source fingerprints and scoped result are retained in `assets/validation/editor-scene-layers-v1/`. Syntax and diff checks pass; the native engine is unchanged in this increment. Wider operational-network diagnostics, maximum-density presentation and the remaining original release gates still require their own evidence.
